## SESSION 26 — 2026-08-22 (DOC-SYSTEM SESSION. No prose. Two canon rulings, one delegated decision, a nine-finding cold-read audit, and the protected-phrasings file given an admission test it never had)

**OPEN.** `verify.sh` OK. Full tiered cold read (~88,000 words). Nine findings reported before any work.

**THE FINDING THAT MATTERED — 00'S APPEND-ONLY DESIGN HAD LEAKED.** Session 20 ruled the fire is no longer the unleashing (Kael unlocks in Book Two). The superseded sentences stayed readable in 00, and **`07/magic-snapshot`, `07/standing-rules` and `07/planted-clues` row 48 all still carried the dead fire-candidate claim as live LAW CORE** — six sessions on. All three corrected. 00's two superseded lines tagged `[SUPERSEDED s20 → §THE UNLEASHING GATE]` **in place** — annotation only, nothing left 00 — and the convention is now written into 00's own status line, so it documents itself. **The structural diagnosis: 00 is the only file that mixes live canon and its own history in one document with no marking, so an untagged line reads as live.**

**PROTECTED-PHRASINGS — THE ADMISSION TEST (author-ratified).** The author's diagnosis was sharper than the one proposed: the problem was never bulk, it was the **selection criterion**. The file had been admitting *plot beats in ordinary English* (`"I was followed."`, `"I'm going to have a brother."`) because the moment mattered — and the gloss on the first of those confessed it, reading *protect the position, not just the words*. It had also been absorbing world doctrine that 02 already held verbatim. **Test now in the header:** *would you recognise this as this book's if you met it in someone else's?* Ordinary words + important moment = BEAT → `chapter-records`. States how the world works = DOCTRINE → 02. Coined language, figures, bent phrasing = stays. Two marked non-growth exceptions: RATIFIED VERBATIM and RULE-CARRYING. Second pass on the author's correction also evicted **ordinary-word refrains** (`"Again tomorrow"`, `"both things were real"`) and **vocabulary duplicating `02/lexicon`** (`far-counting`, `the standing game`, `the lazy place`, `the family trade`, `Evening`) — the latter being six of the script's multi-chapter hits, since vocabulary is *supposed* to recur. **444 → 346 quoted phrases; 6,524 → 4,725 words; multi-chapter noise 26 → 20.** Commentary stripped to normalised tags; headers standardised to `**Ch. N — Title**`; the two orphan session-blocks re-homed to their chapters. **ZERO-LOSS VERIFIED, NOT ASSERTED:** archive embed byte-identical to HEAD; every kept phrase found verbatim in the archive; phrase-level script parity across all 22 chapters with every disappearance mapped to an eviction key. All 73 evicted entries + 2 blocks live in `archive/protected-phrasings-archive.md` with reason codes.

**A CORRECTED ESTIMATE, WORTH KEEPING.** The first pass predicted ~150 survivors and delivered 396, because the estimate was extrapolated from Ch. 19 and Ch. 22 — the two most beat-heavy blocks in the file. Ch. 3–17 are almost entirely coinage and pass cleanly. **The offenders cluster in the recent, plot-heavy chapters, where the close transcribes dramatic weight instead of language.**

**COLD-READ COST.** Ranked the session open: four files were half of it (`chapter-records` 20%, `planted-clues` 11%, 00 10%, 05 8%). **CLAUDE.md amended (author-ratified): changelogs and the archive are not cold-read material** — −4,511w (5%) per open; git is the authority on what changed. 00's own 240-word embedded update-chain rolled verbatim here and replaced by a 94-word status line. **THE VERBOSITY HYPOTHESIS WAS TESTED AND DOES NOT HOLD:** 00 grew 6,157 → 9,255 across eight commits (~+450w/session) and that growth is *decisions*, not prose — of six rhyme bullets, five are load-bearing and one is cuttable at ~30 words. **When 00 starts to hurt, TIER THE READ, NOT THE FILE.** Also flagged, not acted on: `planted-clues` is labelled LAW CORE but grew 113 → 165 rows and is empirically an accumulator with no eviction path — misfiled on 07's own growth axis.

**CANON RULING 1 — THE GAMES ARE MARTIAL PROWESS AND NOTHING ELSE.** 04 beat 18's session-8 wording *"trials of arms, wits, structured magic"* struck: it implied separate scored events, never reached any page, and could not stand against the bracket committed in Ch. 22. **The author's frame made it doctrine rather than a deletion — the tournament measures what the empire takes its own strength to be.** The absence is the point: the Temple's boards already measure letters, sums, the chronicle and natural philosophy twice a year and nail the order to a colonnade, so the one instrument that has measured Kael's real axis for four years is a school ledger the empire does not read. His mind wins inside the fight (06: *fights are puzzles*) or not at all. The session-8 amendment stands: first magic close and whole, as spectacle.

**CANON RULING 2 — THE BIRTH AND THE SEMIFINAL (00 appended).** The s25 collision is **intended**. Neris goes into labour **as the semifinals begin** — the round the raid lands in. **The tournament wins the two-places-at-once problem: Severin is above the sand, not at the house**, because the chair is the only enforcement the games have. The one delivery that requires him happens without him; **Neris is alone for a violent Kynemir birth**; **Vask is split three ways and can hold two at most** — a wife in a house, a son on the sand, and the man who could save her in a box above it. *Dies buying time for both his sons* becomes geometry: he spends his last hour running that distance. **Supersedes 00 s22's ripple note** (tagged in place). **DO NOT RESOLVE:** who he reaches, in what order, whether Severin arrives in time, or Neris's survival of the delivery.

**DELEGATED DECISION — MELORAN.** Seated as the Temple six's surviving senior-cohort entrant, off the page's own arithmetic: Ch. 18's sheet holds the two seniors at lines 3–4, Ch. 21 puts Ledron at line 3, and line 4 went unnamed until Ch. 21 named him — seventeen, entered, and the boy Aurelian seeks out four days a week to be *enormously better* than him. Not one of Halden's yard pair, who sit four lines below Kael. Available as a round-of-8 body: a spear and four years of reach.

**REST OF THE AUDIT CLEARED.** The spent-name ledger fed at last (**Meloran · Ledron · Tavrian · Talrane · Iscane**, and **THE COLLEGIUM** as the capital's school — 02 had still claimed both visiting-school names were reserved). PLANNED 7 → 6: the spent war-pre-seeds row struck (it was duplicated in PLANTED as row 143), bird-word and chart rescoped **Part III → Part IV** since Part III closed under them. Ten stale 03 Glance stamps refreshed, including Valeria's self-contradictory *12, as of Ch. 20* against a thirteen-year-old Kael. Four phantom phrasings struck — **all four first appearing at the Ch. 21/22 commit itself and never in `manuscript/`** — the fourth, *"the split rock with a chain on it"*, confirmed by the author as a line he cut in the pass, which is the disease exactly. `07/index` counts refreshed; 05's s21 block rolled here; clue parity **140/6/4/10**.

**NO PROSE TOUCHED — baselines unchanged except 00 (9,255 → 9,606, the append).** Three commits: `8e3fa4b`, `96e3c3f`, `d098b50`.

## SESSION 27 — 2026-08-22 (RECONSTRUCTED at the session-28 open. THE SESSION NEVER CLOSED)

**Reconstructed from git, because the document system had no memory of it.** Three commits ran after the session-26 close and none of the closing protocol did: no session record here, no 05 status block, the NEXT-SESSION OPENER still addressing session 27, and three commits unpushed.

**WHAT HAPPENED.** `8ce0c9a` cleared a cold-read audit — eight stale-canon fixes, including the Ch. 12 prohibition row still carrying a dead *concession ladder OPEN, dedicated design required* instruction three sessions after 00 s21 killed the ladder, and the protected-phrasings header still claiming 395 survivors against the true 346. `c7c2ea9` parked a **Ch. 23 draft, "The Hilt"** (353 lines) and added `archive/writing-style-ideas.md`, a condensed fight-scene reference. `8fcedb8` **deleted the draft.**

**THE FAILURE, and it is the one worth recording.** The reason the draft was discarded was written nowhere — not in 05, not here, not in a commit message. The hard rule is *nothing lives in chat history*, and it was broken on the single most expensive artifact the session produced. Session 28 opened blind on a chapter that had already been attempted and rejected once, and had to ask.

**RECOVERED AT THE SESSION-28 OPEN, FROM THE AUTHOR: "The Hilt" was BORING — lots of internal thought, little action.** That diagnosis became standing craft law and is the reason Ch. 23 exists in the shape it does.

**LESSON: park the reason, not just the draft.** A discarded draft costs nothing — git is the safety net and cutting is free. A discarded draft with no written reason costs the *next* session, because it redesigns blind and walks straight back into whatever failed.

## SESSION 28 — 2026-08-23 (CH. 23 "THE GHOST" — 4,211w. Beat 18 opened on the sand. Plus the Art capitalised across the whole book)

**OPEN.** `verify.sh` FAILED on a modified `.gitignore` — one line, `+.vscode/`, benign. Full tiered cold read (~90,000 words). The audit's one real finding was structural rather than textual: **session 27 had never closed** (reconstructed above). Everything else came back clean — clue parity 140/6/4/10, `07/index` counts exact to the word, timeline consistent through Ch. 22, baselines matching.

**THE DESIGN.** The author supplied the discard reason and the shape in one go: **Kael wins the first bout with no Art and no weapon** — pure physical, against Lucan's air columns and one-handed swordplay — and the fight is *dominance*, brutal and practical, with no recognisable style and no honour in it, because all he knows is what killers taught him. The people love it; the nobles read humiliation. The chapter's thesis was that **the whole Drusane system needs a distance**: guard, straight back, the doctrine's empty off-hand, the launched column — every part of it assumes a man standing four feet away with a blade. Kael arrives with neither. So declining the sword is not a flex for its own sake; it is the correct answer, and the arena reads it as contempt. Both true at once.

**THE CRAFT LAW THAT CAME OUT OF THE DISCARD:** in a fight chapter the thinking happens BEFORE the first exchange and AFTER the last; the fight itself is bodies. All the reasoning was moved to the staging and the aftermath, so the reader holds the key before the first column and then watches it turn.

**THE ENDING WAS REBUILT TWICE, and the author was right both times.** V1 ended on a pin and a judge — too clean. V2 went brutal but put the enforcement on the page: Severin transmuting sand to stone from sixty feet to stop Kael mid-strike. **The author cut that too, and it was the better call**: no Art from Severin at all. He stops it with his OFFICE. So the final shape is Lucan spent to the floor of his capacity and reaching anyway, disarmed, looking up nine rows at a father with *the face of somebody watching an expense* — who then sits down — coming at Kael with the form gone off him, put down three times, and **knocked out cold with one exact blow under the jaw.** *His hands did not come up.* Four seconds of silence in a building of eleven thousand. **Halden smiling in the judges' enclosure, and he had been smiling for some while**, unseen by Kael. Then the First Master stands up in his own box and says *"The bout is decided. Kael, of the Island."*

**WHAT THAT BOUGHT.** Severin's earth and his mending are both **still entirely unspent on the page**, banked whole with five printed days of games left. 00 s8's first-magic reserve is **OPENED, not spent** — Lucan's columns are the first unfenced art in the book, and true spectacle stays with the top-tier bouts. And the horror is cleaner: nobody intervened because there was nothing left to intervene in, legally, under a rule that permits exactly this.

**AUTHOR RULING THAT AMENDED A STANDING INSTRUCTION — THE SHAPE, NOT THE COLLISION.** 02 and 05 had been read as *plant that Kael and Valeria share a half*. The author struck it from the draft: they are children in a single-elimination bracket who each have their own bouts to win first, and pre-announcing the summit spends every intervening fight and turns the Sedrane bout into a stepping stone. The structure must be legible; the collision assembles itself as the rounds fall. Amended in 02 §Tournament Structure with its scope written in.

**THE DAY-MAP (author ruling).** One round a day — R16 · R8 · SEMIS · break · a final nobody reaches. The games print six days (Ch. 22, on the page) and die on the third with half the programme unspent. **Kael's road is Drusane → SEDRANE → VEL**, which means **Elarine's Ch. 20 gun fires in the round of 8** — *you will not be kind to me · I will know · I am extremely good at not forgiving things* — and she loses the Valeria she wanted, because Kael takes her out the day before.

**THE PHANTOM OF THE SESSION — "BLUNTED".** `03/kael` and 05 both had Kael bringing *blunted twin shorts*; the word was **never on any page**. The Ch. 21 shed pair are only ever *two short blades*, and every *blunted* in the manuscript belongs to Ch. 15's training steel or the Temple's issue blades. Minted at the s25 close off the design, in two files, with an instruction hanging off it — the exact class the standing instrument exists to catch. Corrected: they are live steel, they stay wrapped, and s25's *"and fights conventional"* is dead.

**THE ART — CAPITALISED (author ruling; retro pass same session).** *"the Art"* takes a capital where it names magic as a DISCIPLINE, and stays lowercase as a count noun or possessed instance (*a rare art · two arts · her art was fire · his own art · art hand*). The pass was unusually cheap and it was priced before it was run: every one of the 51 manuscript instances is the magic sense, so there was no disambiguation risk; **`wc -w` moved on zero chapters**, so no baseline shifted; and `check-echoes.sh` lowercases before matching, so no protected phrase could break — all four art-bearing entries were re-run and found. The counter-argument was recorded honestly rather than buried: it makes the Art the first abstract common noun in the book to take a capital, against a strict habit where every coined magic term and every one of Kael's coinages is lowercase. It is licensed because *Resonant* already capitalises — practitioner and discipline together — and the fence written into `02/lexicon` bars it spreading to the boards, the dish, the yard, the games or the coat.

**AUTHOR CUTS WORTH CARRYING.** Both forward-reaching narrator lines were removed in the pass — *"the part of it the island talked about for a year afterward"* and *"he took it away whole and carried it for the rest of his life"* — **and the two-noises framing went with them.** The device (Ch. 6's *later, much later*; Ch. 18's *for years afterward*) is expensive, and it was cut twice inside one chapter. The nobles now land entirely on *half that arena had watched a fight; the other half had watched an argument, and had lost it.*

**TWO OF MY OWN BUGS, CREATED BY THE REWRITES AND CAUGHT BY GREPPING FOR THEM.** The Severin pan-away still read *"had not risen from his chair"* after he rose to declare the bout; and Lucan was still walking off between two menders after being knocked unconscious. Both fixed — he goes past the gate on a board, awake by then, not looking at Kael. **When a scene's ending changes, grep the rest of the chapter for what the old ending was load-bearing for.**

**METRICS.** Dialogue 3% — a fight chapter, quiet on purpose, and the second such running after Ch. 22; 06 permits it and the flag is drift in a chapter built out of scenes, which this is not. 30.9 w/para; dashes 2.8/1k; **and 51.0/1k**, against the book's recent 40–42 — the excess is entirely inside the fight (59.4) as chosen cadence, with staging at 38.0 and the summons at 38.5, and every default *and* outside the fight was trimmed. Similes 4.4/1k; not-buts 0. Echo run: one script hit, *"Everything heavy is lazy"*, deliberate, plus three logged echoes the script structurally cannot match.

**VERIFIED, NOT ASSERTED:** clue parity 147/6/4/10 (headers = row counts); every Ch. 23 protected phrase machine-checked against the committed chapter before entry; the archive roll of 05's two s22 blocks confirmed byte-identical against HEAD; `wc -w` baselines all matching after the Art pass; the echo script re-run on the four art-bearing entries.

## SESSION 29 — 2026-08-23 — CH. 24 "THE TABLE" (4,442w) AND CH. 25 OPENED (1,326w)

**OPEN — COLD-READ AUDIT: THREE FINDINGS, TWO REAL, AND THE THIRD WAS MINE.**
1. **The DEPARTURE LEDGER was a spend behind.** `07/standing-rules` still read *Pan-aways: 1 spent* and described only Ch. 12; Ch. 23's five micro-cuts were recorded in 05 and the chapter record but never in the law-core file that 06 §POV names as the authority. Same class as s26's finding. Fed, with a counting convention added (a chapter's micro-cuts count as ONE spend, however many).
2. **Ch. 23's bracket arithmetic was wrong.** *"whichever of the four in the quarter under it was still standing tomorrow"* — in a sixteen-draw with no byes, tomorrow's opponent is the survivor of one PAIR. **Author ruling: it is simple single-elimination.** Fixed to *the two in the pair*; identical word count, so the 4,211 baseline never moved.
3. **THE THIRD WAS WRONG AND IT WAS MINE.** I reported the reader notebook three chapters behind, off the session log's last mention (*"notebook holding 19"*). It holds all 23. **The syncs had been running; only the logging of them had lapsed.** Verified by listing the notebook's sources before touching anything, which is the only reason it did not become a pointless re-sync. **Lesson: a log's last mention of a state is not the state.** Check the artifact.

**AUTHOR RULINGS.** (1) **The camera may pan away — the device is ESTABLISHED**, and is now exempt from 06's per-use argument; scoped to pan-aways alone (interludes and the slips keep theirs, slip 3 firmly reserved), all five craft fences untouched, and the ledger demoted from authoriser to recorder. (2) The bracket is simple single-elimination. (3) **The chapter shape was replaced wholesale, twice, and both replacements were better than mine:** first *audience → cistern four → parents → send-off* in place of my two-chapter split; then **the parents movement moved again to open Ch. 25**, so Vask's *"What has she not got?"* and Kael's answer sit twenty lines from the first exchange instead of a chapter away. (4) The princess is **arrogant, prideful, NOT evil**; the audience was simply to watch the thing up close; **Kael has none of the royal worship everyone else has, argues with her, and she ends angry.** (5) The room must overwhelm him — ice, flowers, gold, meats, fish, drink, perfume. (6) The quartet is warm, light, dialogue, **no tactics** — friends before the chaos; everyone laughs except Kael and Val; Kael flinches by instinct and clocks that she did not laugh either; **she says she will miss them, and smiles.** (7) **Kael says "Val" on the page** and the others take it up — Aurelian quirky, Elarine teasing with an edge. (8) **LOVE is the key** to the parents movement. (9) Aurelian lost to **MELORAN**.

**AUTHOR-CAUGHT IN THE PASS, AND WORTH CARRYING: *"entered boys"*.** The phrase is Ch. 21 vocabulary and therefore technically planted — and it still lost the author on the page. **A compressed noun phrase built on an adjective nobody uses that way is opaque even when it is canon.** Rewritten without the term; the re-derivation of Ch. 21's privilege went with it (06: *the causal chain stops at the first why*). The author also cut the count-fails paragraph whole — the count now lands straight on *Nobody was eating*, which is the same instrument in a fifth of the words — shortened the ice speech, and replaced *"You may go"* with **"Kneel. Say your farewells. And leave afterwards,"** which turned the exit from a dismissal into rank reasserted by a girl who has just been made to feel looked at.

**MY OWN DRIFT, MEASURED AND SENT BACK.** The first draft came in at **and 51.3/1k, uniform across every section** — which is drift, not cadence, and 06 flags exactly that. Two trim passes took it to **42.3**, with the excess left only inside the 294-word sensory-overwhelm block (74.8 there). The Ch. 23 shape: high where it was chosen, at the norm everywhere else.

**TWO CONTINUITY ERRORS CAUGHT BEFORE COMMIT, both mine.** *"four months ago on a stone in the rain"* — Ch. 20's "Val" beat is on the **sea court, cold stones, nearly dark**; the rain belongs to a different movement in a different court; and the interval is ~two months, not four. And *eleven months* → **a year** in three places: Ch. 21 says eleven and then runs eight more weeks to the games, so by the arena it is a year — which now rhymes with her first words in the book.

**A PHANTOM IN `chapter-records`, AND IT REACHED THE DRAFT BEFORE I CAUGHT IT.** Ch. 8's record quoted *"with her whole body the way she listened to birds"*. The page has **"she listened with her whole body"** (Ch. 8) and **"the way she listened to birds"** (Ch. 10) — two halves, two chapters, welded in the record. I wrote the welded form into Ch. 24 off the record, then checked it and cut it to the single Ch. 10 echo. **Ninth-and-tenth instance of the same failure mode since s23, and the first one where the phantom actively propagated into new prose.** The standing rule holds and needs restating: *never write from the ledger; write from the page.*

**VERIFIED, NOT ASSERTED.** 261/261 non-empty lines present across the Ch. 24 / Ch. 25 split. Clue parity **155/6/4/10** (headers = row counts). Echo runs: zero script hits on either new chapter; full-manuscript parity **22 multi-chapter hits**, the only one touching new prose being the logged deliberate *"the way she listened to birds"* — **and one of my own new protected-phrasings entries was pulled after the script flagged it**, *"In front of all of them"* being ordinary words already incidental in Ch. 15 (admission test: that is a beat, and beats belong to `chapter-records`). Baselines all match. Notebook re-synced.

**SESSION 29 ADDENDUM — THE PHANTOM TRIAGE (same day, author-approved after the close).**
The author stated the principle plainly — *if it isn't on the manuscripts, it should not be on the ledgers* — and it turned out to be **already law in exactly one file** (`07/protected-phrasings`, since s25) and absent everywhere else, which is precisely why this session's phantom lived in `chapter-records`. Ratified as a **CLAUDE.md hard rule binding on every file**, with the retirement convention attached: quote marks are a claim that the words are on a page; anything else goes unquoted, in italics.
**`scripts/check-quotes.sh` built, documented in TOOLS.md, and run to zero (53 → 0.)** Every hit confirmed with `git log -S` before being called.
**THE FINDINGS.** (1) **Eleven spans traced to `68de4b6` "Delete Ch. 10 for redo"** — `chapter-records` and `planted-clues` quoting a chapter that was deleted and rewritten. Invisible to every previous hunt because those only ever checked `protected-phrasings`. (2) **The worst single one: *It had not noticed the first time*** — the second sentence of Ch. 7's road echo, cut in the s13 revision, still carried in two files as spent page canon **with the instruction *road echo SPENT* attached**, so a future session would have believed a payoff had landed that is on no page. (3) **02 was quoting its own doctrine and folklore in slightly wrong words** — *the body keeps the receipt* / *something came down off the mountain* — and those are the phrases a later chapter re-mints from, so the drift propagates into prose the same way the Ch. 8 weld did. (4) Split: **20 had existed and been cut**, **33 never existed at all**; nine corrected to the page, the rest unquoted.
**THE LESSON, AND IT IS ABOUT THE INSTRUMENT, NOT THE LEDGER.** The first version of the checker flagged 130 spans and **the very first one I inspected was not a phantom** — the page reads *"I can be wrong by a finger's width," his father said. "The purse forgives me…"* and a contiguous match cannot span a speech tag. Two more bugs surfaced the same way: a one-word prefix before a tag, and naive quote pairing capturing the span between a closing quote and the next opening one. **Three of the original 56 were the tool being wrong.** A detector is only worth its output after you have tried to disprove it.

## SESSION 30 — 2026-08-23 — CH. 25 "THE DOOR" (1,326w) AND CH. 26 "THE THIRD BELL" (5,078w)

**THE SEDRANE BOUT, AND A CHAPTER SPLIT IN TWO.** The session opened on a clean tree, `check-quotes` at 1, and a cold read that produced three findings: the ratification phrase in 05 was quoted when the rule it created says italics-unquoted; the bout's thesis was quoted in `05` and `chapter-records` in its pre-edit form, because a prose edit had been made inside a doc-hygiene commit and the two words *You've said* were never picked up; and **Ch. 25 dated the packing to *the second day of the games* on a night that is day 1.** All three fixed, the third by author ruling.

**FOUR RULINGS WERE OWED BEFORE THE BOUT COULD BE DESIGNED, AND ALL FOUR CAME BACK DIFFERENT FROM THE RECOMMENDATION.** The twin shorts COME OUT. The threading is SPENT in public. Severin spends **no earth at all** — only the mending, on both children, after the bout. And Kael pays a burn that is HERS plus the day's line-reinforcement. Then two follow-ups: the fence breaks mid-bout with Kael **refusing** it, because *his desire to win is greater than to follow laws, always has been a free mind* — an author ruling that is now standing character law and is explicitly **not** beat 16's refusal, since nobody asks him anything; and the threading stays single-bladed, so **dual threading and the BIND both stay banked.**

**THE STRUCTURAL CALL — AND IT WAS THE AUTHOR'S.** The bout was drafted into the open Ch. 25 because 05's NEXT TARGET and the opener both said to. At ~3,900 words that instruction was describing a chapter that no longer existed. Asked *why did you append?*, the honest answer was that I followed a standing instruction instead of re-pricing it against the draft. **Split at its own seam** — the send-off ends Ch. 25, the bout opens Ch. 26 — which rhymes with Ch. 22 → Ch. 23 one round earlier. Ch. 25 renamed **"The Door"** (both parents at it, the bundles beside it, and Ch. 12's *the door has held* standing behind it without being re-minted). **LESSON: a plan in 05 describes the chapter that existed when it was written.**

**THE FIGHT WAS REWRITTEN WHOLE, ONCE, AND THE ENDING TWICE.** The author's verdict on the first version: *kinda confusing at points, and frankly not fun or dangerous enough. lots of random callbacks.* — with a brief that became the chapter: fire bursting out of her hands in multiple directions while she lunges; Kael dodging, blocking, baiting, one blade threaded to slash through fire and the other going at her heavy; he gets scorched, she gets cut, she is fast and he is faster; **he never just defends, he counters at every chance, making her REACT and spend, whittling her down while taking minimal damage.** Then the ending was replaced again: **both emptied, no Art left to either, pure steel, dual daggers against a longsword, and he disarms her and she kneels from exhaustion.** With the flash the author asked for and rules as truth about the boy — **the smile he is aware of, because when a fight turns critical he simply follows instinct.**

**AND THE BEAT THAT CAME OUT OF IT LATE:** Severin, who knows exactly what those two people were, watching what they made and finding it *just as terrifying as the two weapons of the Sanctum he once saw* — and knowing they never put the love of it there. On the page it is four lines and **he sources nothing:** *"Your father and your mother taught you to fight." / "Yes." / "They did not teach you that."* Then, on the author's note that he should offer kind advice, the only thing his doctrine owns: *"You watch it."* The old enforcer who believes education can outrun rot, meeting something education did not put there, and answering it with more teaching.

**THE FINDING OF THE SESSION — A PHANTOM *FACT*.** The author asked: *she fights with fans? was that in past chapters?* **No.** `03/elarine`, `05` and `07/chapter-records` all carried **hand-borne fans**; the page has *"a flat fan of flame sweeping from her palm at knee height"* (Ch. 11) and Aurelian throwing *"a fan of fire"* (Ch. 17). The word is a SHAPE, not an instrument, and I had built a bout's worth of mechanics on the object reading before the question was asked. **`check-quotes` structurally cannot catch this, because it was never a quotation** — it was an unquoted invented noun. **And it is the second phantom in two sessions to live in `chapter-records`** (s29's welded birds line was the first), which is not coincidence: **the tiered cold read has the record substitute for the chapter, so a record's invention is never checked against a page again.** Corrected at the origin in all three files. Proposed and not adopted: when a chapter is designed off a character sheet, spot-read that sheet's source chapter for its load-bearing nouns first. **The fix improved the bout** — she fights the empire's doctrine, sword right and off-hand for the Art, the same silhouette Kael dismantled the day before, held by the one person who watched him do it.

**AUTHOR CRAFT NOTES, APPLIED TO THIS CHAPTER ONLY** (taste, not rules; no retro pass, nothing into 06): **relative distances, not surveyed ones** — *four feet high, ten feet in front* became *higher than a man and the whole width of the floor, well out in front of her*, and Ch. 26 now contains **no numeric distance at all** except *the inch*, which is the line-reinforcement and earns its number. And a pre-bell exchange was written, rejected (*I do not get this dialogue* — it leaned on Ch. 24's name-billing and its punchline had no clear referent) and replaced with one that says one plain thing each and makes a promise the fight then keeps.

**MY OWN DRIFT, CAUGHT AND SENT BACK.** The first draft ran **and 55.2/1k uniform across every section**, with the mending, the Elarine scene and the hill walk all *above* the fight — the exact s29 diagnosis, drift and not cadence. Two trim passes fixed the distribution; dashes came 5.1 → 2.9/1k; one not-but rewritten. **Final Ch. 26: dialogue 9%, 22.9 w/para, dashes 2.9/1k, and 46.6/1k with the excess only inside the fight and the quiet close at 33–36.**

**AND THE PARSER TRAP, RE-SPRUNG THREE TIMES IN ONE SESSION BY ME.** A chapter title quoted inside `protected-phrasings` was adopted by `check-echoes` as a protected phrase (the s25 lesson, verbatim); and three struck lines plus one of the author's own spoken sentences were quoted inside the s30 status block instead of italicised. All four caught by running the instruments after writing the ledgers rather than before. **Run the instruments last, not first.**

**VERIFIED, NOT ASSERTED:** `check-quotes` **1,077 spans checked, 0 phantoms** — every quotation in both new chapter records confirmed against the committed page. `check-echoes` **zero script hits on the new prose**; full-manuscript parity **22 → 23**, the single addition being the deliberate re-run of Ch. 23's doctrine line. Clue parity **166/6/4/10**, headers equal to row counts in all four sections. `wc -w` baselines matching on both chapters (and Ch. 26's baseline corrected from a Python word count to the canonical `wc -w`). All six touched 03 sheets still carrying their three sections — the splice hazard checked, not assumed.

**A TOOL GAP FOUND AT THE CLOSE — `sync-reader-notebook.sh` ADDS BUT DOES NOT REMOVE.** Renaming Ch. 25 left the notebook holding **both** "Ch. 25 — The Third Bell" and "Ch. 25 — The Door", which are the same scene, so every future clean-reader probe would have seen the last ordinary night twice and nobody would have known why the answers were strange. Removed by hand (`nlm source delete <id> --confirm`); the notebook now holds 26 sources for 26 chapters. **Standing consequence: after any `git mv` in `manuscript/`, list the notebook's sources and delete the orphan — the sync script will not do it for you.**

## SESSION 31 — 2026-08-23 (COLD-READ AUDIT ONLY. Five findings, all cleared. No prose; no baseline moved) — RECORD WRITTEN AT THE SESSION-32 OPEN

**THE SESSION DID NOT CLOSE, AND THAT IS WHY THIS ENTRY IS DATED FROM THE NEXT ONE.** s31 opened on a clean tree, ran the cold read, produced a five-finding audit, cleared it, and committed it at `38db122` — and then stopped. No session record was written, no close block was added to 05, and **05's NEXT-SESSION OPENER was left addressing session 31**, so the following session would have opened on a paste-block aimed at a session that had already run. **The difference from s27 is the whole of the difference:** s27 lost the reason a chapter draft was discarded, which cost the next session a redesign. s31 lost nothing — every finding, every fix and every parity number was written into 05's `s31 open` status block before the commit, so this record is a transcription rather than a reconstruction. **The lesson is not about loss, it is about the streak: a session that banks its work in 05 and skips the log leaves the doc system with no place that says the session happened.** Written at the s32 open, from 05 and `git show 38db122`.

**THE FINDING THAT MATTERED — §ARC STATE WAS A FULL SESSION STALE.** Its header read *as of session 29 close*, its newest entry described Ch. 25 as an unfinished opening — which it stopped being when the bout split out at the s30 close — and it carried **nothing whatever on Ch. 26**: not the round of 8, not the broken prohibition, not the threading in public, not the smile, not the announced collision. §NEXT TARGET and §HOLDS & OWED were both fed at that close; ARC STATE was not. **This is the block a future session reads for *where are we*, and it was answering with a chapter that no longer existed in that shape.** Header restamped, the *opening only* qualifier struck, Vask's door question added to the Ch. 25 line, and a full Ch. 26 entry written. **The structural point s31 drew: the s30 close fed the two blocks that face FORWARD and skipped the one that records where the story now stands, and the tiered cold read cannot catch that, because ARC STATE is not checked against anything.**

**THE OTHER FOUR.** (2) **Two word counts for one chapter, in one file** — the s30 status header called Ch. 26 5,088w against a canonical `wc -w` of 5,078; the s30 log records the baseline being corrected from a Python count, and the status block kept the pre-correction number. (3) **`07/index` stale on two of eight** — timeline 814 → 965w, standing-rules 947 → 1,191w; all eight recomputed, the other six exact. (4) **Two 03 Glance stamps a chapter behind** — `valeria` and `aeliana` both read *as of Ch. 24* while carrying Ch. 26 DYNAMIC lines. (5) **`03/aeliana`'s orphaned Ch. 23 block removed** — 104 words, the sheet's only un-bulleted DYNAMIC entry, stating the naming device for the third time in one section and carrying the dead claim *the audience itself is unwritten*.

**THE NEAR-MISS WORTH KEEPING, and s31 was right to keep it.** The first-bout stillness detail inside that deleted block sat in the sheet **unquoted**, so `check-quotes` was structurally blind to it — the s30 phantom-*fact* class — and it had to be checked against the manuscript by hand before deletion. **It was real** (Ch. 23), and it is homed in the departure ledger. **Disbelieve the sheet, then check the page, then delete.**

**CARRIED OUT OF THE SESSION UNRULED — THE DAY'S CARD.** Ch. 26 closes on *four bouts from the end of the second day*; on the Gold bracket alone the round of 8 is four bouts and Kael's was at the third bell, so a bracket-counting reader gets one left, not four. s31 flagged that it holds if the bells are hours and the Silver league's bouts are in the count, declined to rule it, and wrote *rule the card before drafting, not during* into 05. **RULED AT THE s32 OPEN — see SESSION 32.**

**VERIFIED AT THE TIME, AND RE-VERIFIED AT THE s32 OPEN:** `check-quotes` 1,074 spans / **0 phantoms**; `check-echoes` full-manuscript parity **23**, unchanged; clue parity **166/6/4/10** (headers = rows); `07/index` all eight exact; reader notebook **26 sources for 26 chapters**; `wc -w` baselines untouched on every chapter; git in sync with origin.

## 05 STATUS-CHAIN ROLL-OFF (rolled at the session-32 close, verbatim)
- **s23 (2026-08-22) — THREE AUTHOR RULINGS, no new prose. (1) THE THREADING WAS NEVER *GIVEN* TO VALERIA.** He **SHOWED** it to her — spent it ON her in a losing exchange and eleven more times at her asking, slower, so she could watch for it. **She is EARTH and cannot use it**; nothing about seeing a lightning thread laid into steel a dozen times puts it in her hands. What Kael spent is the CONCEALMENT, not the technique: she has the sight of it and the question of what it was, and that is all she will ever have of it. Corrected in `07/planted-clues` (row retitled THE THREADING SHOWN TO VALERIA, handling rewritten), `07/chapter-records` Ch. 17, `03/kael`, `04` beat 13, and this file's ARC STATE; a hard FENCE added to `02/magic.md` — do not let her acquire it, the same rule as AIR. *(The page's own "He gave her the threading" reads as giving a blow, which is what it is; it is what seeded the ledger's misreading and is left standing.)* **(2) 03's DYNAMIC BLOCKS ARE STANDING STATE, NOT CHAPTER HISTORY.** Perma-refreshing them chapter by chapter is too expensive and was duplicating 07. All 15 sheets recast: present tense, one line per fact, **no chapter stamps, no "Previously", no beats.** 3,475w → 2,377w; sheets 8,824w → 7,727w; a chapter now moves 2–5 lines instead of a block. The contract is written into `03/index.md` with its admission test (*could a future chapter contradict this line?*). **ZERO-LOSS VERIFIED, not asserted:** 159 units checked against 07 + the new blocks; every one homed; the two with no home anywhere (the sibling-evidence beat; Neris's warmth spent once, on the head of an unborn child) were filed into 07's Ch. 19 record, and Neris's spend-ledger line restored as state. The pass also cleared **stale state the stamps had been preserving**: Kael's *cannot tell anyone why he is not enrolling* (dead since the entry), Valeria *"observed… unknowing"* (dead since the catch), Severin's concession door (CLOSED, 00 s21), and Vask's Glance *"the prohibition his mouth"* → *the extraction plan his mouth*. **(3) THE "OLDER THAN" LINE — STRUCK, AND IT WAS NEVER TRUE (author ruling, s23).** `07/protected-phrasings` carried **There had never in his life been anybody he was older than** (unquoted — struck, never on any page) under Ch. 19, marked *protect absolutely* — and the sentence was never on the page. **Struck, and nothing added**, because as an absolute claim about his life it is FALSE: he is twelve at a Temple that takes *the young cohorts* (plural) into the yard and has a second cohort sparring below his. The true version belongs to the HOUSE, not his life — everyone he has ever loved is older than him, and nobody has ever been his to be older than — and **the page already lands it without a narrator saying it**: *"Will I be allowed to hold it,"* asked in the voice he asked things in when he was five, and *"I know about heads."* 00 s22's ruling (*he reacts as a boy who has never been older than anybody*) is a note on his REACTION and is fully paid by those two lines; it was never an instruction to state it. **Do not re-mint it.**

## SESSION 32 — 2026-08-23→24 (two days, one session per the numbering rule ruled in it. AUDIT CLEARED, LEDGER OVERHEAD CUT, and CH. 27 "THE FOG" COMMITTED — 4,656w)

**NUMBERING, RULED (author):** an audit-only session does not take a number of its own. s31 and the first day of s32 were cold-read audits; this is one session 32 with one record.

**DAY 1 (2026-08-23, committed at `9401549`):** the day's card ruled — a bell names an HOUR and a day's card is BOTH leagues, so Ch. 26's close stands and no prose moves. Four audit findings cleared.

**DAY 2 (2026-08-24) — FIVE MORE FINDINGS, THEN THE CHAPTER.** (1) **Two consecutive sessions failed to close**, and the block that went stale both times was §NEXT-SESSION OPENER — the only block that addresses the *next* session and the only one nothing checks. (2) **The pan-away tally retired (author ruling).** It read 1, 2 and 3 for one ledger across three files in two audits, and gated nothing after s29 established the device — *more work to keep the ledger than to write the prose.* Craft fences kept whole; the first-person slip ledger untouched, because there the count IS the rule. Zero-loss verified: all three instances homed in `chapter-records`. (3) **The 5,088 phantom had a second home** — `07/chapter-records`, the file the tiered cold read substitutes for the chapter. s31 fixed the copy it was looking at. **Grep the number, not the file.** (4) The spectacle reserve ruled SPENT. (5) `03/kael`'s FORWARD carried an instruction its own DYNAMIC had killed.

**THE CHAPTER — CH. 27 "THE FOG" (4,656w).** **THE RULING THAT MADE IT: NO FIGHTING.** The bell strikes to begin the semifinal and the invasion arrives in that instant; there is not one exchange between Kael and Valeria on any page, ever. **AUTHOR RULINGS:** the fleet out of a fog, trebuchets mostly, off hundreds of ships · Kael does not know about his mother, and the birth tension comes later · **Severin reaches the house** (resolving 00 s26, and Ch. 27 makes the lateness his own on-page choice — he could hold one stone and not two, so he stopped holding the sky and walked four hundred children out) · **they do NOT separate**: Valeria takes him inland to the walled nobles' district, and **the thing to hit is the GATE — she is admitted and he is not** · the island is emptied, not raided · the flinch spent, the lag and *"Val"* **de-listed** · **the raiders speak the downhill language and Kael understands every word**, having heard it in two mouths in his life, and he draws no conclusion.

**THE CRAFT NOTE THAT CHANGED SENTENCES:** *no need to justify that Kael understands what he is seeing; no *he was nine and did not understand*.* There is no retrospective comparison anywhere in the chapter. **AND THE ONE THAT ADDED A REGISTER:** the chaos wanted voices — fourteen unattributed fragments, all things he hears from where he stands.

**MY OWN DRIFT, THIRD TIME RUNNING.** First draft **and 52.5/1k uniform**; two trim passes to **41.9**, quiet sections 33–41, excess left on the bombardment and the gate. **This is my default, not cadence, and it needs checking per section at the end of every first draft.**

**THE DOC-SYSTEM RULINGS, AND THEY ARE THE AUTHOR'S.** **00's append-only rule is DROPPED** — worthless and counterproductive; it forced superseded lines to stay readable, which is exactly how three law-core files came to carry dead canon at s26. **00's test is narrowed: truths and things to hit — not how, not when, not in what order** unless deliberately fixed. `CLAUDE.md` amended on both. **A one-session 00 eviction pass is OWED** and is written into the opener.

**VERIFIED, NOT ASSERTED:** `check-quotes` **1,096 spans / 0 phantoms**; `check-echoes` Ch. 27 one script hit (*"Two at once"*, deliberate), full-manuscript parity **23 → 24**, that hit the only addition; clue parity **174/6/4/10** (headers = rows); `07/index` all eight counts exact; all 15 `03` sheets intact after the stamp edits; the s23 status block rolled to this file **verbatim, 3,095 chars, byte-checked**; reader notebook re-synced to **27 sources for 27 chapters**; `wc -w` baselines match on every chapter.

## SESSION 33 — 2026-08-24 (THE PLATE SYSTEM, author-directed. No chapter written; one clause of prose moved)

**What it was.** The author opened with an idea — illustrations for the chapters, AI-generated — and the session became a plate system plus, unexpectedly, a run of canon rulings. `08-Plates/` was built, torn down at the author's word once he had a better method, and rebuilt around his own `portrait-prompt-system.md`. **Nothing in the day was the standing target;** the fire (Ch. 28) was never approached and is untouched.

**The system, as it now stands.** One fixed STYLE block pasted verbatim, a short per-character SUBJECT block, generated **unanchored** for portraits — which is what finally killed the age drift, since every anchored run inherited an older face and aged it further — and **anchored** only to extend an adopted face into a full figure. Adopted: six face portraits at two ages each with the +1 age gap preserved, plus full figures for Aeliana and Valeria. Kael's full figure is owed.

**THE CANON, which is the part that outlives the pictures.** An image cannot decline to answer, so every drawing forced a question the prose had been free to defer.
1. **THE VESSANTINE SILVER** (author ruling) — hereditary house colouring and **not a mark** — with the drain-mark's own register named **PURE QUARTZ WHITE**. It cost nothing to establish because the page had already planted the image in Ch. 8: *one clean seam of it, like quartz through rock*. Written into 00, `02/magic`, `07/magic-snapshot`, `03/aeliana`. **Undesigned consequence, and worth having: a Vessantine is identifiable on sight, hereditarily and permanently** — including one who ends Book One in a boat with an arc 00 leaves open.
2. **KAEL IS HANDSOME** (author ruling), and filthy with it, explicitly not the ornate royal register — which is Aeliana's, and the author's own distinction is what keeps her *never in competition* without Kael having to be plain. **The load-bearing move was a re-basing, not a deletion:** *what others react to: nothing* now rests on **status and stillness** — no house, no name, working cloth, a body trained not to move — instead of on his face, so the long inversion still pays when the island starts looking at him. `03/appearance-ledger`'s plain-cast line rescoped: **plain means unadorned, not plain-featured.** Vindicated by the tool itself, which cannot render *plain* against its attractiveness prior at all.
3. **AELIANA: long silver hair, pale blue eyes.** Both were free — Ch. 22 spends the gown, the gold and the construction of her hair and never once its colour.
4. **THE TWO VISUAL REGISTERS** (`02/geography`) — the island keeps its own material culture, the capital is Latin-Byzantine. **Recorded by default rather than explicitly ruled**, because three anchors generated with nothing steering material culture split the island from the capital by themselves, along exactly the seam 02 already believed in: a conquered city keeps its own tongue's name, and the empire does not rename what it takes.
5. **KAEL'S BLADES ANSWER TO NO SCHOOL AND NO SELLER** (`02/dueling`) — outside the empire's honourable-arms grammar because Vask's school is its antithesis, not a cheaper version of it.
6. **THE CH. 24 BURN SITED — the LEFT forearm**, with its cost written down rather than discovered later: it sits on top of the Ch. 6 blade scar and makes the tally-mark rhyme with Vask quieter in plates than it is in prose.

**THE ONE PROSE CHANGE.** Valeria's hair, lengthened **on the page**. The author asked for the story to match the images; the check found the length was page canon, not sheet canon — Ch. 14's *Black hair to her shoulders, kept clear of ornament* — so the bible could not simply be amended without putting it in conflict with the manuscript. The page moved instead, one clause, cadence untouched: **well past her shoulders.** Chain fed through `03/valeria`, the Ch. 14 spend in `07/chapter-records`, and the baseline **4,302 → 4,303**. No conflict elsewhere: Ch. 27 has her hair *bound* for the bout, which long hair satisfies at least as well.

**FIXED IN PASSING.** `03/valeria` STATIC called the microscopic flinch *unspent — banked* while its own DYNAMIC recorded **THE FLINCH IS SPENT (Ch. 27)** — stale spend-state inside a STATIC block, the class that gets read as live and spent a second time.

**A RESTRUCTURE, AND THEN ITS DELETION.** The anchor sheets were rewritten from session narrative into production documents at the author's direction, under zero-loss discipline: two orphans were found and rehomed *before* anything was cut (the burn's siting to `03/kael`, the coat-is-a-metaphor rule to the style anchor), a shingle pass over all 127 old non-empty lines left 32 substantive ones unmatched and each was classified, and 15 migrated claims were re-verified by content at their destinations. The pass caught a real staleness nothing else would have — the style anchor still calling the scene palette OPEN after an image had settled it. **The whole tree was then deleted an hour later at the author's word**, recoverable at `29bcbfb`, and the canon above was explicitly kept.

**VERIFIED, NOT ASSERTED.** `check-quotes` **1,120 spans / 0 phantoms** at every commit of the day. `check-echoes` on the revised Ch. 14: 16 protected phrases, all of them the chapter's own, none touching the changed clause. Reader notebook re-synced, Ch. 14 replaced. `verify.sh` green at close.

**THE GAP FOUND THE HARD WAY.** An image file left the working tree unnoticed at `d6a9a82` and had to be recovered from `2767ab6`. **`verify.sh` checks the chapters and 00 and nothing else** — no file under `08-Plates/images/` is guarded by any instrument, and the tree can lose one silently. Worth an instrument if the plate library grows.

**THE STRUCTURAL LESSON OF THE DAY.** The prompts are written in a separate tool that **cannot see 00, 02 or 03**. Every canon error of the session came from that one blind spot and none from craft: pale blonde when silver had been ruled that morning, a pauldron on a girl the book calls *a symbol learning to boil water*, hair against a sheet, a single longsword on the girl whose entire martial identity is the twin-blade heresy, a collar insignia against a clue banked for the fire. The division of labour is sound — that side does style, this side does canon compliance — but it needs an instrument. **A CANON BRIEF IS OWED:** a short paste-able per-character sheet of the appearance facts that may not be violated.

**OWED INTO SESSION 34.** Kael's full figure (prompt written, audit attached — he carries no weapons, and his two references disagree on clothing in a way that quietly literalises THE COAT). The canon brief. And, still, **the 00 EVICTION PASS**, owed since s32 and untouched.

- **[SUPERSEDED s20 → §THE UNLEASHING GATE]** **The true unleashing: RESERVED — much later in Book One.** Leading candidate: THE FIRE. ## SESSION 34 — 2026-08-24 (THE PORTRAIT SYSTEM CONTINUED: five faces, one new canon ruling, and 08-Plates made self-contained. No chapter written; no prose touched) — RECORD WRITTEN AT THE SESSION-35 OPEN

**Why this record was written late.** The session ran eleven commits (`c55ff83` → `6a126b3`) and never closed: no `05` status block, no record here, and `05`'s NEXT-SESSION OPENER still addressed *SESSION 34* — the number this session had already spent. **Nothing was lost** — the plate work is recorded inside `08-Plates/` in full detail, and `03/changelog` carries its s34 line — so what follows is a transcription from git and from those files, not a reconstruction. **The structural cause is new and is the point:** this session's own ruling took `08-Plates/` out of CLAUDE.md and out of a writing session's concern, and the session that made that ruling was itself a plate session. **It wrote itself out of the protocol that would have closed it.** The two things it did *outside* `08-Plates/` — a CLAUDE.md amendment and a `03` canon ruling — are exactly the two things that then had nowhere to be recorded.

**WHAT WAS ADOPTED.** Face portraits for **Aurelian (14)**, **Elarine (14)** and **Neris**, at `08-Plates/images/`. Plus one method test, `scene-tests/quartet-conversation.png`.

**THE ONE CANON RULING, and it is the same class as s33's silver.** **AURELIAN'S EYES ARE AMBER / GOLD-BROWN (author ruling).** His eye colour was unruled in 00, 02 and 03 and unspent on every page, and the portrait system cannot write a SUBJECT block without one. Chosen to carry fire's warmth in the colouring beside the dark-gold hair without either being named as fire. Fed to `03/aurelian` and the canon brief; **unspent on any page and free to stay so.** *Drawing forces what prose was free to defer* — twice now. **Checked and clear in the same pass:** `03/elarine`'s *dark eyes* needed no ruling, and the page's deliberate refusal to name their colour (*her eyes did what her eyes did*, Ch. 14 and Ch. 15) is now protected in the canon brief as a thing a plate may not spend.

**NERIS, RULED THREE TIMES AND EACH TIME CHECKED FIRST — which is the habit s33 paid to learn.** Hair **long and loose** (her hair appears nowhere in the manuscript and nowhere in `03` beyond *dark-haired*, so no prose moved); hair **dark brown** (Kael's hair colour is not on any page either, and `03`'s *dark-haired* already admits it — so no sheet moved, the maternal inheritance stays true, and no adopted image was invalidated; a *mid*-brown would have been a different question and would have had to reach Kael and Wren); **eyes dark**, which is what she looks like every day of her life — she can switch them off (00 s20) and the Kynemir red is the truth underneath and is **not** what a canonical reference shows. **Each of the three was checked against the page before it was changed, and each cost nothing.** Vask was revised before his first run off what Neris's run taught, and has not been generated.

**THE METHOD FINDINGS.** (1) **The style block split three ways** — STYLE / AGE / HAIR — forced by a successful run: the "identical across every character" block never actually was, because **age anatomy is per-character by nature and is the one thing a shared block cannot carry.** (2) **The cheek-colour clause CUT from the fixed block** — `most colour in the cheeks, nose and ears` was taken literally; the ears running hot was logged as a nuisance at s33 and Aurelian made it a fault, because **a hot cheek competes with the eye for the one saturated point a picture is allowed** — fatal to a warm eye, expensive for a dark one. Cutting it is what let the amber win. (3) **HAIR DEMOTION** — a dark eye cannot beat dark hair on value, because the hair is twenty times the area; render the hair as open washes with bare paper through it and the eye wins. Neris proved it on a face with dark hair, dark eyes and no colour in the frame. (4) **`no freckles` does not suppress freckles**, because the fixed block asks for pigment granulation and negating a trait cannot beat an instruction to granulate — ruled TEXTURE, not a mark, and the fight abandoned. (5) **Four figures work**, raising the tested scene ceiling from three, with no garment bleeding between them. (6) **Filenames drop the age where `03` leaves it unpinned** — a number in a filename gets read as canon by the next session, and Vask's and Neris's ages are deliberately open.

**THE DOC-SYSTEM RULING, and it is the author's.** **`08-Plates/` IS SELF-CONTAINED.** CLAUDE.md now carries one line about it and nothing else, so the plate system costs no context in a writing session; its contract, its admission test, its eviction path and its hard rules live in `08-Plates/README.md`. **THE PLATE FOLLOWS THE PAGE moved with it, verbatim** — it was a CLAUDE.md hard rule and is now the README's, unchanged in force. Not part of any cold read; opened only when doing plate work.

**CORRECTED AT THE s35 OPEN (the one defect the plate work left in canon).** `portrait-prompt-system.md` read *Kael's and Aeliana's freckles remain their sheet canon and their prose detail* — and **neither was true.** Freckles were in two SUBJECT blocks and nowhere else in the doc system or the manuscript. **A block's invention had been cited back as the sheet's, which is the one thing that file's own binding rule forbids**, and being unquoted it was structurally invisible to `check-quotes` — the s30 phantom-*fact* class exactly. Resolved in the sheets' favour at the author's word: `03/kael` and `03/aeliana` now carry them, unspent on any page.

- **STANDING INSTRUMENT (s24) — THE PHANTOM-PHRASING CHECK.** **What it is NOT:** protected phrases are never *required* to reappear. Most are said once and never again, and that is the healthy case — `07/protected-phrasings` is a repeat-DETECTOR (*echo deliberately or not at all*), not a must-use list. A chapter that hits one protected phrase is not thin. **What it IS:** a check that every entry claiming to be on the page actually is. Machine-check the quoted lines against `manuscript/` (punctuation-insensitive, fragment-level), then confirm each survivor with `git log -S"<phrase>" -- manuscript/` before believing it. **SEVERITY — this is the part that matters.** A phantom on its own is hygiene; strike it and move on. **A phantom with an INSTRUCTION built on it is a real bug**, because the instruction outlives the sentence and the next chapter gets written off it. Three of the four found so far were that kind: *"That is not why."* had 05's NEVER RESOLVE rule, `03/severin` and the clue ledger all encoding a contradiction between the parents that the page never stages; *and did not ask again, then or ever* had a standing KNOWN OPEN ruling protecting a sentence that does not exist; *"older than"* was marked *protect absolutely* and was untrue besides. The fourth (*the single most expensive thing…*) carried nothing and cost nothing. **So: chase the rules, not the mismatches.** **It is noisy** — speech-tag splits (*"Because I don't know what it does," Kael said, "and you're the only…"*) throw ~10 false positives per run and need eyes. Run it at each open until it comes back clean twice, then only when a phrasing gets a rule hung on it.
- s24 and older: rolled VERBATIM to archive §*05 STATUS-CHAIN ROLL-OFF*.


### 05 ARC STATE ROLL-OFF — chapter entries for Ch. 19–25 (rolled VERBATIM at the s35 eviction pass)

*§ARC STATE had accumulated eight per-chapter entries and 2,159 words, and **`07/chapter-records` carries a fuller record of every one of them** — the block was a second, shorter, independently-drifting copy of the same facts. It went stale in two of the last three audits (s31 finding 1; s32·day-1 finding 2) precisely because nothing checked it against the file that owned it. **New rule, s35: §ARC STATE keeps the last 2–3 chapters only**; older entries roll here and `chapter-records` is the home. The block's non-chapter lines — the part structure, the standing state, THE CLOCK and the slip ledger — are live state and stay in 05.*

- **NEW AS OF CH. 24: the empire looked at him up close and did not like what it got.** The princess sent for a thing to see it nearer than eleven thousand people had, found *nothing to him*, and ended the audience angry, because **he has no royal reflex at all** — absence, not defiance — and he corrected her about Drusane and asked what happens to the food on her table. **He reached no verdict and carried it out whole; the distaste is planted and unassembled.** Told to kneel and say his farewells he knelt and **had no farewell.** **THE CISTERN FOUR, one last time:** Aurelian is out of the games to MELORAN and tells it himself; Elarine refuses the bout as a subject and bills Kael for four years of *Sedrane*; **"Val" goes public and Aurelian and Elarine both take it up** — she still has not used it back; everyone laughs except Kael and Valeria, and **each sees the other not laughing**; and **Valeria says she will miss them, and smiles for the first time in the book.**
- **NEW AS OF CH. 25: the last ordinary night, and nobody in the room can do the arithmetic.** Vask was **sitting down**, which has never happened. Kael gave the audience whole and un-holed; **the hole is still Halden.** **Neris asked three questions and stopped**, which she has never done, and the love is entirely in the stopping. The child is days or a week away. **Two roped bundles by the door since the morning the games opened**, and *"when it starts, the master comes"* — and the reader already holds Ch. 22's *six days above that sand* and Ch. 19's *Severin must take the delivery*, so **00 s26's collision lands in the reader two days before it lands in the story.** Both parents at the door at dawn, and Vask's one tactical question answered on the step — *what has she not got?* / everything he knows about fire, and she taught him all of it.
- **NEW AS OF CH. 19: the mountain knows.** The tail reported and the entry told; Halden's name means nothing to either parent, **and they disagree, in front of Kael, about whose past is walking up the road — and cannot settle it.** The parents give the danger and never the history (three sentences, ratified). **NERIS IS ~3 MONTHS PREGNANT, on-page and plainly told; the family CANNOT LEAVE until after the birth** (four reasons, the load-bearing one sealed — 00 s22). Severin will deliver the child and find the hull, arranged before anybody asked. **THE CHEST IS PLANTED, LOCKED.** Kael keeps walking to the Temple — *a boy who stops going is a boy worth asking about* — differently, and carrying his own knife. **He will compete, and be taken off the field mid-tournament: stated in front of him, never asked of him, and he said nothing.**
- **NEW AS OF CH. 21–22: the routine ran one last time, and then the empire arrived.** Training is LETHAL — live edges, *Dead.* five and six times a morning, the throat the one place his father will not touch. **The dual-dagger style is BEGUN AND NOT HAD**: derived from his own constraints, refuted by his father's own school (*Get loose* — two full hands delete every escape he owns), and eight weeks of finding out how it fails. **Vask teaches the threading's route and sends him back to bare hands.** **NERIS HAS MOVED HIM INTO FORMAL SCIENTIFIC VOCABULARY ON PURPOSE** — retiring far-counting, what fire eats and the standing game to his face, giving no source, working past her hour at eight or nine months, going faster for a reason that is *true and is not the reason*; **Kael stands near the shut door and does not knock.** *Capacity* has replaced *the purse* in his mouth. **THE ISLAND IS FULL AND GARRISONED**; **AELIANA CASSIVARA VESSANTINE IS ON-PAGE**; the games' law permits sharp steel and unlimited resonance and **the First Master is the only thing enforcing it**; and **the empire has answered *Kael of what?* with a place.** He is 13, in the Temple's Gold six, drawn against LUCAN DRUSANE, and the first bout has been called.
- **NEW AS OF CH. 20: the two bonds are named and not symmetrical.** Elarine has called Kael a friend and **he could not say it back** — the word was never handed to him. Valeria has told him she is cold and always has been, that her house has no word for either thing, and that she feels no injury in the lack; she **refused** the *nobody stands beside you* verdict and answered *"Stand in front of me. It is worth more."* **He called her Val once. She did not answer it.** Oss is a question in Kael's head and nothing more. And Kael does not sleep: the attention will not turn back down, and **he has stayed out past *Evening* for the first time in his life.**
- **NEW AS OF CH. 23: he has won the first bout, and the world has a name for him.** He did not draw and did not spend one thread; a system built to fight at the measure had nothing to say to a boy with no blade and no distance. **He knocked Lucan Drusane out cold** after putting him down three times, and waited to see whether he would get up. **The standing ground named him THE GHOST inside a minute; the boxes said nothing at all.** He carries a second scar, above the left elbow. **The First Master declared him** — with his office, and not one grain of his Art. **Halden watched it smiling and Kael never saw.** **The princess has sent for him: alone, at the third hour after the closing bout.** He fights **ELARINE SEDRANE in the round of 8, tomorrow at the third bell**, and she has already spent a morning writing him down.


## SESSION 35 — 2026-08-24 (THE OVERHEAD PASS, author-directed. No design, no new prose; one clause of Ch. 27 corrected)

**What it was.** The session opened as a normal cold read against the fire as its target, and the cold read produced six findings. Then the author asked the question that replaced the session: **"why do we have to do so much god damn — are there things that we are updating and perma checking that are more work than worth?"** The design argument for Ch. 28 was deferred by him and the session became an overhead audit.

**THE ANSWER, AND IT IS WHAT MADE THE RULE.** I went back through the last five audits — roughly twenty findings. **Three were *a session failed to close*** (s27, s31, s34). **Exactly ONE was an error in the story** — Ch. 27's *half the field*, found this session, in 35 sessions of writing. **Every other one was a hand-copy of a fact disagreeing with another hand-copy of the same fact:** two word counts for one chapter in one file; a pan-away tally reading 1, 2 and 3 across three files in two consecutive audits; `07/index` drifting twice; 03's Glance stamps drifting in four consecutive audits; Valeria's STATIC contradicting her own DYNAMIC; Kael's FORWARD carrying an instruction his own DYNAMIC had killed; the spectacle reserve spent in one file and reserved in another. **The system was spending its audits reconciling its own filing, not protecting the book.**

**NEW HARD RULE (author-ratified) — ONE FACT, ONE HOME.** *If a script can compute it, do not type it. If another file owns it, point at it — do not copy it.* Written into CLAUDE.md §Hard rules with the evidence attached, because the argument is what makes the rule stick. Before adding any counter, stamp, tally or summary, name the file that already owns the fact; if one does, do not add it. **The author had already found this himself at s32** when he retired the pan-away tally on the grounds that *it was more work to keep the ledger than to write the prose* — this generalises it.

**THE FIVE CUTS, all approved in one line by the author and all run zero-loss.**
1. **05's OWN EVICTION RE-ENFORCED.** §STATUS had reached **thirteen blocks and 8,433 words — 57% of a file read in full every session** — against a session-18 rule that says *keep the last 2–3 inline*. The rule had simply stopped being run. **And every rolled block already had a full `## SESSION N` record in the session log**, so it was pure duplicate. §ARC STATE was the same shape: eight per-chapter entries, each of which `07/chapter-records` carries in more detail, and it went stale in two of the last three audits for exactly that reason. **Capped at 2 blocks and 2–3 chapters, in CLAUDE.md so it cannot rot back. 05: 14,924 → 5,934w before the close was written.**
2. **THE PER-SESSION CHANGELOG LINE DROPPED.** CLAUDE.md already ruled changelogs *not cold-read material* and *git the authority on what changed* — so ~300 words a session were going into three files (8,547w in total) that the protocol itself says nobody reads. Now written only when a change's reason will not survive in the file it changed.
3. **03's GLANCE CHAPTER STAMPS CUT.** The author's own objection produced the cleanest case in the system: *Wren does not exist — why do you want to update his character sheet?* He was right. The stamp was a hand-copy of `git log`, it drifted in four consecutive audits, and **it contradicted 03's own rule one line below it**, which already forbids chapter stamps in DYNAMIC. The Glance keeps the age, which is canon, and states what is true now.
4. **`07/index`'s EIGHT WORD COUNTS CUT.** No decision in 35 sessions was made off them; they drifted at s31 and again at s35. `wc -w` prints them free.
5. **04's EVICTION RUN.** The file's contract says spent beats keep a one-line pin; beats 12b–14c and 18 carried **1,426 words** of retelling, **852 of it in beat 18 alone** — all of which is law in `02/temple` §Tournament Structure and record in `07/chapter-records` and `07/planted-clues`. Collapsed to pins, which keep the beat numbers because those are the addresses other files cite. **04: 3,738 → 2,518w.**

**THE ONE PROSE CHANGE, and it is the session's only story finding.** Ch. 27 read *Half the field was gone in two days.* **Two days of single elimination removes three quarters, not half** — 16 → 8 → 4, on any reading of *field*, Gold or Silver — and Ch. 26 had closed on *tomorrow there would be four children left* one chapter earlier. 02 makes the bracket's legibility load-bearing, so a reader holding the structure got eight. Now **Two days had taken twelve of the sixteen**, **flat at 4,656**, baseline untouched.

**THE CANON CORRECTION THE PLATE SYSTEM LEFT.** `portrait-prompt-system.md` claimed Kael's and Aeliana's freckles as *their sheet canon and their prose detail* and they were **neither** — freckles were in two SUBJECT blocks and nowhere else in 00, 02, 03 or the manuscript. **A block's invention had been cited back as the sheet's, which is the one thing that file's own binding rule forbids**, and being unquoted it was permanently invisible to `check-quotes` — the s30 hand-borne-fans class exactly. **Resolved in the sheets' favour at the author's word:** `03/kael` (across the nose, under the weather and the grime) and `03/aeliana` (nose and cheeks, pale untouched skin), both unspent on any page.

**RE-HOMING, under the new rule.** §STATUS's one non-history entry — *STANDING INSTRUMENT (s24) — THE PHANTOM-PHRASING CHECK* — was split rather than rolled: its *what it is NOT* half is now the header of `07/protected-phrasings` (that file's own meaning), and its severity doctrine and noise warning are now in `TOOLS.md` beside `check-quotes.sh` (a rule about an instrument). **Added there while writing it:** the instrument is blind by construction to the worst class — *an invented fact that was never quoted* — which is both phantoms it has failed to catch.

**ALSO CORRECTED IN PASSING.** CLAUDE.md said 00's eviction pass was OWED; **s33 ran it**, and the note now says so and names what genuinely remains (the welded truth-plus-staging bullets, the author's call). Its script count read *five* against six in `scripts/`. `05` §HOLDS was headed *refreshed at the session-30 close* while carrying s32 entries.

**WHAT WAS DELIBERATELY NOT DONE.** `08-Plates/` is **not** guarded at session open — the author ruled it out explicitly, and the s33 note proposing an instrument for it is closed, not deferred. And I did **not** touch the bibles, `planted-clues`, `protected-phrasings`, `chapter-records` or the two scripts: the cold read is still ~107k words, because the bulk of it is the thing actually doing the work. **The relief is in the bookkeeping, not the reading** — three recurring finding-classes are dead at the source and the close drops from about seventeen artifacts to thirteen.

**AND THE STRUCTURAL FINDING OF THE OPEN, which is why s34 has a record at all: SESSION 34 HAD ALREADY RUN AND NEVER CLOSED.** Eleven commits after 05's last edit, including a CLAUDE.md amendment and a `03` canon ruling, with no status block, no log record, and the opener still addressing the number it had spent. **Third time in this class** — and the mechanism was new and is worth keeping: **s34's own ruling took `08-Plates/` out of a writing session's concern, and the session that made that ruling was a plate session, so it wrote itself out of the protocol that would have closed it.** Its record was reconstructed from git and the plate docs and appended above.

**VERIFIED, NOT ASSERTED.** Every rolled line located verbatim in its destination before anything was deleted: **14/14** status blocks, **6/6** arc entries, **8/8** beats. `check-quotes` **0 phantoms** before and after, spans 1,118 → 1,095 with the whole delta inside rolled material (the archive is not scanned). `check-echoes` full-manuscript parity **26, unchanged**. Clue parity **174/6/4/10**, headers = rows. All **15** character sheets still carrying Glance/STATIC/DYNAMIC/FORWARD — the splice hazard checked, not assumed. `wc -w` baselines match on every chapter and on 00. Reader notebook **27 sources for 27 chapters**, no orphans.

**OWED INTO SESSION 36.** The design argument for the fire, which is the standing target and was deferred by the author, not by the work.

- **INTERNAL REINFORCEMENT IS NOT A VEL SECRET (author ruling, s24).** A known earth technique; the Vel own the DEGREE, not the technique. Kael's line-reinforcement is his own route and is permanent kit — and it never becomes cheap: it empties him every time, and he cannot do it twice in a day.
- **THE "OLDER THAN" LINE — STRUCK AND UNTRUE (s23). DO NOT RE-MINT.** Full ruling in §STATUS.
- **THE THREADING IS SHOWN, NEVER GIVEN (s23).** Valeria is EARTH and cannot use it; do not let her acquire it (fence in 02 §Valeria, beside the AIR rule).

### §ARC STATE
## ARC STATE (as of the Ch. 27 close. **CAP: the last 2–3 chapters only** — older entries roll to the archive; `07-Story-Ledger/chapter-records.md` is their home.)
- **NEW AS OF CH. 27: the bell went, and the world ended on the same beat.** **The semifinal was never fought.** He walked onto that sand with no answer — a year of hunting one, and *everything he had ever taken off her, he had taken once* — told her so when she asked, and **the judge's hand came up and never came down.** **THE FLEET CAME OUT OF A FOG** that had sat on the island since the night before: hundreds of ships, trebuchets off them ranging on the arena, *it was not a landing, it was a pouring.* **Eleven thousand people tried to leave a stone bowl through four gates.** **SEVERIN'S EARTH IS SPENT AND SO IS ITS LIMIT** — he stopped one stone dead over four hundred children and could not stop the next, priced it in a breath and a half, turned his back on the sky and spent the morning walking students out; **his being late to the mountain is now his own decision, made on the page.** **THE MEN AT THE EASTERN GATE SPEAK THE DOWNHILL LANGUAGE AND KAEL HAS IT** — two mouths in his whole life, and he reaches no conclusion and has no minute in which to. **HE FROZE** — not fear: every school returning everything at once, all correct, *and a thing with no order in it cannot be started* — **and Valeria restarted him open-handed and asked him to come.** **He is walking INLAND, to a walled district, away from his mother.** *It was the wrong direction. He had worked that out before he took the first step, and he took it anyway.* **Oss was found and lost. Elarine, Aurelian, Halden and the princess are all off the page.** Day 3 of a printed six, and the games are over.
- **NEW AS OF CH. 26: he won the round of 8, and it cost him everything he had.** **The island says *Ghost* to his face** on the road down — a man takes his hat off, a woman puts a hand flat on his back, and two words stand in whitewash on a bed sheet. Against Elarine Sedrane he was beaten in the open inside four minutes, and then **broke his father's twin-shorts prohibition mid-bout** because he wanted to win more than he wanted to obey — **and told his parents all of it, blades included; the hole is still only Halden.** **The threading is public and it CUTS a working** (new page mechanic); he did **two at once** — earth into one line of his palm, the threading into the hand — and it emptied him of every grain in a fifth of a second. **His capacity is a dry hole and the whitening has been priced against his own head for the first time.** **The burn wrist-to-elbow is hers — the first mark on him he did not spend himself.** The ending was **pure steel**, both Arts gone: he took the length away from her, took the wrist and not the sword, and she knelt from exhaustion and did not get up. **HE WAS SMILING AND HE IS AWARE OF IT. Elarine saw it and will never say so; Severin saw it from the box and said so to his face** — *they did not teach you that* — sourced nothing, refused to moralise, and gave one instruction: *You watch it.* **Severin's MENDING is spent in public at scale, on both children, his hands not entirely steady; his EARTH is still wholly untouched.** **Elarine gave her *because*** — the win as proof she could still write her own destiny — **and a boy with an appetite took it off a girl with a reason.** The purpose thread is at its widest and is NOT discharged. **The collision is ANNOUNCED**, in her mouth at the gate. Day 2 of a printed six is spent; four children are left.
- PART I COMPLETE (Ch. 1–7). PART II COMPLETE (Ch. 8–13). **PART III COMPLETE (Ch. 14–22)**: Ch. 14 (beat 12) · Ch. 15 (beat 12b) · Ch. 16 + Ch. 17 (beat 13 SPENT; beat 15 PARTIALLY spent) · Ch. 18 (beat 13a — THE ENTRY) · Ch. 19 (beat 13b — THE MOUNTAIN HEARS IT) · Ch. 20 (beat 14 — THE WORD) · Ch. 21 (beat 14b — THE TIMESKIP) · **Ch. 22 (beat 14c — THE OPENING DAY; Part III closes here per the s26 rescope, and the same chapter half-spends beat 16, which is Part IV's — the beat list is a list, not a chronology).** **PART IV OPEN: Ch. 23 (beat 18 — the first bout) · Ch. 24 (beat 18 — the audience and the cistern four) · Ch. 25 (beat 18 — the last ordinary night) · Ch. 26 (beat 18 — the round of 8) · Ch. 27 (beat 18 closes / beat 19 OPENS — the bell, and the fire).** *(Fed at the s32 open: this line stopped at Ch. 20 and still read PART III OPEN six chapters after s26 closed Part III at Ch. 22 — `07/planted-clues` rows 182–183. `04`'s own Part III header carried the same staleness and is corrected with it.)*
