## SESSION 41 — 2026-08-25 · THE DISTRICT (Ch. 31 "The Line"), and THE FIRST INTERLUDE

**Written:** `manuscript/book-one/31-The-Line.md` — 6,399 words. and/1k 35.9, em-dashes 2, not-buts 0, protected-phrase hits 0, phantom audit 0. **The first draft ran 49.9 and/1k with three sections at 57–61** and took a full tic pass, which landed it at 35.2 — well *under* the book's 41–47 band, and left there deliberately: the and-chain is Kael's rhythm (child-logic accumulations grown into pattern-jumps), and Valeria is precise, formal and economical. The lower figure is the register contrast the interlude exists to carry. Roughly a third of the chapter is dialogue-led, so narration bulk is far under the raw count.

**SESSION OPEN.** `verify.sh` failed on a Ch. 30 baseline mismatch, traced to the author's own commit `a087f3c` changing *"Mother—"* to *"Mother. Is she—"* (+2 w) with no baseline refresh. Explained, not a continuity break. The cold read then produced four items, all done before any new work: (A) the amendment had aged `03/vask` and the Ch. 30 record, which both still said Vask *answered the one word Kael got out* — he now answers a question about the mother, with the child, and not with her; (B) four 03 Glance lines were still at Ch. 29's end-state against their own DYNAMIC blocks (Kael walking east alone while DYNAMIC had him carried home; Neris and Wren *hours or days away* through a labour that had started; Severin still in the arena); (C) Ch. 29's closing montage pre-narrated the eastward crossing Ch. 30 then stages, rewinding behind its own last step and doubling the lie-flat-and-let-them-pass beat — cut, 4,311 → 4,245, and the chapter now ends on the sound; (D) 05 §STATUS carried word counts and and/1k already owned by the baselines row and by these records.

**THE TARGET WAS SPLIT THREE WAYS.** 05 named THE HOUSE and it carried eleven load-bearing beats plus beat 16. Argued and ratified: **31 = the district · 32 = the house where a child is born · 33 = the house where his father dies.** A chapter that has to do a birth and a death cannot be present at either, and 00's *pure joy directly before the catastrophe* is a setup if the catastrophe is twenty lines later.

**THE AUTHOR'S CHAPTER.** The interlude was the author's idea and grew across the session into the whole chapter: Valeria inside the district, the reunion, a council of nineteen houses, the division, the goodbyes, the report. **06's *post-island acts* scoping for interludes is retired** and the first one is spent, whole-chapter, inside the fire; the per-use argument stands for every later one and the count is still the rule.

**WHERE CLAUDE WAS WRONG, AND IT MATTERED THREE TIMES.** First: told flatly that Severin *cannot* be in the district because he is on the mountain. The canon says he is fetched and reaches the house; it says nothing about the hour, and stacking an inference on top and stating it as canon was the error. The author then ruled the thing that made it work — **Vask went for Kael and left Neris alone; Severin arrives at dusk with them** — which is 00 s32's *alone for the beginning and not for the end* at full strength, and **00:89 was amended accordingly.** Second: the doorway scene priced the princess as a logistics problem and closed on *it did not once occur to her that there was anything else to have* — the exact inversion of 03's CORE. Rebuilt so the pricing IS the devotion. Third: the replacement then explained the indoctrination over her shoulder (*it had been put in her on purpose, by people who were right to put it there*), and the author's correction is the session's best note — a Valeria who can see it was installed is already half outside it. What is left is the want, and her pride that nobody ever had to ask her for it, which is 03's seam stated from the wrong side.

**THE AUTHOR'S TWO STRUCTURAL NOTES.** (1) The nobles love their children and see commoners as inferior, and the hypocrisy must reach the reader unstated: the sound off the hill runs under the whole council, nobody mentions it once, a man closes a shutter mid-sentence, and by the time the party is made it has stopped and only Valeria works out when. The adjacency then indicts the POV as well as the room — *she had been angry since the middle of the morning… It had never once been about them.* (2) The goodbyes were missing entirely, and then had to be scene rather than narration, in each house's own dialect.

**NEW CANON AND RULINGS:** the interlude scoping retired (06) · **OSS IS ALIVE** and in the party, reversing Ch. 27's *resolve nothing* on survival only — Kael is never told, and the arc is untouched · **00:89 amended** · beat 16 provisionally ruled **(b)** — he is told the plan at the house and answers a question nobody put to him · Severin's dark eyes spent (03, ruled s34) · the district's interior, its two gates and its dead north side, and the cut below the goat ground (02/geography).

**THE CONSEQUENCE NOBODY HAD PRICED.** Severin's hull is already canon as the family's — *something of his own, kept dry on their side of the mountain* — so the council spends Kael's family's escape on the empire's children, and the old man is the one who spends it. Nobody in that room knows and nobody in the kitchen will. It runs as dread under the whole birth chapter and no page may say it.

**AUTHOR EDITS ARRIVED AS DIFFS, four times, twice mid-edit** — which is the protocol working: one collision put a commit in with the wrong message and a stale baseline, amended immediately. Their trims took roughly 500 words out and are better than what they replaced; the narrated verdict on the Drusane man was cut entirely, so the page now passes no judgement on him at all.

**Caught by the instruments, not by reading:** Valeria counting her own kills (*she had killed nine men on the way*) against 03's **she has no first one and no number** · the escape party leaving by a *northern gate* three pages before the captain says the north side has no gate · a judges' box a hundred and eighty feet above water · a dialogue tag that lost its punctuation in a trim · and a phantom in this session's own chapter record, where a line split by a tag on the page had been typed as one continuous quote.

**Still owed and unruled:** Valeria's *"Understood, sir"* against Ch. 29's marked first-*sir* · two soft line breaks against three in the other thirty chapters · the author's Sedrane exchange conceding the argument is bad and then claiming to have taught it · and everything carried from s37–s39. **Taste note, not a rule:** 402 *said* to 2 *answered* across the manuscript is flagged as probably a weakness; new material may vary, nothing is codified into 06, and there is no retroactive pass.

## SESSION 42 — 2026-08-25 · PLATES: CH. 5 TO CH. 9 (image generation only)

**Twelve plates adopted, taking the folder from 8 to 20.** Ch. 5 `hand-on-the-rock` + `past-the-split-rock` · Ch. 6 `carried-past-the-rock` + `the-treeline` · Ch. 7 `kael-of-what` + `the-wasters` · Ch. 8 `the-hand-over-the-face` + `the-one-who-did-not-laugh` + `the-one-who-did-not-laugh-cold` · Ch. 9 `aurelians-candle` + `the-roller-alone` · Ch. 4 `the-master-arrives`. **No manuscript file touched, no `00`, no `04`, no `05`, no `06`.**

**THE PROGRAMME WAS THE AUTHOR'S CORRECTION AND IT IS NOW THE STANDING RULE.** Ch. 9's candle was built because a lighting proof had freed it, not because it was next, while Ch. 6 had none — *should be ch 6 next? 2 plates per*. **Chapter order, two per chapter, the first gap in the sequence is the target, no naming needed.** Recorded in `08-Plates/README.md`.

**THE GREY IS SPENT FOR PLATES (author ruling).** Asked why a plate could not show Kael's eyes, given the actual reason from `03` — the grey belongs to no one in the house, and its design is about which channel delivers it: other mouths, other eyes, never a mirror, and a plate is the book looking at him. Ruled: spend it. **Moved across all six live homes** (`README`, `canon-brief`, `scene-prompt-system`, `candidates`, `03/kael`, `07/planted-clues`), with the page staging unchanged and one consequence written down plainly: **the Temple mouth that was to deliver the grey now confirms rather than reveals, and no writing session may draft that beat as a reveal.**

**TWO THINGS THE AUTHOR STOPPED CLAUDE FROM CODIFYING, and the second is the more useful.** A prompt linter was built after repeated complaints about bloated prompts — *not sure a script is the way to do it, I'd like you to just be better at knowing what a good prompt is.* Kept as a backstop, with the ruling written into `scene-prompt-system.md`: **do not write a bloated block and then lint it down.** Then two colour rules were written off three runs, and the third run's "fault" turned out to be the best picture of the set and was kept as drawn — *no need to be a rule; case by case we do adjust.* Both sections are now evidence rather than procedure, and `README` carries the general bias: **the systems are for what reliably repeats; the pictures are judged by looking at them.**

**THE ONE RULE THAT DID EARN PROMOTION, four instances in four unrelated domains:** *dagger* → a near-longsword · *a plain dark coat* → a Victorian frock coat · *a soft sheet of flame off a candle* → a wick flame · *one upright standing stone* → a menhir. **A concrete noun arrives with a default picture attached and the default beats every adjective hung on it.** It has two failure directions — name the thing and get its default, ban the default's features and get nothing at all (*no guard, no ornament* returned a flat plank) — and one answer: describe the construction. **And a trap on the far side:** correcting a default takes clauses, and those clauses buried the fracture that gives the split rock its name. Say the defining feature first, with a measurement, then correct the shape.

**LIGHT FROM BELOW RESOLVED ACROSS FOUR RUNS, and the earlier reading was wrong.** It had been recorded as an in-frame/out-of-frame rule from a single run. The variable is **distance**: a candle on the bench lights a face, a town at the foot of a mountain does not, and the renderer is right to refuse the physically false instruction. That unblocked Aurelian's candle without waiting on the Ch. 8 doorway.

**A NEW CATEGORY: SOME MOMENTS ARE UNDRAWABLE.** THE PLATE FOLLOWS THE PAGE assumes spend-state is the only thing that can stop a plate. `ch06/the-wheel` — a bound child in a yard, the chapter's most human beat — was refused twice, the second time naming depictions of children. **The page is willing and the instrument is not.** Recorded as BLOCKED with a do-not-rebuild, and `the-treeline` took its place. A prompt fault gives a wrong picture; a refusal gives none — do not iterate on one. **And the first refusal taught its own lesson: a negation puts the word in the prompt.**

**A REFERENCE CARRIES EXPRESSION, NOT ONLY A FACE, and in a close crop it beats the prompt.** `the-fact-lands` opened with an override in priority position, in caps, and returned `kael-9`'s own wide-eyed asking face anyway. **The fix is a second reference, not a longer sentence:** `kael-9-still` was cut and wired into `build-prompts.sh` — same boy, same age, only the mouth, lids and brows move. Unrun.

**THE TWO-CLAUSE MECHANISM DID CHARACTERISATION.** `03`'s *forgettable at a glance and unforgettable the third time you notice she hasn't moved* is not a note about Elarine's personality; it is a value instruction, and it rendered. **And the two-subjects problem was solved three separate ways** — split across channels (value vs ink line), and finally by naming one thing both figures attend to, which is the cheapest.

**A CONTROLLED PAIR NOW EXISTS FOR THE OPEN FINISH QUESTION.** The bench plate was run warm-washed and cold-drawn — same staging, same crop, same mechanism — and **the author adopted both.** `README`'s earlier guess that `THE INK CARRIES THE CLOTH` was the lever is corrected: it reaches only garments.

**AUTHOR RULINGS, all scoped to their own picture:** the age and the gaze in `aurelians-candle` stand as drawn, against this session's own reading · Vask's parents look at Kael and their faces still give nothing, moving the attention and not the expression (the `vask-kael-goat` solution) · Severin stays in the mountain coat rather than Temple robes, because the two registers carry a fact · both bench treatments kept.

**Where Claude was wrong:** amended an adopted prompt in place twice and had to restore it byte-identical both times · deleted ~280 lines of `scene-prompt-system.md` with an unguarded span replacement, caught by the build check and restored from HEAD, nothing committed while broken · leaked markdown into a fenced block, which the linter now catches · asked distant lights to behave like near ones for two runs · and buried the split rock's split under its own correction.

**Still open:** the wasters' plank and the corrected object · the split rock's fracture, and the disagreement it creates with the adopted `ch05` plate · three ★ candidates passed over for being wide — Ch. 6's door, Ch. 7's Temple from the road (the book's main location, still undrawn) and Ch. 8's roll · `kael-9-still` unrun · `seeing-about-the-goat` unrun since s40 · and Kael reading older in solo frames, three times, one of them ruled correct.

## SESSION 43 — 2026-08-25 · PLATES: CH. 10 (image generation only)

**Two plates adopted, each on its first run, taking the folder from 20 to 22.** Ch. 10 `the-honey-and-the-bread` + `the-waterer`. **No manuscript file touched, no `00`, no `03`, no `04`, no `05`, no `06`, no `07`.**

**THE CHAPTER WAS TAKEN AS TWO CHAPTERS.** Ch. 10 is the Temple study and the mountain garden inside one number, and one plate from each half buys the pair more distance than any two moments from the same half — it also puts the only machine in the plate set on a page. **The author delegated both open decisions** (*you choose*): the pair, and the finish.

**THE FINISH QUESTION IS SETTLED THE ONLY WAY IT COULD BE.** `README` had the set drifting between glossy and drawn, with the `ch08` controlled pair as the instrument. **Only two of that pair's three clauses are about finish** — bare paper reserved by name, and the ink protected from a general softening; the third is cold hues on named objects, which is colour, and colour is adjusted per picture by the s42 ruling. So both blocks took the two finish clauses and each named its own temperature. **The set gets consistent where it was actually drifting and every picture keeps its own weather.**

**THE STAR MOVED OFF THE FERN.** *Nothing in the room said a resonance had happened* is the beat, so a fern that has lifted draws as a fern and the only way to show the lift is a sightline diagram. **It was kept as set dressing in the study plate instead**, on the sill, which establishes the room Ch. 18 comes back to at the cost of one clause. `the-honey-and-the-bread` was promoted out of the chapter's alternate, where the jar had been sitting as set dressing beside the empty book: **four chapters plant on that jar and none of them explains it** — Ch. 2's honey in the milk, Ch. 4's *you're the reason the honey's always gone*, Ch. 7's wall speaking what the honey said, Ch. 18's *jar of honey that was never mentioned*.

**THE FINDING OF THE SESSION: A POSE BEATS A REFERENCE'S MOOD WHERE AN OVERRIDE DOES NOT.** §What the reference carries had two runs of a stated override losing to a reference's own expression — the second in caps, in priority position — and concluded the fix is a second reference. **The honey block never argued with `kael-9`'s asking face; it put his eyes down on the jar**, and the asking face did not arrive, because a reference holds no downcast version of itself to supply. **One clause against a whole run.** Recorded on the sheet with a pointer from the rule file, marked one instance, not promoted. It also makes `kael-9-still` less urgent than it looked.

**AND THE SECOND WAY A NAMED TELL DIES IS OCCLUSION, NOT SIZE.** Twice in one session a block named a tell and then staged it out of its own picture. The honey plate was cropped close *because* `ch04` had lost Severin's wrists at full length and ruled the fix is crop — and then the garment paragraph ended his sleeves at the wrist, which is where a sleeve covers a forearm. **The instruction was physically self-contradictory and the renderer resolved it to the sleeve, correctly.** The waterer put Vask's temple-grey behind hair in a profile crop. **Neither would have been fixed by more emphasis.** Added to §Name the winner beside the size limit: read the framing, the pose and the costume back, and check the thing is visible in the picture you have described. **The fix shape is to name where the cloth stops on the arm, not where the sleeve ends** — the garment habit puts every clause on the garment, and the garment is not the subject.

**THE VASK REFERENCE WAS PUT TO THE AUTHOR AND THE PICTURES WON.** The waterer came back a lean, long-haired man in his middle thirties with no grey in him — and that is `vask.png` reproduced faithfully, not a prompt fault: **the anchor carries no temple-grey and does not read *weathered far past his years*, so no plate anchored on it ever will.** `03` and the brief agree with each other and the picture in the library is the odd one out. Both ways were priced — a re-cut leaves four adopted plates showing a face the reference no longer holds — and the author ruled: *it looks like Vask to me.* **The reference is not re-cut, the four plates stand, nothing in `03` moves**, and the cost is written into `canon-brief` §VASK directly under the temple-grey line so no future session re-opens it.

**THE NOUN RULE TOOK A FIFTH INSTANCE, AND THE FIRST THAT LOOKED LIKE CONSTRUCTION ALREADY.** *A hollowed half-log a little longer than his forearm* produced a flat plank with one raised edge — water would run off its sides. **One adjective on a compound noun is a name, not a construction:** what was needed was the surface itself, *a channel scooped out along its length, the cavity a hand deep.* Same shape on the counterstone, which arrived hanging from a ring instead of lashed to the trough's short end, so the balance that stands it back up does not read. **The author adopted the picture and both stand as drawn**, kept as findings for the next picture with a mechanism in it.

**A HYPOTHESIS, DELIBERATELY NOT A FINDING.** The waterer's demotion clause lost — the drystone wall came back drawn stone by stone and competed with the hand. In every picture where the two-clause mechanism has worked, **the rival had somewhere to go**: deep shadow, a dark room, a cool wash. Flat open daylight has nowhere. **One run; do not act on it until a second says the same.**

**A TOOLING CHANGE THE AUTHOR ASKED FOR.** The built `.txt` is what gets pasted, so it now says which file to attach: `Image 1: severin.png — portrait reference of the old man.` **The filenames live in the block**, so the sheet stays the single source, and `build-prompts.sh` lifts the reference paragraph above the house Medium and Colour paragraphs — the order the scene skeleton already documented. **Blocks that have already run are not retrofitted**, since a prompt that produced an adopted image is the record of what ran.

**AUTHOR RULINGS:** faces are preferred but **not obligatory** in a plate, softening the s42 reading of it · the pair and the finish were both delegated · **`vask.png` is Vask** and the pictures stand · the waterer's plank and its ringed counterstone stand as drawn.

**Where Claude was wrong:** wrote a physically self-contradictory clause into the one plate whose whole crop existed to render the wrists, and lost them again · named the temple-grey and then chose a profile crop that hides both temples · hoisted the `Image N:` lines by line rather than by paragraph on the first attempt, which orphaned the second half of five wrapped reference instructions in the middle of their files, caught by a parity check before it was committed · told the author the waterer's face was not Vask before checking that the reference is exactly that face.

**Still open, unchanged from s42 except where this session moved it:** the split rock's fracture and the disagreement with the adopted `ch05` plate · Ch. 6's door, Ch. 7's Temple from the road and Ch. 8's roll, three ★ candidates passed over for being wide · `kael-9-still` unrun, and now less urgent · `seeing-about-the-goat` unrun since s40 · **the Ch. 10 alternate keeps the empty book**, and `the-mist-off-the-palm` is held rather than rejected because a near warm source under a face is `aurelians-candle` one chapter back.

## SESSION 42 — 2026-08-25 · THE MOUNTAIN (Ch. 32 "The Mountain"), and THE MARK

**Written:** `manuscript/book-one/32-The-Mountain.md` — 6,075 words. and/1k 40.1, em-dashes 12, not-buts 0, phantom audit 0, protected-phrase hits 8 (all newly registered from this chapter, plus one deliberate contrast echo). **The first draft ran 7,521 words at 50.7 and/1k** — the known default — took a full tic pass to 36.2, which overshot into Valeria's interlude register, and was brought back to ~40 by restoring the accumulations that were carrying Kael's rhythm rather than padding. The author's own passes then took roughly 1,400 words out of it.

**SESSION OPEN.** `verify.sh` clean. The cold read produced five flags before any work: **(A) "fetched" was false in six places** — Ch. 31 puts Severin at a council in the district all afternoon leaving for the mountain on his own initiative, while Vask went east for Kael and Neris was alone, so nobody fetched him, yet 00:81/85/88, 04 beat 17, 03/neris, 03/severin ×2 and 02/temple all still said he must be. **(B)** `03/severin` carried two contradictory `Now:` bullets, one still at Ch. 29's end-state. **(C)** 05's design note for the chapter assumed a two-room house — *"the other room"*, *"a birth heard through stone"* — against Ch. 1's **"The house had one room and a loft and the fire-hole and the door that held"** and Ch. 25's *"a one-room house on a mountain."* **(D)** 04 beat 16's stated motive (*because he wants the tournament*) died with the games. **(E)** 04 beat 17 still called the order of Vask's legs unresolved.

**THE TARGET WAS REBUILT FOUR TIMES, BY THE AUTHOR, MID-SESSION.** The agreed s41 design — an intimate one-room birth chapter on the instrument of proximity — was replaced beat by beat: the escape party arrives at the house · the army follows it · the house is held for the delivery · Kael fights · the party is cut from twenty-five to fifteen · the birth, Lucan and the fighting all move to the next chapter, and 32 ends at the encirclement. **Each collision was named and priced before it was written, and each was ratified rather than refused.**

**NEW CANON AND RULINGS.** 00:89 amended a second time — **Severin no longer arrives with Vask and Kael; he comes hours later at the head of the escape party**, and *he has to be fetched* is retired outright · **the house falls because a princess came to it** (the party brings the army; 00's raid causality untouched; Kael's hands are in the chain and he must never assemble it) · **NERIS IS DRAINED TO THE MARK** mending her son with nature out of her own element, taking the manuscript's first on-page whitening — permanent, two places, quartz-clean — which also spends her capacity, so her unsheathing will land on steel alone · **the coalition general leads the column**, resolving 00's *what he does next is OPEN*, and the column is ordinary men · **the concealment inside that house is over on purpose** (a SPEND, not a 06 amendment) · **the twin-shorts prohibition is answered as an action and dead** · **Oss is alive and Kael has seen him**, retiring s41's *Kael is never told* · **LUCAN DRUSANE DIES FIRST**, next chapter, with no redemption and his father's overruled warning proved right on a page that never says so · **05's *do not quietly heal him* is retired** — Kael fights, and the honest version is that a boy who cannot take a blow can only fight the way the island named him.

**WHERE CLAUDE WAS WRONG.** The party's size was invented at fifty-to-seventy against Ch. 31's explicit twenty-five, and populated with house men and a carried child who had all stayed on the district wall. The birth-count went through three failed attempts before it read: the referent was never named (*the last one* pointing at nothing until *a pain took her* was added), the unit was never stated, the eighty-second threshold was produced out of nowhere, and the yard report recycled the opening numbers while claiming the gaps had shortened. The Severin meeting had him arrive as a physician with no human beat in front of it. The yard stood about unarmed while fifteen unknown people came up a path on the night the island was being emptied. **And the beat-16 pin was written into 04 and the chapter record as SPENT when the author had cut the exchange — caught by `check-quotes.sh`, which found two phantoms and nothing else.**

**THE AUTHOR'S CRAFT NOTE, and it is the session's most useful output:** *"you love to mention things in the past, planted stuff, and as such we lose the present, lose creativity sometimes"* — and, sharpened: **it makes the prose less immersive and less emotional.** The first draft carried fourteen backward-looking constructions in 6,600 words from one narrow pattern alone. The author's own pass then demonstrated the correction four ways: **delete the sentence after the beat** (every instance, without exception) · **give a mouth what was given to a camera** (Vask, Severin and Valeria all gained lines) · **feeling on the surface beats withheld irony** (an apology, a thank-you, a kindness, a gaze on a belly) · **scenery gets one clause.** Calibration only — nothing codified into 06, no retroactive pass.

**AUTHOR EDITS ARRIVED AS DIFFS throughout, twice mid-write**, which is the protocol working: one collision meant a commit carried both parties' changes at once, and one of the author's own passes left a soft line break, a duplicated sentence and a stray comma behind, all repaired at the close and reported rather than fixed silently. **Left standing and unruled:** Severin conferring *the Ghost* on Kael in front of a royal officer, against `02/lexicon`'s hard rule; the flipped house description; and the thirty-minutes-versus-hours arithmetic.

**SESSION 42 — ADDENDUM (2026-08-25): the three carried items ruled at the close.** **(1) SEVERIN SAYS *THE GHOST*, and `02/lexicon` is amended to fit it** — the licence is narrow and named: a man *reporting* what the island calls the boy so that a captain of the royal guard will take a thirteen-year-old's arithmetic seriously, which is not an institution conferring a name. The rest of the fence is untouched: still the people's word, still minted in the cheap seats, and no herald, official, noble house or Temple register may follow him without a ruling. **(2) HOURS → MINUTES WAS DELIBERATE** — the child is close. Kael's early *"Hours. Three, four. It could be one"* is an early number made off almost no data and his *"Thirty minutes"* is the same instrument with more gaps under it; the estimate improving as the count accumulates is the character, and nothing needed fixing. **(3) The house flip stands as written.**

Age has to be written into the block or the picture is fourteen.

**AND THE AGE INSTRUMENT WAS ANSWERED, THEN WALKED BACK, IN ONE SESSION.** `ch11` ran an accidental controlled pair: two close-ups, same eye-line rung in the same words, same anchor ruling, same treatment. The stitch came back eleven and the fan came back a young woman, and **the only difference was that one block gave a ratio against an object in the frame and the other gave adjectives.** §A close-up plate and the age line was promoted to answered on that pair — and `ch12/furniture` then carried **both** instruments and still came back fifteen. **The refinement is the real rule: a ratio is only an instrument where the framing can perform the comparison.** The stitch put a hand and a copper bar side by side in one crop; furniture compared a hand in the foreground with a hand across a hall. **Same failure as a tell written under a sleeve — the fact is in the block and out of the picture.** Promoted on one pair and corrected one run later, which is the folder's own bias arriving as a lesson rather than a maxim.

**A HYPOTHESIS CLOSED PROPERLY, FOR ONCE.** `ch10/the-waterer` failed to demote its background in flat open daylight, and the guess written on that sheet was that **a rival needs somewhere to go.** `ch11/the-fan-in-the-rain` ran the same clause in the opposite condition — a dark wet yard, one small warm source — and it held completely. Two runs, opposite conditions, one cause, promoted to §Name the winner: **where a picture has no dark for a background to sit in, the instrument is the crop, not the clause.**

**AN ABSENCE INSTRUCTION HAS A CONSEQUENCE FOR THE GAZE.** The fan named the absence of everybody else, correctly per §Plates, **and then gave its subject's eyes nowhere to go** — so they went to the viewer and the plate slid toward a portrait, which is the exact line `README.md` draws between the two. **Every block since names where each visible face is looking**, and it has held twice.

**THE AUTHOR CUT THE NEGATION HABIT OUT OF A PAIR BEFORE IT RAN.** *This is so inefficient and redundant for image gen.* **The rule was already there and was the author's own** — §Negative inventory, s40, written after the same lines were cut from every run of that session with no strays appearing. Roughly 160 words came out of the Ch. 12 pair: both absence lines, four gaze negatives down to one, ten expression terms down to six, nine burning terms down to four. **What survived survived by the channel test, not by word count** — and **the cuts cost nothing across both runs**: no adult arrived in the hall, nobody arrived in the yard. First direct evidence in the folder for a rule it has had for four sessions.

**A CANDIDATE WAS REJECTED AFTER ITS RUN, WHICH IS NEW.** `ch12/the-hole-through-the-tree` came back with the best bare-paper reserve the folder has made and correct tally-marks, **and the author rejected the moment rather than the picture:** *I just think it's a bad illustration to show, should pick something else from the chapter.* The block, the run record and the staging fault stay on the sheet so nobody re-proposes it or tries to fix it. **The replacement was chosen from the candidate list rather than invented**, and is the better plate.

**THE ONE PLATE THAT NEEDED NO CORRECTION AT ALL** was `the-law-on-the-table`: four hands flat on the boards, the boy's in his mother's gesture directly opposite hers, a plant from Ch. 7 paying out. **Staged rather than prioritised** — §Name the winner has two runs where a small tell lost to a face, so the hands were put where they could not be missed and the faces were allowed to be finished too, and the hands still win the picture.

**AND ONE INSTRUCTION WAS PHYSICALLY FALSE AND THE RENDERER WAS RIGHT TO REFUSE IT.** That block named the lamp as the only light and then asked for *all the clothing* to stay cool — on three people sitting inside the lamp's throw. **Site the cool on what the source does not reach.** Kept on the sheet and deliberately not promoted: the two colour rules it would have joined were removed by the author at s42.

**AUTHOR RULINGS:** the fourteen-year-old anchors for every plate · both Ch. 11 plates adopted over a face that came back years past its anchor · the tree rejected as a candidate · **`neris.png` ruled good** against the brief's *handsome and unremarkable*, recorded beside the `vask.png` ruling. **That is five rulings in the same direction now**, and `README.md` says so: one consistent face across the book, over accuracy in any single picture.

**Where Claude was wrong:** promoted a rule to *answered* on a single controlled pair and had to walk it back one run later · wrote the negation padding back in after the author had already ruled it out four sessions ago · named a hand-to-hand ratio the frame could not perform · wrote a lighting instruction that contradicted its own light source · and staged a picture whose whole subject is a causal link without ever saying where the two halves of it sat relative to each other.

## SESSION 43 (2026-08-26) — THE BREACH
**Target:** 05 §NEXT TARGET — the house: the fight and the child. **Delivered:** Ch. 33 "The Breach", 9,790 words — the longest chapter in the book, and the one Book One was built toward.

**Open.** `verify.sh` clean. Cold read flagged four things: two stale lines contradicting the ratified s42 arrival ruling (`03/severin.md` DYNAMIC and `07/timeline.md`'s Ch. 31 row both still had Severin *arriving with Vask and Kael*) — fixed; `07`'s Ch. 32 record read *the board is still down* against 00/05's *up* — fixed; Valeria's crowd count in Ch. 31 being Kael's Ch. 29 number and instrument, produced after she was through the gate — **raised, still UNRULED**; and the register of four author-written lines in the Ch. 32 pass — **ruled by the author: it stands, and it is the point** (*it is good to see new sides of them; they are showing their raw selves amidst all this*). Scoped in 05 to that house and that night, no retro pass.

**Design.** Author supplied the beat sheet; the design argument named four collisions and priced them. **(1) The flicker vs. the sealed absorbed-state rule** — resolved by 00's own prescription (*through terror into the cold place*), which made Severin's reaction the instrument. **(2) Five commanders at a herder's croft** — resolved by 00 s42's own causality (*the house falls because a princess came to it*), so the convergence never implies the family was the target. **(3) The throw** — moved before Vask turns, since Kael must see the death; author then ruled it hand to hand rather than thrown. **(4) Oss's death** spends 03's unsealed *he will eventually wound Kael in earnest*; inverted rather than lost — the courage arrives under load once and kills him. Author overruled the planned knockout: **Kael watches the head come off.**

**Author rulings this session.** The coalition's five are **NAMED** on the page — PELLAN · DUNMAR · THIRO · FENNA · GALLOS — each with a different fire, amending the s39 bible-only ruling; the fence held to its tightest scope (**names in their own mouths only; no rank, no number, no structure, and *general* still on no page of Book One**). **PELLAN is the only one who dies**, killed by Vask. **The throw is hand to hand**, amending 00's *throws* and 04 beat 19's *as he dies*. **No knockout.** Neris's own eyes go red in the delivery, seen by Severin and the princess alone, and Severin takes a promise out of the girl on the spot. **Wren's eyes are red and his mother binds them** — Severin tears the strip and holds it out before she asks.

**Revision, chapter-by-chapter, all author-driven.** The fight had no start — added the wait, the four Temple children visibly frightened, Oss talking and then not, *"UP. UP. ON THE BEND."*, and Kael's own body going. Lucan's death was too fast and too cheap — Kael now shouts and the form will not let the boy turn in time, and stays with him until the head gets heavier, and Elarine presses a wound she has not worked out first and cries without knowing it. The princess got a body: a backward step she did not decide on, then obedience, then being very thoroughly sick against the wall. Thiro's kill of the royal guard was **summarised, which the author correctly called lazy** — rendered in full. **Valeria was killing a commander too easily** — rebuilt as a puzzle she survives rather than wins (a man who cooks a volume of air cannot cook the volume he stands in), and it earns Thiro's return as the reason Vask's arms are down at the end. Vask's last stand was expanded to attrition — spear through the thigh with the shaft broken off, arrows, burning, four hundred men refusing to come in — and his hands were made to **cut**. Author added the mother on the broken ground.

**Metrics.** First draft ran **62.8 and/1k** against 40–48 in the neighbouring chapters and **77 in the death section** — flagged by my own instrument as drift, not intent, and passed down to **43.5** before handing over; final **~45** across a chapter that grew 40% during revision. Similes 4.5/1k, dashes 0.7/1k, not-buts 0. `check-echoes`: one protected hit, *Everything heavy is lazy*, deliberate — the landslide's mechanism. `check-quotes`: 0 phantoms across 1,133 fragments.

**Process note, recorded against repetition.** Twice this session `git add <file>` swept the author's concurrent working-tree edits into a commit under my message; the first was amended to *Author pass on Ch. 32*, the second caught by diffing before staging. **Diff before staging when the author is working in the same file.**

**Canon written.** 00 amended in six places (the general named and killed; the throw; the flicker spent; Wren's eyes and the binding; Neris's control failing; the birth spent). New: `03-Character-Bible/coalition-generals.md`. Amended: 02 factions/naming/magic, 07 established-facts/standing-rules/planted-clues/timeline/chapter-records, 03 × 11 sheets, 04 beats 17/19/20, 05.

**Flagged forward, unruled:** the INSIGNIA beat (*an insignia the father recognizes with horror*) has lost its father and must move, be reassigned to Neris, or die; **the Phantom asked and not answered** has lost its only mouth; the chest's archaeology has lost the house that held it and survives only as the subtraction; beat 16 is still not cleanly spent.

Why is Kael looking into nothing.* The block said **his eyes up** in the gaze paragraph and **eyes level** in his expression paragraph two lines down, and horizontal won. **This folder reaches for *level* in almost every block it writes.** Where a gaze is not horizontal, the word cannot appear anywhere in the block, and the direction has to be restated as anatomy inside the expression paragraph.

**THE AUTHOR'S EXPRESSION FIX BEAT THIS SESSION'S OWN: *I think Kael should be kinda mad/surprised.*** Run 1 had him *mouth closed, jaw set, still counting* — **the coat, written into the one scene where the page takes the coat off him in public** (*all of it showed*), with an abstraction where a face should be. Run 2 changed that one paragraph and nothing else and the plate arrived. **Both gazes landed the same run, once both were written as anatomy rather than as attention.**

**PROMPTS ARE `.md` NOW (author ruling).** The extension only: these files are pasted verbatim, so no heading, fence or emphasis ever goes into one, and `prompts/README.md` says so. **Zero-loss verified rather than asserted** — 53 files out, 53 in, every one identical word for word; `build-prompts.sh --check` in sync; `check-prompt-bloat.sh` returning the same files, findings and counts with extensions normalised. The linter's default glob now skips `prompts/README.md`, which would otherwise be linted as a prompt.

**AUTHOR RULINGS:** hand crops are oversubscribed, the chest-up with a face is the default · `both-hands-full` adopted first run · `one-finger` re-run on the author's expression note and adopted · everything under `prompts/` in markdown.

**Where Claude was wrong:** wrote a sixth hand-and-object crop without counting the five adopted ones · wrote a mark extent past `03` and trusted a brief that was missing the fact · raised an age hypothesis on one run and had it bruised by the next picture in the same session · used *level* in two senses in one block · applied that lesson to one figure out of two the very next commit · dressed Kael in the coat in the scene that strips it · committed a block edit whose write had silently failed on a wrapping mismatch · and estimated word counts twice instead of measuring them.

**Still open:** Ch. 15 to Ch. 17 have candidates and no plates · **Oss and Halden have no references, and two Ch. 13 candidates wait on that** · Ch. 14's third candidate, the blood and the refusal, held as the same bout as `one-finger` · the laughter re-run on `the-spots-of-colour` · `kael-9-still` unrun · `seeing-about-the-goat` unrun since s40 · Ch. 6's door, Ch. 7's Temple from the road and Ch. 8's roll still passed over for being wide · `ch08.md` and `ch09.md` both cite a §Name both temperatures that no longer exists.

**Author's brief:** *a lot of redundancy, a lot of unoptimized stuff, a lot of bland… my favourite images are the portraits and the scene-tests… less bloated, more efficient system, not overcomplicating prompts.* The diagnosis was put to the author and approved whole (*i like all of it*).

Every plate inherited the portrait Colour paragraph (*the warmth belongs to the skin alone*) — 54 of 57 built prompts carried it, and the three that did not were the two author-written plates and the quartet. The value-structure paragraph, the named single source and the colour ban had become rituals carried by every block. Identity was restated per figure with a reference attached. The favourite register — the scene tests — had no template and, bar the quartet, no stored prompt. `check-prompt-bloat.sh` had passed a block the author called *very bloated*.

**AND TWO BUGS.** `prompts/portraits/elarine-14.md` had been built from the alternative eye clause that sat between her heading and her SUBJECT block — the build took the first fence. The full-figure prompts never received the closing line the doc said to close every SUBJECT with, and carried their `Image` line mid-file.

**WHAT MOVED.** `README.md` (3,196 → 1,144 words: contract, files, hard rules, standing rulings one line each) · `canon-brief.md` (3,078 → 1,322: facts and fences, no reasoning, no `00` truths) · `portrait-prompt-system.md` + `scene-prompt-system.md` (25,428) → `prompt-system.md` (8,088, more than half of it the verbatim blocks) · the author's recovered scene-test prompts → `scene-tests.md` (`### SCENE-VERBATIM:` blocks; the quartet moved in from the scene system). The four old docs to `08-Plates/archive/findings.md` verbatim. `check-prompt-bloat.sh` retired (author, s42: judgement, not a script). The plate sheets are untouched; they cite the archived sections by their old names and the README says where those are.

**THE RULING (author, s47): the scene-test register is the plate's starting shape** — one implied wall, soft daylight from one side, faces near and engaged with each other, colour named on the objects that have it. A named light, a demotion, a reserve, an absence list are instruments, not paragraphs every block carries. The template is `prompt-system.md` §Plate template, cut from `talk-kael-valeria` and the quartet.

**THE BUILD.** Plates take the 25-word scene style line (the references carry the medium); a plate with no reference gets the STYLE block's Medium paragraph; full figures get the `Image` line first and the closing line; scene tests build to `prompts/scene-tests/`. 63 files.

**ZERO-LOSS, verified:** all four old docs found verbatim in the archive; 37 fenced blocks, 35 live and two deliberately archive-only (the Elarine alt clause; the old scene skeleton, replaced by the template); built prompts 14 identical · 34 header swapped to the scene line · 5 header to Medium only · 3 full figures closing line added · 1 Elarine corrected · 6 new scene-test files; `build-prompts.sh --check` in sync.

**THEN THE FIRST PLATE ON THE NEW REGISTER.** `ch16/the-ear-on-the-brick` rewritten under the template (built prompt 884 → 524 words: the run-1 fixes kept, the value-structure paragraph, the appearance lines and the *only light* clause cut), run once, **adopted** (*pretty nice*). Warm throughout, landscape unasked, the grin in the one face it was written into; the drift is on the sheet and none of it worth a run. Ch. 16 has its two; the target is Ch. 17.

**CH. 17, THE FIRST SHEET WRITTEN UNDER THE TEMPLATE.** Two composed from the scene — the four in the last light (*who lives in it*) and Valeria in her doorway looking at her own hands — 570 and 355 words built. `her-own-hands` ran once and was **adopted** (*amazing*): the crop landed at the named landmark, the full-figure sheet returned her whole kit unauthored, and the face came back pensive where the block asked for surprise — a face Kael could not read, which is the page. `who-lives-in-it` ran twice: run 1 put Valeria's eyes on the viewer; one clause changed — *her face flat* out, the direction restated inside her paragraph — and run 2 put them on the bench and was **adopted** (*perfect*). *Flat* joins *level* in §Gaze as a temperament word the tool reads as a direction. **Ch. 17 has its two; the target is Ch. 18.** Three plates adopted on the new register in one session, four runs.

**NOT DONE, by choice:** the plate sheets (61K words, 1.7× prose to prompt, rising to 2.8× on recent chapters) — spot-read only; a later pass if wanted. No adopted plate re-run. s46 (the vault sweep, commit `cb8aa27`) has no entry in this log.

## SESSION 48 — 2026-08-26 · THE RUN (Ch. 34), AND WHAT THE MOTHER IS
**Target:** 05 §NEXT TARGET — the descent, the separation and the boat. **Delivered:** Ch. 34 "The Run", 6,982 words, ending with all eight aboard the hull. The separation did not happen and moves to Book One's last chapter.

**Open.** `verify.sh` clean. Cold read flagged four, three ruled: **a welded paragraph in committed Ch. 33** — the narration of Severin looking east ran into *"Yes," said Severin* with no blank line, collapsing the beat before he agrees to drop the mountain (**fixed**); **colour/color spelled both ways inside Ch. 33's own red-eye rhyme** — line 193 against line 475, the mother's eyes and the son's (**unified to *colour***); **`the mending Art` still unruled** from s43 (left unruled); and **no session record for s46** (**written, above**).

**Design.** Built from the scene, per s46: eight people on a broken mountain, one of them being carried and facing backwards, and the engine was *Kael works out that he is the load.* Two rounds of author redirection reshaped it substantially. **Round 1:** the Temple children stay on Aeliana and Kael stays on his mother; desperation and sadness clear on everyone; tears on both Kael and Neris at a run; four Resonants hold the ground and **Kael can do none of it**; he blames himself for his father; the pride collapses; they reach the boat. **Round 2, on the collision I raised:** `standing-rules` said Neris's unsheathing must be *steel and skill and nothing else* — the author **overruled it and widened it**: this is the first air at mass scale in the manuscript, it **cuts**, she was mended by Severin and has recomposed, and her real ceiling is Book Two's.

**Author rulings.** **THE INSIGNIA IS DEAD** — struck from Book One, not reassigned to Neris (the word in a raider's mouth and the mother's face do the work with one instrument). **The Phantom is not asked on this walk** — *no time for talking about that right now*, which also killed the ground-level debrief I had designed and made the chapter better. **The knife is unremarked** and the sword carries every object beat. **Neris's air is full-scale carnage** — heads, limbs, blood into the trees and the ground, *truly earning the name* — with her face turned away for the whole of it so nobody sees her eyes, which the page stages and never remarks on. **Do not write Elarine as spent-out.** **The pursuit is what is left of the four hundred**, not a patrol. **The men scream** — silence does not match that violence.

**Where Claude was wrong, four times, all caught by the author.** Wrote Elarine lighting **one bush** when her sheet says *a treasury with discipline* — rebuilt as four hundred simultaneous fires, which is the discipline half at scale. Wrote the pursuit as **nine men** against four Resonants spending everything — absurd, and rebuilt to a hundred and sixty in the slot plus a bigger column round the head. Wrote the carnage **silent** for an eerie effect that fought the scene. Wrote *at the height of a man's chest* **three times in one draft**, which is not only a repeat but a **surveyed** measure that 06 already bans (*relative distances, never surveyed ones*) — all three cut. Also mis-referenced a pronoun (*"He's—"* where Kael was following his mother) and reflex-recycled Ch. 27's *the way you call across a field*, both raised by me or the author and fixed.

**Metrics.** and/1k **39.2–64.6 across ten sections** (avg ~52) rather than the model's flat 51–55 default. Em-dashes 2.5/1k, not-buts 0, triads 6, similes 2.0/1k. Narration 87% of body at 27.7 words/paragraph — the highest ratio of the recent run, flagged to the author. `check-echoes`: **0 hits**.

**Canon written.** `02/magic` gained **AIR AT MASS SCALE** (it cuts; the working is silent and the men are not), **NATURE'S OTHER FACE** (living wood — root, vine, thorn, standing timber up through rock; mending is the same element turned on flesh), and the Elarine/Neris combination as the book's thesis on one page. `02/factions`: the insignia struck. `07/standing-rules`: the *steel and skill* clause **retired**; the face-turned-away rule added; Ch. 34 takes no departure of any kind; **beat 16 discharged**. New VALVE: Severin's *"I know what she is."* Amended: 04 beats 16/19, 05, 07 timeline/chapter-records/planted-clues (+4 planted, +1 valve), 03 × 8 sheets.

**Flagged forward, unruled:** three of the author's own Ch. 34 edits read as slips — *Wren crying intensified*, *Aeliana blistered feet*, and *It was neither* answering a list of three; all left standing per 06's do-not-fix-an-author-line rule. Ch. 33 carries eight *the height of* constructions; four are the lamp refrain and two are load-bearing, and **no retro pass was run or requested.**

## SESSION 49 — 2026-08-26 · THE RANGE (Ch. 35), AND THE END OF BOOK ONE REDRAWN
**Target:** 05 §NEXT TARGET — *the parting*. **Delivered:** something else, on the author's ruling: Ch. 35 "The Range", 3,827 words, the escape off the eastern water. **THE SEPARATION MOVES TO BOOK TWO.**

**Open.** `verify.sh` clean. Cold read flagged three, all ruled. **(1) The princess's given name is on the page** — bare *Aeliana*, three times, in Ch. 34's narration and nowhere else in the manuscript, against 03's *full chain or "the princess" only* and Ch. 24's record listing it as reserved. Author: *its fine.* 03 now owns the register, `established-facts` points at it instead of restating it, and the clue ledger's two *device NOT spent* fences follow the page. **(2) An unflagged echo** — the mother's hands *perhaps eleven times in his life* (Ch. 25) came back as the father's *perhaps eleven times in Kael's life* (Ch. 33), with Severin's *eleven times since the autumn* (Ch. 32) between them. I offered to log the phrase as protected. Author: *yeah you repeat a lot of stuff. thats on you.* **No tooling was added**; it is a judgement standard and it is Claude's to hold. **(3) The two s48 lines closed** — *Wren's crying* takes its possessive, and the three-item list lost *rain*, because the same page says there has been none since the storm-weeks, so *It was neither* is now a word for two against two. Ch. 34 baseline 6,982 → 6,981.

**AUTHOR RULINGS — the shape of the book's end.** **THE SEPARATION IS BOOK TWO'S.** Nobody comes off that hull on any page of Book One; *Neris and Wren go with Severin and Kael does not, and there is no farewell* is deferred whole and unspent. **This chapter is JUST the escape** — a first draft carrying the aftermath was cut back by about 2,600 words on the author's instruction, and the drift is its own chapter: *them finally away, a breath, drifting in the ocean.* **The mast is ruined and they drift**; the hull runs about a month and crashes on the **south coast of the continent**. **The Phantom is not asked here.** Consequences written into 01 (the pitch closed on three children in a boat), 04 (beats 19–20), 07 (`standing-rules`, `established-facts`, `open-threads`) and six 03 sheets. **00 duplicates beats 19–20 and needs the author's hand.**

**Design.** Built from the scene: eight people who cannot fight, on water, with the enemy twenty minutes behind them. The fight is therefore **arithmetic and silence, not steel**, and it uses **no new magic** — Gallos's mass, Fenna's dividing line, and Neris's Ch. 34 instrument turned to defence. Two of the four cannot reach a boat (no ground for one, ten feet for the other) and the page renders that as two men standing still and explains nothing. **The staging solved the face rule without contrivance:** she works from the bow, he sits behind her with the child, and he never sees her face.

**Two author redirections on the draft.** **(1)** *the generals just blasting the ship with different shapes of fire… the count goes from one, to two, to like 10, 11, 20, 30 all so fast* — the measured duel-cadence was rewritten as saturation, and Kael's count was broken on it: he loses thirteen entirely, arrives at twenty with nothing between fifteen and twenty, and stops speaking because a number is only worth anything if it arrives before the thing does. **(2)** *instead of just "counting", his count should be accompanied by directions, speed, trajectory* — the calls became fire-solutions (a side, a height, a speed, and what the thing will do), and what saturation takes off him is those terms, one at a time. **A fence fell out of it:** he has never been on a boat, so the narration inside his head has no *port*, *starboard*, *beam* or *astern*; the boat words stay in Severin's mouth. **Author's own edit *MORE INCOMING* was replaced** by the new call vocabulary and flagged.

**Also ruled: *shelf* is out of Ch. 35.** The author called it confusing and he was right twice over — Ch. 34 uses the word for two different pieces of ground within one chapter, and Ch. 35 inherited both; and the chapter carried a real error, putting the runnel mouth on the third shelf, which is the one the sea is at. The land is now grounded once — three great steps down to the sea — and after that it is the shore, the rocks, halfway down, the water's edge. **Ch. 34 was not touched**; a landed chapter gets no retro pass without asking.

**The author's own pass** cut every explanatory tail hung on a landed beat — the restatement of the count, the knife's history, the reason the small old man spends nothing — and moved the ending onto the child stopping. **Carried out unruled (s49):** *gripped her sword with all shed had* reads as a typo; **Elarine has no sword on any page since the mountain** and the rope-cutting instrument was Kael's knife in the cut paragraph; and the close's *Almost as this day old baby knew he was finally, safe.* reads as missing *if*, carries a comma before *safe*, wants *day-old*, and lands *silence* three times in four lines.

**Metrics.** Ch. 35: 3,827 words; and/1k 55.9 whole, saturation section 61.9 — deliberate, reported rather than smoothed. Echo script: 0 hits. Departures: none of the three. **Cut and held for the drift chapter, in commit `8518949`:** the bare hull at dawn, the bailing, **the third whitening**, **the seed-bread's inversion**, the water count, **the archaeology as subtraction in Neris's own mouth**, and the island at last light with no lights on it.

**Parallel sessions.** A plate session and a Ch. 21/23 session committed to this repo throughout; every commit here was staged by explicit path.

### SESSION 50, continued — THE ITALIC RULED, AND CH. 34 TAKES A SLIP
**The convention question came back as a proposal (author: *maybe change to first person then*), and the check that followed found the fence had already rotted.** `07/standing-rules.md` had declined a Ch. 34 slip on one stated ground — *a retrospective voice on that descent would name that it is the last time he sees her* — and **s49 moved the separation to Book Two, so it is not.** The refusal is therefore **superseded by the author's own later ruling, not re-argued.**

**The chapter's italics were sorted before anything was changed.** Legitimate and untouched: Vask's *You will grow into it in a year.*, the raiders' downhill talk, Neris's *I have got you. I love you.*, and three word-level emphases. That left two, and they were **not the same problem**:
- ***You are nothing special.*** **STAYS, italic and in the second person.** It follows *It had now come over and told him* — the sea is speaking, which puts it in the quoted-utterance family with Vask's line, not in narration. Converting it would have made the man agree with the verdict instead of the world delivering it, and collapsed the personification built in the two sentences before it. **Never convert it.**
- ***He had wanted to know what was on the other side of that sea since he was four years old.*** — Claude's own line, plain close-third narration wearing the slip's clothes. **De-italicised.**

**AUTHOR RULING: the close becomes the slip.** *Finally, his dream had come true.* → ***I got what I wanted.*** Four words. In close third the line had to tell the reader how to feel about the night; in the older voice it states the account flat and refuses to comment on it, in the arithmetic register, which is his mother's. It **pays** the de-italicised line thirty lines above rather than repeating it — the wanting is stated there, the getting lands here, and neither explains the other.

**THE FENCE THAT REPLACES THE OLD ONE:** the slip **does not touch the mother.** An older voice going elegiac about her hand on his head would pre-arrange a separation this book may not arrange (s49), so it stays on the sea and on the wanting. Feeling and judgment only; zero future facts; no landfall, course or duration; telling-frame untouched.

**Ledger consequences.** **Slips inside the fire: THREE — Ch. 28, Ch. 33, Ch. 34.** The triptych is untouched and still complete at Ch. 33; this is a further panel under the s37 amendment and never a replacement for the third. A second standing rule was minted — **the italic belongs to the slip and the quoted utterance and to nothing else** — ruled off this chapter, with **no retro pass and 06 untouched.** Ch. 34's record had also been carrying a close that no longer existed on the page (*He had wanted it since he was four years old.*, replaced by the author at s48 and never updated); corrected. `03/kael.md` had been carrying *slips 1–2 spent, slip 3 reserved for the fire* two chapters after the fire spent two of them; it now points at the one file that owns the count.

**Baseline Ch. 34 = 6,979** (−1 on the close). **Verification:** `check-quotes.sh` 1,144 fragments / 0 not found; `verify.sh` baselines match, phantoms 0; every bullet following an edited one confirmed intact; reader notebook re-synced.

**AND THE SESSION OPENED ON A THIRD CORRECTION** (*care with these bloated prompts. new system has been great with less*). Both blocks had been written carrying nearly every instrument at once. 767 → 516 and 775 → 566 words, into the band `ch16` (524), `ch20/val` (491) and `ch24/the-smile` (480) occupy. Cut: a demotion where there was no rival, a hands inventory where one hand was the beat, a clause restating a pose Composition had already set, an ownership clause for three people in identical wool, a colour ban that violated the s42/s45 ruling outright, and a four-negation stack aimed at one thing.

**TWO FINDINGS ON THE RUNS, and the second is an amendment to §Gaze.**
- **AN EXPRESSION OVERRIDE LARGE ENOUGH TO WIN TAKES THE IDENTITY WITH IT.** `four-seconds` run 1: the laugh landed exactly as written — and came back with brown curls, a round face, no jaw and the eyes creased shut, on a boy who read nine. The two adults were unmistakably their references; he was the only one carrying an extreme expression. **§Scenes and plates knows that the reference's mood beats the prompt in a close crop; this is the other direction.** And the age fault was the same fault: §AGE's eye line is the only age instrument that works alone, and a creased-shut eye has no eye line. Run 2 gave the laugh its eyes back, put the adolescent rung in as anatomy with no number in it (no refusal risk against the binding), and **authored the hair, because the reference had not carried it** — a reference that loses a feature once is authored in every block after, which is why `both-hands` carries the hair clause too.
- **A WORKING PART MUST BE A STATE THE PICTURE CAN HOLD, NOT AN ACTION THE FACE PERFORMS.** `both-hands` run 1 (*their expressions are kind of a bit dead tho*). Both paragraphs obeyed §Gaze in letter — each named a working part — but hers was a movement (*the eyes go over his face from one part of it to another*), which a still picture cannot draw, and his was four absences of motion. Strip the unrenderable clause and she had a closed mouth, unmoved brows and four negations. **Vacancy returned by a different door than s48's.** Run 2: lids raised, eyes fixed, brows drawn a fraction, jaw set, lips just pressed — and for him, caught rather than composed. Both landed on one run.
- **And run 1 dropped a hand.** Two hands in a profile is awkward enough that the tool resolved it by dropping one; siting them against the viewer rather than against each other fixed it.

**Below the threshold, recorded not corrected:** Vask's *small sound in his nose* did not render at that scale — the smallest facial instruction the folder has attempted, the same limit that lost the tow on `ch21/the-nine-things`. **And the crop came back loose for the fifth and sixth recorded times**, after `ch17` twice and `ch20` twice; never once has the correction been wanted, which is what still holds it below §Findings' bar.

**Housekeeping.** Both blocks are `PLATE-VERBATIM` and the built prompts were diffed byte-identical across the flip. `README.md`'s programme ruling had been carrying *Ch. 1–17 have theirs*, eight chapters stale; the stamp is dropped and `images/plates/` answers it, under ONE FACT, ONE HOME. **This session's rulings were first stamped s49 and corrected to s51** — the log was at Session 50 and s49 is already THE RANGE.

**Still open:** Ch. 26 onward have no candidates · Oss and Halden have no references, and two Ch. 13 candidates wait on that · `kael-9-still` unrun · `seeing-about-the-goat` unrun since s40 · the laughter re-run on `the-spots-of-colour` · Ch. 6's door, Ch. 7's Temple from the road and Ch. 8's roll still passed over for being wide · `ch08.md` and `ch09.md` both cite a §Name both temperatures that no longer exists · finish is still not consistent across the set and the author has not settled it.

**THREE FINDINGS, ALL PAID FOR BY A FAILED RUN.**
- **DAMAGE MUST NOT BE PAID FOR OUT OF THE FEATURES THAT SEPARATE A CHARACTER.** `ch26/not-kind-to-me` run 1 came back a beggar who looked like Valeria. Both faults had one root: the block wrote a Sedrane's *very good quality* cloth as *torn through* and let her severe binding come loose down one side — surrendering the quality that is her station and the **one axis README's §Silhouette separation uses to hold her apart from Valeria.** `ch23/empty` had already returned *much mended* as rags one chapter over. Run 2 named what survives first, then the damage as an exception, and banned the archetype rather than the tears; it fixed both in one pass. Now in §Injury.
- **AN EXPRESSION OVERRIDE LARGE ENOUGH TO WIN TAKES THE IDENTITY WITH IT** (`ch25/four-seconds`, recorded in the earlier entry) — and its corollary ran four times without a miss this session: **a reference that loses a feature once is authored in every block after.** Kael's hair twice, Elarine's binding twice, Valeria's bound hair once.
- **§HANDS' ONE-LINE-PER-ITEM RULE GENERALISES OFF HANDS ONTO ANYTHING COUNTED.** `ch27/the-sky` carried four burning objects at four depths on one run, each given its own line and its own place, with the count stated after. *Three blades total* failed twice; this is the same instrument at four times the load.

Nothing in either picture depended on it. ## SESSION 50, continued — CH. 36 "THE QUIET", AND BOOK ONE IS FINISHED
**Design.** The first design was an INVENTORY — five counts, each coming out short, with the water, the bundles, the bread, the people and her hair as the spine. **The author redirected it and was right:** *mourning the fallen, family, honour, survival · the quiet sea · Kael should be beside everyone a bit.* The clever cold structure became a warm one: **Kael goes round the boat**, and the counts survive as texture inside the people-scenes instead of as architecture. Recorded because it is the same failure the memory file names — building a mechanism where the brief asked for people.

**Two author expansions during design, both load-bearing.** **(1)** Elarine and Aurelian are **bereaved, not orphaned of a house** — *their families were not all at the island, in fact most were not* — which inverted what Claude had drafted: Aurelian carries his father's message to a brother alive at court, and Elarine's house survives and *will go on with her*, so what died is the only person who ever told her she was good enough. **New world fact logged in `02-World-Bible/temple.md`.** And Kael does **not** hold the gate against them: the Ch. 29 verdict was never about fault — it is a measurement, not a charge — so nothing needed correcting and nothing was. **(2) The three wants** close the book: mother and brother alive, to know what his parents were, and to kill the man who killed his father. **04's purpose thread pays on the last page** and the narration never says so. **The vow fence held:** a want is not a promise — nothing is sworn, there is nobody to swear it to, and the man is still unnamed on any page.

**THE ACCUSATION (author, mid-draft).** *Should Kael blame the princess?* Yes — and the first attempt drew the fence in the wrong place, keeping his father out of the count. **The author corrected it: the invasion followed them home, and the father is a result of it.** The fence is not the yard; **the fence is his own hands** — he may blame HER for the whole chain, and may never notice that his reconnaissance put that house in the party's road or that his arithmetic chose the ground. Recorded in `00 §True causality` and `07/standing-rules.md`. **He is also wrong at the level that matters, and Book One ends with him wrong.**

**VALERIA STOPS THE HAND AND NOT THE MOUTH.** She does not move while he says it; she is in front of him before the arm is anywhere; the author added her *"Kael, stop it. Please,"* and **he pushes**, and it is his MOTHER's voice that ends it. **This is not the crown-vs-Kael choice** — she stops the body so as never to have to make it. *Please* spent a second time and still rationed.

**Corrections taken during the pass, all Claude's own failures.** The condolence exchange was written as an epigram (*"We know." / "We were there."*) — **cold, and out of character for the boy who puts his body between people and the thing**; rewritten so both of them comfort him and Aurelian's answer is that he has not got one. The boat was called *her* in narration and in Severin's mouth; the author ruled it out entirely and it is **"it" or "us"** throughout, matching Ch. 34–35. Four of Claude's own explanatory tails were cut before the draft was handed over, plus the split-stone simile (Ch. 32 owns that image) and two canon collisions (seven houses' colours per Ch. 31; *the northern arch*).

**THE STORES.** The author supplied the reason there is not enough — **the hull was provisioned for FOUR: Kael, Vask, Neris and the child** — and asked whether Severin should say it. **He does not.** `03/severin.md` fences the hull absolutely (nobody on any page knows it is the family's boat but the reader; nothing joins the two) and he is still inside his own Ch. 31 deferral. What he says is *"It was not stocked for eight."* — true, and it answers nothing — **and nobody asks him what it had been stocked for.** The four is never on the page and Kael does not derive it. **Banked for Book Two: Kael counting four portions and understanding that the fourth is his father's** — the best version of the material, and it costs the seal to spend now.

**THE SEED-BREAD IS CUT** by the author, in his own pass, with no ruling attached. **Recorded as CARRIED, not struck** — Claude's assumption, and the reversible one: unlike the insignia at s48 this beat's owner is alive and aboard. 04 amended; 05 §OPEN ITEMS carries the ruling.

**A workflow note worth keeping.** The author edited the manuscript live while Claude worked, and one Claude commit swept an entire author pass in under its own message. Nothing was lost — every edit read the file fresh — but the attribution was wrong for one commit. **From here: check the tree before each edit and commit the author's changes under their own message first.** His insertion also left a contradiction two lines above it (*She did not say anything* followed by her speaking, with *either* dangling); fixed.

**CH. 36 TAKES NO DEPARTURE OF ANY KIND** — argued and declined for the book's last page: the closing reflection is the thirteen-year-old's, his conclusion about the enemy is wrong, and the older voice arriving there would either correct him or be seen withholding. **Slips inside the fire stay at three (Ch. 28, Ch. 33, Ch. 34); the triptych stays complete at Ch. 33.** It is also **the first chapter since Ch. 21 with no Art on the page at all.**

**Metrics:** 6,543 words. 0 protected-phrase hits. Tics on the pre-pass draft: 7 dashes, 0 not-buts, similes 2.7/1k, and/1k **53.2 overall but NOT uniform** (45.4–74.0 across sections) — the 74 is the debrief, where the prose accelerates by design, and the non-uniformity is the part 06 actually tests.

**Close.** Baselines: **Ch. 36 = 6,543**, and 00 = 19,788 after this session's fence note. `05` STATUS back to two blocks (s48 rolled to the pointer); **NEXT TARGET is now a decision, not a chapter, and is the author's** — Claude did not improvise one. Records written to `07/chapter-records.md`, `07/timeline.md` (Ch. 36 row, DAY 4), `07/standing-rules.md` (six new rules), `07/open-threads.md`, `07/established-facts.md`; 04's Part IV marked COMPLETE and beats 19/20 closed; eight `03` sheets refreshed, Glance lines included, every following header confirmed intact. **A total word count was typed into 05 and then removed** — the baselines own the counts, the same rule applied to the stale STATUS figures this morning. **Not done, and flagged:** `07/chapter-records.md` rolls per Part and Part IV has closed — that is a ZERO-LOSS restructure and wants its own session, and rolling now would leave the accumulator empty until Book Two's first chapter lands.

**RULING (author, s50), after the close: THE SEED-BREAD'S INVERSION IS STRUCK.** Not carried, not reserved — **struck from Book One outright, like the insignia at s48.** It was drafted twice and cut twice, at Ch. 32 and again out of Ch. 36. **The plant itself stands as page canon** (Ch. 4 / 7 / 8 — the feast bread that rises for the master and no one else) **and nothing whatever is owed off it.** The object is free for a later book; do not re-owe it. Amended in three places: `04` beat 19, `07/planted-clues.md` (whose row was still reading *the inversion is still available and still owed*), and `05` §OPEN ITEMS, which loses the item. `06` §Staging is untouched and needs nothing — its mention of the seed-bread is historical, naming it as one of the Vask beats that pointed at the throw, and the throw is spent.

### SESSION 52, continued — BOOK TWO'S NUMBERING RULED, AND THE SCRIPTS MADE BOOK-AWARE
**Ruling (author):** *book 2 proceeds from the last ch in number* — **Book Two opens at Ch. 37.** Directory: `manuscript/book-two/`, Claude's default as the obvious sibling of `book-one`, open to override. Recorded in CLAUDE.md, TOOLS.md and 05; the 05 item is closed.

**The scripts.** Because the number is now unique across books, every script that hard-coded `manuscript/book-one/` globs `manuscript/*/` instead — `verify.sh` (a `Ch. N` baseline resolves to `manuscript/*/NN-*.md`, and the existing one-file assertion now refuses a duplicate number loudly), `check-quotes.sh` (the phantom audit reads every page of every book), `check-echoes.sh` (the whole-manuscript echo scan spans books), `sync-reader-notebook.sh` (change detection over `manuscript/`; the notebook stays *Book One — Reader Copy* by default, since a reader of Book Two has read Book One — a separate notebook is the author's call when Ch. 37 lands). `build-manuscript.sh` takes `[book] [epub]`, default `book-one`, and builds `build/<Book-Name>.md`; it refuses a book directory that does not exist. No per-book config, no new counters: the trade-off is that a future book restarting at 01 would make `verify.sh` fail, which is the right failure.

**Parity, run against captured before-outputs:** `check-echoes.sh` whole-manuscript output identical (84 lines); `build/Book-One.md` byte-identical (156,553 words); `check-quotes.sh` 1,145 fragments / 0 phantoms, unchanged; `verify.sh` BASELINES all match through the new glob; `sync-reader-notebook.sh` reports nothing to sync (no prose touched); `check-echoes.sh <file>` unchanged. `manuscript/book-two/` is not created — git keeps no empty directories; it appears with Ch. 37.

## SESSION 53 (2026-08-27) — PLATES: CH. 28 TO CH. 36 (design only; nothing run)

**Book One's last nine chapters have their two each — eighteen blocks, composed and built, none run.** Ch. 28 was designed first and the author was offered eight candidates and handed the pick back (*you choose*); the rest were chosen the same way, in chapter order, from the scene. `plates/candidates.md` gains a section per chapter with everything that was considered and why it was passed over, so the shortlist survives the choosing.

**THE SET AS IT NOW STANDS, Ch. 28 → Ch. 36:** `the-hand-back` / `rearranging-the-road` · `the-eighteen-inches` / `the-shawl` · `the-dock-leaves` / `the-white-in-all-of-him` · `the-hand-flat` / `the-shutter` · `the-white-in-her-hair` / `the-lights-on-the-mountain` · `the-sword` / `the-lamp` · `the-elbow` / `the-bowl` · `the-white-room` / `the-spotter` · `the-finger` / `no-lights`.

**THE ONE INSTRUMENT THE WHOLE RUN IS BUILT ON IS Ch. 27's:** *you do not draw the stroke, you draw the men.* Part IV is the worst material in the book — a first kill, a village, a wall that shuts, a father killed in his own yard — and **every block is composed on the second before or the second after.** `ch28/the-hand-back` is a boy's open hand a second after; `ch33/the-sword` is four seconds before; `ch29/the-eighteen-inches` is a door still open by the width of one person. s51's dropped grim plates are the folder's own evidence for which half of a chapter like this to take.

**THE MISSING PORTRAITS BLOCKED FIVE GOOD CANDIDATES AND SHAPED TWO BLOCKS.** `ch13.md`'s rule reaches all of Part IV: *a plate is not where a person's face gets decided; whatever it drew would become his reference by default.* No portrait exists for **Halden**, **Oss**, **Lucan Drusane**, **the small neat man who kills Vask**, or **the big man on the threshing floor**. So the judge in `ch29/the-eighteen-inches` is a broad shape filling a lit slot with no features on him — **which the block uses rather than works around**, because that is what a boy outside a gate can see of the man deciding about him — and `ch31/the-shutter` resolves no face anywhere at all. **The work that unblocks the rest is portraits, not plates.**

**FOUR STANDING RULINGS WERE SPENT OR RE-APPLIED, ALL NAMED ON THEIR SHEETS.**
- **The pregnancy ruling did its job twice and cost nothing.** `ch32/the-white-in-her-hair` crops above the collarbone with her arms coming down into frame, so her hands are in it and her body is not; `ch33/the-lamp` keeps the mother, the child and the whole of the birth out of the frame and draws the girl holding the light instead.
- **Aeliana's hands are occupied for the first time in the folder**, on two plates. `ch26.md` had passed over a candidate on exactly that ground and called it *an argument worth having later*. The page is what buys it: she carries a lamp through a birth and a newborn through a fight, and neither block admires her, which is what keeps a portrait *plate* of her illegal and these two legal.
- **The drain-mark is drawn for the first time.** One piece of hair, white from the root, hard cold white with no warmth in it, every other hair on her head dark — and in `ch34/the-elbow` the two whites stand in one frame and are forbidden to each other by name.
- **The reserve/erasure lesson from `ch21/the-surge` governs `ch30/the-white-in-all-of-him`:** the white is given a form, stays on what it runs along, and nothing arcs off — which is also the fence Vask speaks on himself in Ch. 21 and the page repeats in Ch. 30.

**AND THE SET NOW CLOSES A RHYME IT OPENED AT `ch01`.** `ch01/fallen-stars` (the lights below), `ch32/the-lights-on-the-mountain` (the lights coming up), `ch36/no-lights` (nothing lit anywhere on it). Three pictures, one view, Book One between them. `ch36/no-lights` is written to be the last plate of the book and spends its single absence on the one thing the frame pulls toward.

**Housekeeping.** Every canon quote on the nine sheets was checked mechanically against its chapter and five were corrected — a merged two-sentence quote, a dropped *that*, a lowercase sentence opening, two lines cited to the wrong chapter — and one paragraph that had italicised the *block's* wording as though it were the page's was rewritten unquoted. `prompts/README.md` §Provenance now records all sixteen new files as built-and-never-run. **This session was numbered s52 while it worked and renumbered to s53 at the close**, because a parallel session on the same repo had already taken 52; the sheets carry s53 throughout.
