### SESSION 54, continued — FACTS, NOT PROHIBITIONS: RULED, AND THE RETRO PASS
**The exchange.** Asked whether the planted clues and standing rules hinder Claude's creativity, Claude answered that the fences (what shape a scene must fit) do not, the debts do a little, and the mass does — fifteen instructions per `clues.sh` query put the drafting into a defensive crouch, and a *sealed, 00* payoff is a tease that pulls design toward the destination. The author: *it seems weird but I kind of dislike having a direction, for you. I know what I kind have in my head, than I see what you have in yours, and then we combine — just don't like rules like: never panics, never x, never do y.* Claude owned that most `never` lines were its own, written at closes by turning a page fact (*his count failed three ways; none was panic*) into a prohibition (*never write him as panicking*), while the author's rulings are positive statements. **Author: *ruling — and do a retro pass.***

**The ruling (CLAUDE.md §Standing rules — FACTS, NOT PROHIBITIONS, author, s54).** The state files (02, 03, 07, 04) record what the page HAS and what is open — never what a future page may not do. A close writes state, not a rule aimed at Claude; the author's own rulings carry their *(author)* tag and are the only prohibitions; sealed truths stay in 00. At design time Claude brings its own version of the scene before reading anything on it, then hears the author's, and the two are combined. **06 is the rule side and is untouched** — its rules (*never explain*, *love never stated*, the never-remark list) are the author's; whether it joins is the author's call (05 §OPEN ITEMS).

**The pass — scope and method.** Every state file archived verbatim first (`archive/state-files-before-facts-ruling.md`: 33 files, 942 lines, checked line-by-line against HEAD, 0 missing). Then, file by file with exact-match assertions: imperatives aimed at a future page (*never write / do not let / must not / may not / no later page may / never resolve / do not have her*) converted to what the page has (*none of the three was panic* · *nobody has corrected him* · *she has not volunteered it* · *Kael has not seen them* · *open by design*); page facts already in the past tense (*never pinned*, *the semifinal never fought*, *he never once said what he was doing*, *never drew his father's knife*) left as they are; world-mechanics uses of *fence* (*the bloodline fence is intact — motion still from muscle*) left as they are; the author's rulings kept with their tags (*the Phantom is not joined to the Ghost*, the valves, *no vow was sworn (author, s50)*, the AUTHOR-DECIDED LINES). The word *fence* in the instruction sense retired from CLAUDE.md, TOOLS.md, 07/index, the ledger's preamble and headers and `clues.sh`'s own comment (*ask it what the target owes* → *what the page already holds*). **Counts:** marker words 590 → 287; imperative forms 216 → 7 across 33 files (magic.md 9→0 · elarine.md 7→0 · kael.md 13→2 · neris.md 10→0 · severin.md 14→0 · standing-rules.md 16→1 · planted-clues.md 110→2). ### SESSION 53, continued — THE EIGHTEEN RUNS: BOOK ONE HAS ITS PLATES

**Eighteen blocks, eighteen adopted pictures, EVERY ONE ON A FIRST RUN.** No re-run, no refusal, nothing held. **Book One's plate programme is complete: thirty-six chapters, seventy-seven plates, every chapter with at least two.** `images/plates/` answers it and no stamp is kept anywhere.

**SEVEN OF THE EIGHTEEN RAN ON THE AUTHOR'S OWN OPTIMISED BLOCKS**, each stored verbatim on its sheet in place of mine: `ch28/the-hand-back` · `ch32/the-white-in-her-hair` · `ch33/the-lamp` · `ch34/the-bowl` · `ch35/the-spotter` · `ch35/the-white-room` · `ch36/no-lights`. **What they changed is the session's real yield**, and it is all recorded on the sheets.

**THE AUTHOR'S SECOND PROMPT-BLOAT CORRECTION, AND THE PASS I HAD SKIPPED.** *Do we not have rules for prompts to not have this much redundant stuff?* — and we do: §Before the run, *each fact once, count the channel, not the sentence*, plus s51's *care with these bloated prompts*. Eighteen blocks had been written without that pass. All seventeen unrun ones were audited by channel, 585→504 on the block in hand and the set brought into the 435–642 band. **The fault has now happened twice with the same cause and the correction was wanted both times, so it is promoted:** §Before the run names the seven channels that keep bloating (one-figure, the gaze's second ban, ban stacks, build under a full figure, the light stated twice, meta-preambles, and stillness).

**FOUR RULES CAME OUT OF THE RUNS.**
- **WRITE THE STATE, NOT THE ACTION** — promoted to §Before the run off two author corrections in one session. *The arm is not quite steady* cannot be drawn and **the lamp tilted slightly off level and the flame leaning** can; *three olive trees splitting apart* cannot and **already split open, the trunks standing as ragged upright shells, the strips out in the air clear of them, mid-flight** can. It is s51's face rule generalised to objects and events.
- **THE GAZE HOLDS WHEN THE HEAD CAN TURN TOWARD THE TARGET.** Five data points in one day: it failed with the head toward the camera (`ch28/the-hand-back`), held with the head turned away (`ch29`), held with the head toward the camera and a target inside the frame (`ch30`), held on a target outside the frame (`ch35/the-spotter`), and failed twice on targets within a hand's width of the face (`ch32`, `ch33/the-lamp`). **A ban alone does not hold it; somewhere to go does.**
- **AN AGE RUNG IN THE BLOCK FIXES THE AGE.** Every plate run without one came back between fifteen and seventeen; the author put §AGE's adolescent rung into `ch35/the-spotter` and it came back fourteen, and a one-clause version carried `ch36/no-lights`. `README.md`'s ruling is amended: the adopted pictures stand as drawn and none is re-cut, and a block still to be run may carry it.
- **A FACE CAN BE FULLY DRAWN AND STILL BE DEMOTED.** `ch28/the-hand-back` is now §The winner and the rival's specimen: the winner given a ratio to a body part, and the rival — his own face — held down by name on both channels.

**AND FOUR FINDINGS THAT STAY ON THE SHEETS.**
- **Faceless figures are solved, four ways, four first runs:** a broad shape in a backlit aperture (`ch29`), silhouettes against bare-paper sky (`ch30`), a head simply turned away in ordinary shade (`ch31/the-hand-flat`), and a whole room where no eye is drawn anywhere (`ch31/the-shutter`, `ch35/the-white-room`). **`ch13.md`'s missing-portrait block is now navigable** — an unreferenced character can be in a plate as long as no face is decided.
- **Small facial marks do not render — three asked for, three missed**, at three different scales, which kills this session's own earlier claim that the variable is size in frame. What the three share is that each was a state of skin on a face already doing something; the shoulder slash that did render had cloth to tear. **Give a facial mark something torn or dressed to sit against.**
- **The count instrument generalises to people and its number has a floor.** *Eight people in the boat and no ninth* landed exactly; twenty-six pinhead lights on a hillside landed as two strings at two depths and not as a number. **The arrangement survives at any scale; the count survives only while the items read as items.**
- **An idle hand gets recruited.** *His left hand hangs at his side* and *her left hand is open and empty at her side* both came back with the hand in the action (`ch31/the-shutter`, `ch34/the-elbow`). Not promoted: no correction has been wanted.

**RULED BY THE AUTHOR:** the s53 pictures **stand as drawn** (*its fine*) — the loose crop, now eleven instances and never once corrected, the age readings, the missed facial marks and the ragged-cloth drift are all recorded and none is a correction owed (`README.md` §Pictures that stand as drawn). **And the Aeliana plates are the author's favourites in the set** (*aeliana pics are the best*), recorded as a taste note and explicitly not a programme — the fence is untouched and both pictures that earned the remark are of her doing a job with her hands.

**HOUSEKEEPING.** A rewrap pass of mine had joined the two `Image` lines into one paragraph in seven blocks; found on the first byte-diff after a flip to `PLATE-VERBATIM` and repaired everywhere. Every adopted block is now `PLATE-VERBATIM` with its style line inside the fence, and each flip was diffed against the built file. The session was renumbered s52→s53 at the close of the design half, because a parallel session had taken 52; near the end that session rebased the shared history under this one and the two were reconciled with nothing lost.

**Still open:** **`ch30/the-white-in-all-of-him` carries an unruled departure** — the lightning arcs off him, which Ch. 30 forbids (*not sparking, not spilling*) and Ch. 33 describes; it is a true picture of the wrong minute, and it stands or gets one re-run of its effect paragraph on the author's word · Oss, Halden, Lucan and the two coalition Resonants still have no portraits, and the strongest unbuilt Part IV candidates wait on them · **the folder still has no plate of Severin**, and `ch36`'s *the boat words* is the best available one · `ch26/the-good-part` and `seeing-about-the-goat` unrun · the laughter re-run on `the-spots-of-colour` · `ch08.md` and `ch09.md` cite a §Name both temperatures that no longer exists · finish is still not consistent across the set and the author has not settled it.

## SESSION 59 (2026-08-28) — CH. 40 "THE HANDS": TWO MONTHS EAST, AND THE PRICE OF ONE HAND

**Open.** `verify.sh` clean (GIT clean, baselines match, 0 phantoms). Cold read: CLAUDE.md, 05, 04, 06, 01, all ten 02 files, all seventeen 03 sheets, the 07 law core, the Book Two chapter records, and Ch. 38 and Ch. 39 in full. **One itch, and the author turned it into a ruling:** `02/lexicon.md` and the Ch. 39 record called *imperial* "Valeria's word for the Koine", where the page has it in **Kael's** mouth, said to her (*one gives orders in imperial, the Koine*). The author's ruling: there is one name — the language IS the imperial language and **the Vessantine Koine is its official name**; *imperial* is the plain way of saying the same thing, not a second name. The clause moved to `lexicon.md` §THE LANGUAGES, which owns them; the record's attribution followed (56a740d).

**Design — the two versions, combined.** Claude's first, before anything was read on it: the two months AS the chapter — the count degrading, Valeria drilling the box, the princess's speech rations, and Aurelian's Ch. 36 *east is a month of nothing and then it is theirs* landing as the cold turn. Then `clues.sh` on what it touched, which produced the spine: Ch. 21's *"Get loose," Vask said. Kael could not get loose. There was nothing to get loose with. Every escape he had was built out of a hand* — the school built against exactly this situation, and the iron built against the school. **The author's version replaced the subject:** *a whole chapter travelling, not my idea* — the two months are the RUNWAY (I: time passes, the count never lost, his concentration frightening the princess; II: two months, and the thing he was waiting for happens — a camp), and **the chapter is the escape**: III the warning, IV the hand, broken and crushed and skinned, *don't be safe … it should shock.* Claude's contributions accepted into it: the mechanics (the thumb is not enough; the width across the hand; pressure not a blow); *neurogenic shock* struck as out-of-world (`02/curriculum.md` register rule) and rendered as a body with no name for it; the door bars from outside, so the hand is done hours early and held hidden; the Art stays gone with one band on; Valeria tries once and stops; the princess is told to look away and does not; the chapter ends on the door coming open. **Two author amendments before drafting:** **(1)** the governor STAYS with the box the whole two months — he got away from Neris and Severin and Qeshet is in chaos, and none of that is on the page; what is on the page is a man who does not turn back and looks down the road behind him. **(2)** the princess is *amazed and scared*, has lost her will and wants to die, and is going a little mad — rendered wholly from outside, through the worst instrument in the book for it. Out of (2) came the section's architecture: **three children, three answers to captivity — a count, a Line, and nothing.** **And one ruling that changed the design:** the what/why split was Book One's always, *but he should be getting better at it now* — spent as **he gets better where he has data and stays blind where he has a verdict**: the eight men read correctly, Kesir's verdict revised on the eighteenth day off his own watching, and the two misses bounded (the governor needs a fact he does not have; the princess is blocked by *not worth it*, never taken back).

**Draft and passes.** Drafted at 60457aa (7,048 words) and committed at once; hot pass at e52da3e — **and/1k 53.0 → 39.0**, uniform across all nine sections as advertised, 57 chains broken, and one explainer cut (*a thing you have already decided sits in front of the instrument and does not move*). **The author's pass came in seven rounds, each read as a diff and committed as it came:** the count's unit (*forty-one seconds*, cdc8d99); Kael pricing all three, because Valeria and he are political assets too, ending on the author's own *Maybe if she had not, he would not yet be alive* (362e643, 479ee4c); the drill rewritten twice and then struck entirely — first into *"Valeria. You should sleep."* and the watch divided (4a1e94e), then all training out of the box, the measurements re-sourced to thumb-and-finger round the arm and **the band going further up his hand and stopping in exactly the same place** (a4386aa); the marks in the boards cut whole (555b278); the princess's palace-floor sentence replaced by the bargain said twice (6951e92); the three preparations made explicit in his mouth (6ff798d); Wren made *his brother* and given the milk arithmetic (6928468); and the climax rebuilt on three notes — literal breakage, the silence as the work, and **the ruined hand doing everything that follows** (1b98c00), with the author's own last turn on top: the first pull is not enough, and he pulls harder (c449203). Mechanical passes at 555b278 and the close (*keep track of all of it*, one soft line break, *instinctively out of*, *came off his hand*, *than a hand*, one trailing space). **Departures: none, argued** — no pan-away (outside the box is the mystery), and a slip at the hand argued and declined.

**Final metrics (Ch. 40 = 7,178):** and/1k 38.3 (§1 43.6 · §2 40.5 · §3 35.5 · §4 37.4 · §5 47.0 · §6 24.3 · §7 43.6 · §8 29.3) · dashes 0 · not-buts 1 · similes 2.5/1k · narration 6,640 words · 35.7 words/paragraph · echoes 0 against the ledger as it stood; its own five phrasings admitted at the close.

**Rulings (author, s59), in order:** the Vessantine Koine is the imperial language's official name and *imperial* is not a second name · **the narration may reach forward** (*He was wrong … and it would be a long time before anybody told him*) — allowed, not a slip, not a departure, not counted; the slips' content fence still binds it · Kael's what/why moves from Book Two · Valeria and Kael are political assets in their own right, and the Drusane name may be what is keeping him alive · the governor stays with the box · the princess has lost her will · **nothing in that box is training** · the marks in the boards are not needed · **Wren is *his brother*, not *the child*** — a warmth judgement, explicitly not a rule, and no retro pass on Ch. 37–39.

## SESSION 61 (2026-08-29) — BOOK TWO PLANNED, AND THE SOLVENNA RETRO PASS (no new chapter; 00 unopened)

**A note on the numbering.** There is no SESSION 60 entry in this log because s60 was a PLATE session (2026-08-28, `08-Plates/`), which left its rulings in `08-Plates/README.md` and did not log here. One of its rulings reached a live file — Valeria's burn on the LEFT side of her face, mostly healed, in `03/valeria.md`, correctly stamped *(author, s60)*. The gap is that session, not a missing record.

**Open.** `verify.sh` clean (GIT clean, baselines match, 0 phantoms). Cold read: CLAUDE.md, 05, 04, 06, 01, all ten 02 topic files, all seventeen 03 sheets, the 07 law core, and the Book Two chapter records. Two itches raised before working. **One was wrong:** the *(author, s60)* stamp on Valeria's burn was flagged as a mis-stamp and is correct — see above; the retraction is logged in 05 §OPEN ITEMS so it is not re-raised. **One was real:** a duplicated *thin without changing shape* in the same §Body line, an artifact of the s59 append; the earlier instance was dropped and the s59 one (*two months further into it*) kept.

**The question.** The author opened with the plan for Book Two: **two years of the trio — Kael, Valeria and Aeliana — ending when they reunite with the rest of the group.** Per CLAUDE.md, Claude brought its own version first: three dense movements with the gaps jumped (the clock named as the craft problem — Part IV ran four days across fifteen chapters and two years cannot be told at that rate); one engine per character (Kael's appetite finding a *because*, Valeria getting the royal body with none of the apparatus that gave it meaning, Aeliana becoming useful or dying); and the cost named — five of the cast off the page for a whole book, mitigated by departures and by an east that has to carry real people.

**THE AUTHOR'S SPINE, WHICH IS NOW THE PLAN.** After the kidnapping the three trust nothing that is not the empire proper, and a city-state is not that; their destination is **SOLVENNA**, the southernmost city of the empire's ORIGINAL borders, five months from Qeshet by carriage. They find they were taken to a border city between the empire and a coalition country; they learn at distance, never by going, that **Qeshet has been struck by the coalition and whatever was there is gone**, and reason that if the others live they are for Solvenna. The continent is at war and the roads are watched. **The main line of the book: they fall in with a crew of outlaws working west to Solvenna who know how to move through law, and pay their passage in work — jobs, heists, whatever the road asks — with three identities held for two years, from the crew and from everybody else.**

**THE RETRO PASS, AND WHAT IT DID NOT COST.** The author asked for one mention: that they must get through Solvenna before the capital. **Ch. 36 is untouched** — Aeliana's *we must go to the capital* was a princess ruling a destination on water with no map, Aurelian's *north and then west along the shoulder* is a schoolboy reciting a classroom map, and Ch. 38's callback to her line is unchanged. The whole edit is one paragraph in Ch. 38 §I, in the governor's mouth, because he is the only person in the scene who knows the road. Two mechanical departures from the approved wording, both reported to the author in-session: the household's gesture was moved up into the speech (the following line already used it, so the exit line was trimmed to *He went out by a door behind him*), and Kael's filing clause was reworded from *put the number away where he put numbers* to *Kael had the number before the door shut*, because *Kael priced them and put it away* stands 150 words later in the same chapter and 06 makes that a cut.

**Rulings (author).** SOLVENNA, chosen off four candidates in the imperial Latin-Byzantine register · the border city is **NOT Ammareth** — a new name, Claude's to choose: **ISHARETH**, eastern tongue, un-imperial per the conquered-places rule, unspent and living in 04 only · the five months go on the page · Kael's filing clause stays in · variant B, with *The empire begins there* · the cover story (chosen once or accreted) deferred — *that's more important when we get there*. **THE CAPITAL HAS NO NAME ON ANY PAGE** and stays *the capital* until a mouth needs otherwise.

**Files.** `manuscript/book-two/38-The-Watch.md` (2,867 → 2,906; committed at 60e32a0 before any bible moved) · `04-Outline.md` §BOOK TWO'S SHAPE — broad only, by destination, with the unminted list kept visible (the coalition country, the capital's name, the crew and its rules, the cover story) · `02/geography.md` §Solvenna · `02/factions.md` §THE ORIGINAL BORDERS AND THE CONQUESTS — the real new world fact, and it sharpens *the south is held through native rulers kept in place* rather than colliding with it · `02/naming.md` §Imperial place names (Latin-Byzantine without the -ANE house ending) · `07/established-facts.md` (SOLVENNA taken; the capital unnamed) · `07/standing-rules.md` §THE NIGHT IN THE GOVERNOR'S HOUSE · the Ch. 38 chapter record · 05 (baseline, status, next target, open items). **00 was not opened, and nothing in this session belongs in it** — the Book Two shape is forward plan, which is 04's, not sealed truth.

**Verification.** `verify.sh` OK at the open and at the close; baselines match; **PHANTOMS 0** · `check-quotes.sh` 1,175 fragments, **0 not found on any page**, run after the manuscript edit and again after the bible edits · `check-echoes.sh` on Ch. 38 returns only the chapter's own five registered phrasings — the insert introduced none.

**NEXT:** Ch. 41 — **THE DOOR OPENS**, per 05 §NEXT TARGET. Design from the scene first, Claude's version before the author's, then `scripts/clues.sh` on what it touches.

## SESSION 62 (2026-08-29) — TWO CH. 40 FIXES, AND THE ESCORT BECOMES FIFTY (no new chapter; 00 unopened except one grep)

**Open.** `verify.sh`: baselines match, phantoms 0; the only failure was the working tree — four untracked plate prompts (`ch06`, `ch08`, `ch09`, `ch10`) written 01:57–02:05 that morning, an in-flight 08 session, left alone and never staged. Cold read: CLAUDE.md, 05, 04, 06, 01, all ten 02 topic files, all seventeen 03 sheets, the 07 law core and the Book Two chapter records, plus Ch. 39 and Ch. 40 in full.

**Two itches raised before any work, both in Ch. 40, both arithmetic.** **(1) The princess's clock.** The back goes *some time in the fourth week* and the escape is the sixty-second day, but two lines priced the gap at *three weeks* — slack, in the narration of the boy whose one rule is that he does not lose the count. **(2) The ninth voice had no body.** *There were nine of them … The ninth was the governor* set the roster at nine; *he had all eight* read the other eight; and *he never got the ninth voice* then pointed the ordinal straight at the governor and said it was not him. Either there was a tenth man Kael had never counted, or the voice should not carry the ordinal.

**Ruled (author):** fix 1; **lose the ninth — it is the governor.**

**What that bought.** The passage stops being a failure between two successes and becomes the roster closing: he reads the eight, places the ninth, and the only thing he does not get is the governor's *why*, which the page already nails as wrong and uncorrected. The man who rides at the back is the man giving the orders, and the page sets those two facts a page apart and joins nothing. And a conquered ruler commanding his own men's road in the conqueror's tongue is the register `02/naming.md` already runs on him — *Ivrit to his own men … the empire's tongue for what he wants done.* The present-tense slip in the old passage (*It gives the orders*, inside past-tense narration) went with it. Word count came out identical at 7,177, so baselines did not move. Commit `716117e`; the clue-ledger row moved to PAID at `cd33f38` (PLANTED 210 → 209, PAID 0 → 1).

**THE AUTHOR'S RETRO PASS: THE ESCORT IS FIFTY.** The author's reasoning, verbatim in substance: *they will end up killing some, but still be on the run — if they are just 8 it would be easy for Valeria to just kill them all.* Eight makes the next two years a problem Valeria solves; fifty makes the problem *being found*, which is what running for two years actually needs.

**The shape that cost nothing.** **The column is fifty; the box's own crew is eight** — two on the seat, six who ride close and work the door in threes, and the governor as the ninth man who ever comes near it; the rest keep their own fires and never come within twenty feet of that door in two months. Every Ch. 40 beat therefore stands: *nine faces, no other work, and eight weeks*, *he had all eight*, Kesir revised on the eighteenth day, the ninth voice. And it sharpens the division of his two instruments — **he reads eight and counts the rest**: *The rest were a number, and a number was all they ever became.*

**Ch. 39 gained more than it gave up.** *He got six, once seven, then six* told him he was inside a small thing on the first morning; the replacement tells him he is inside a large one — *More than twenty. He could not honestly make it fewer, and it was the boards, and not himself, that stopped him making it more.* Deliberately NOT framed as the count failing: the four named count-failures (the gate, the house, the water, Qeshet from inside) stay four, and the reason here is boards and wind, not him. **The one-box deduction was moved onto wheels rather than riders** so the load-bearing line survives untouched — *four of them and no more, and no other wheels anywhere on that road … there was no other box on this road.*

**Two knock-ons.** The escape's arithmetic hardens in the right direction — *Fifty men, which was not a number anybody was going to fight* — so unseen becomes the only door he will ever get, which is the plan Ch. 40 already commits him to. And the fifty **feeds** his wrong answer about the governor instead of disturbing it: *with fifty men round them because fifty men is what you put round a thing like that.*

**One catch.** The arithmetic left forty-one men unaccounted for and Ch. 40's own opening line is *Forty-one seconds*; the remainder was dropped rather than written — he has the fifty and the eight, and the reader subtracts.

**Files.** `manuscript/book-two/39-The-Wake.md` (3,270 → 3,334) and `40-The-Hands.md` (7,177 → 7,387) · `04-Outline.md` (beat 1's escape shape, and *a column of fifty* in the door-opens pin) · `05` (STATUS s62 + s61, s59 rolled off; NEXT TARGET rewritten to Ch. 41 with the two unruled items; baselines) · `02/factions.md`, `02/geography.md` · `03/kael.md`, `03/aeliana.md`, `03/hazoun-sabrane.md` · `07`: standing-rules (§THE COLUMN IS FIFTY, the orders line), established-facts, timeline, chapter-records (Ch. 39 and Ch. 40), open-threads (the ninth-voice thread closed), planted-clues. Commit `5f55f1f`.

**Verification.** `verify.sh` baselines match and phantoms 0 at every stage · `check-echoes.sh` on both chapters returns each chapter's own five registered phrasings and nothing new · reader notebook re-synced twice.

**THE CH. 41 DESIGN, AS FAR AS IT GOT.** Claude brought the scene first per CLAUDE.md §Work 4. Its argument: the chapter's thesis is that **they have been watching the wrong prisoner for sixty-two days**, which is 06 §KAEL'S DEFINING AXIS in one morning; the cost is not the fight but that **he knows exactly who he is killing** — nine faces, eight weeks, the best-read people of his life, the one who is kind about the mules where the others cannot see — and it lands on 03's live fact that it stays where he puts it and has never once asked him anything. The hinge image is his own rule paying: *I intend to know exactly where on the earth I am standing when they take me out of this box* — two months of a hand's width of world, then the whole country at once, ground he knows and has never seen.

**Ruled by the author, and now in `04-Outline.md` §BOOK TWO'S SHAPE beat 1:** the **bite at the throat** (both hands spent, so the mouth is the only silent kill available, and a man with his throat opened cannot shout) · **Valeria freed, and she frees the rest** — Claude's mechanic, unchallenged: Kael's whole job is to put a knife inside her reach, she is six inches from a dying man with a knife on his belt and hands chained a hand's width apart, which is enough to hold one and saw a rope, so *I'll need your full strength when the time comes to go* pays exactly and he is not the fighter in his own escape · **the sneak out of a camp of fifty** · **a search party, and Valeria kills it** — no power-up, since fifteen grown men could not lay hands on her on the island and she has eaten and slept for two months while everyone else starved; and it answers why fifty men do not catch three children, because the pursuit learns and afterwards comes slower and in bigger numbers.

**Unresolved and carried to next session:** whether the search party is Ch. 41 or Ch. 42 (Claude's read: keep it in, at eight to nine thousand) · whether the princess acts at the door or is simply carried (Claude's version: she obeys exactly and then does one thing more, off the governor's own *She is not to be marked* — the currency that failed as an offer inside the box working as an obstruction outside it, and Kael unable to read why). Claude also offered, and the author did not rule on, letting the fifty **move** — men detaching and rejoining — so that *how many are in this camp tonight* is a live term; not written, and available.

**Two things deliberately not done.** The iron stays on all three: the pins are peened over (Ch. 39) and there is no key on that road, so nobody gets the Art back and three fugitives in prisoner's irons is Book Two's engine — flagged to the author, unchallenged, still unstamped. And 00 was not opened; it was grepped once at the close for escort-count references and has none.

**NEXT:** Ch. 41 — **THE DOOR OPENS**, design first, per 05 §NEXT TARGET.

## SESSION 63 (2026-08-29) — CH. 41 "THE SECOND": THE DOOR OPENS

**Open.** `verify.sh` clean: tree clean, baselines match, phantoms 0. Full cold read — CLAUDE.md, 05, 04, 06, 01, all ten 02 topic files, all seventeen 03 sheets, the 07 law core, `chapter-records.md`, and Ch. 39 and Ch. 40 in full (drafting).

**Two itches raised before any work, both in Ch. 40, both Claude's own draft text, both ruled *fix*.** **(1) The arithmetic of the door.** *Six or seven breaths a time, twice a day* over sixty-two days is 124 openings of about half a minute — **a little over an hour**, not four; four hours needs the door standing open for thirty breaths. This is the one chapter where the boy who does not lose things cannot be out by four times, and the smaller number is the crueller one. Fixing it also cleared an unintended echo: *four hours* had been running three times in the chapter, twice correctly (the wait until dawn) and once not. Ledger followed the page — the quoted phrase moved in `kael.md`, `chapter-records.md` and `standing-rules.md`. **(2) *The nine men outside*** survived the s62 fifty pass. Defensible — the nine are the near ones — but that same pass added *Fifty men getting up in the cold, a long way back* fifty lines on, and the two numbers ended up three paragraphs from *thirty feet of sleeping men*, so a reader keeping the tally stops to do arithmetic during the one paragraph that must not be interrupted. Dropped to *the men outside*. Ch. 40 = 7,386 (-1). Committed as its own thin commit before any drafting.

**Design, from the scene first.** The argument Claude brought: Ch. 40 was the price paid in advance and Ch. 41 is the spending, and **the escape works because for sixty-two days fifty men correctly identified the dangerous one, and it was not him** — 03's sealed line (*what others react to is nothing, which slowly becomes the thing they react to*) paid at the highest stakes the book has had, with nobody on the page saying so. Underneath it: he bought one second and three people improvised everything after it. The teeth were made to survive on the page by mechanism, not by ruling — the knife is on the dead man's belt, not in his hand; reaching the man silently means stopping the mouth in the same motion; he has one usable hand and needs it for the knife; so the mouth does the work the second hand would have done.

**Two open questions from s62, and Claude's answers.** **(a) The search party — the s62 read REVERSED at design time.** s62 had recorded *keep it in, because the seam is inside a held breath*; designing the scene changed it, and the deciding factor was the last line. Ending in the fold of stone lets Kael count what is coming and get a number **smaller than fifty**, off the governor's looking back — Ch. 40's largest unexplained plant paid by his own arithmetic, right number off the invisible wrong reason. Nothing in a search-party sequence beats that; moving it also gives Valeria two payments instead of one, gives the hand and the princess air, and opens Ch. 42 on ground the book has never stood on. **(b) The princess acts.** She does not block a door or fight anyone — **she stands up**, five weeks after she last did, and every man in that doorway looks at her, because she is the only thing on that road any of them was ever told to be careful of. It runs on the governor's own *She is not to be marked* (Ch. 40) without restating it, answers *whether one word of it had gone in* without narration, and she is gagged, so she never explains it. Both approved by the author with *draft*.

**The author's craft direction for the draft: be immersive, no overexplaining, little "because of X".** That is the opposite register from Ch. 40, which was two months compressed and ran on causal clauses. The chapter was written in the body, in fast cuts, with the explanatory subordinates cut.

**Drafted at 2,823 words, which was well under the 6,500–7,500 Claude had quoted, and said so rather than quietly shipping it.** On re-reading, two sequences were not lean but dodged: Valeria's kill had been summarised away (*she did the thing she had been given eleven generations for*), and the way out of the camp had no jeopardy in it. Both re-rendered — **her kill turned into WITHHELD rather than summarised**, arriving as sound through the open door because Kael is holding a mouth shut (*Nine. He had counted it without meaning to.*), and a near-miss added at the wheels with a man's feet a hand's width from the princess's. Plus the air coming out of the box, and the cost of the shelves. 3,209 → after the author's pass, **3,215**.

**Tic pass.** em-dashes 0 · not-X-but-Y 0 · similes/1k 1.6 · words/paragraph 30.3. **and/1k came in at 66.1 against 06's known 51–55 first-draft default**, with one section at 91.2. Held as intent rather than sanded flat — the polysyndeton here is sequence under pressure, not accumulation for gravity — but the lazy instances were cut on a pass: a restatement (*it went the wrong way* followed by the concrete line that says the same thing), three accumulating and-chains, and **one self-repeat caught in the cut — *a smell like the block after a goat*, which is Ch. 39's goat-and-block image reused three chapters later.** Removed; the sound already carried it. Final and/1k 62.6, sections 54–74, section 1 (the throat) highest and deliberately one motion. Flagged in the commit rather than hidden.

**The author's own pass: one word.** ***Lucan* → *Kael*** in the princess's first line out of the gag. That is a story ruling, not a wording fix: she had only ever had the letter's name for him, and *Kael* was not said at the governor's table (Ch. 37). Carried into `standing-rules.md` §THE SECOND, the chapter record, `established-facts.md`, `aeliana.md`, `kael.md`, the Drusane-letter clue row, and 00's road-east record.

**What the chapter deliberately does not do.** Nobody gets the Art back: the pins are peened over (Ch. 39), three men are killed and their belts stripped and there is still no key, no chisel and no fire, so **all six wrists come out of that box in painted iron** — and at the stop Valeria simply holds hers up and neither of them says anything about it. Kael does not get to the princess. The narration does not reach forward a second time (Ch. 40 closed on one, and a second inside two chapters is a tic). No departure of any kind; a slip at the door argued and declined.

**Close.** Ch. 41 record + FACTS in `chapter-records.md`; `standing-rules.md` §THE SECOND (Ch. 41); `timeline.md` row 41; `open-threads.md` §New at Ch. 41; `established-facts.md` §CH. 41; five phrasings admitted in `protected-phrasings.md` — including *where chain lies when it is doing nothing*, admitted **because it is a deliberate echo of Ch. 40's *the shape chain makes when it is doing nothing***, so the detector catches a third use; `planted-clues.md` — five rows carried forward (the Drusane letter, the painted iron, the governor on the road, the hand, Valeria sleeping on his word) and **two new rows** (the princess stood up; the box left barred on three dead men), 209 → 211; 03 Glances and DYNAMIC for Kael, Valeria, Aeliana and Hazoun Sabrane; 04 beat 1's door half pinned SPENT with its s62 text rolled to `outline-archive.md`, and the search-party half kept live; 00's §THE ROAD EAST given the Ch. 41 spend and the author's name-turn. Instruments at the close: echoes 0, phantoms 0, baselines match.

**NEXT:** Ch. 42 — **THE PARTY**, design first, per 05 §NEXT TARGET.

**Numbering:** the parallel plate session claimed s65 and s66 in `08-Plates/` while this one was running, so this is **s67**; it also committed throughout, and only this session's own paths were ever staged.

**Open.** `verify.sh` failed on the tree only — an uncommitted Oss portrait block and an unrun prompt left by the plate session, untouched here and since committed by them. Baselines matched, phantoms 0. Full cold read: CLAUDE.md, 05, 04, 06, 01, all ten 02 topic files, all seventeen 03 sheets, the 07 law core, `chapter-records.md`, and Ch. 41 and Ch. 42 in full.

**SIX ITCHES AT THE OPEN, ALL CLAUDE'S, ALL FROM THE s64 CLOSE, ALL RULED *FIX*.** **(1) On the committed page:** Ch. 42 had Valeria kill the prisoner *the way she had done it to the man at the ring* — but the man at the ring was killed by Kael's teeth, and her Ch. 41 knife-kill was on Kesir, under the arm, twice. The clause was **cut rather than repointed**, because her free hand flat on the chest two sentences later already repeats Ch. 41 and carries the callback without asserting a wrong fact; Ch. 42 = 6,297 → 6,285. **(2–6) State files:** the record's invented *five days without a healer* (the page says a few days, and the record's own §II had it right); ***plus survivors*** in 04, 05, `03/hazoun-sabrane` and `standing-rules` — all seven who went into the wood died and exactly five are left, all with the horses; `open-threads` had eight coming back with six dead, where it is five back and seven dead; Valeria's tally given as *the first three, silent* in two homes, where the page gives her two and the third shouted and was Kael's from the bank (standing-rules was totalling seven kills in a six-death fight); and `03/kael`'s kill-list crediting the princess with one, when she put her knife in a thigh and that man lived to be the prisoner.

**Design, from the scene first.** Claude brought a Doresh in which kindness and betrayal are the same gesture — a village that feeds you and sends a boy up the road while you eat. **The author replaced the frame:** *the poor village should be kind to a bunch of battered children … we should show the good of humanity here, in this rural non imperial town*, they hide the princess, the irons come off, the wounds are healed by a regular healer and not by the Art, **and one person tells**. Named as character work for the princess — *seeing that the countries the empire conquers are filled with people like these*. Three further rulings: the search party comes to the village and asks and the people hide them; the betrayer learns what she is from that visit and then catches a glimpse of the hair; and he is **the smith's son, a young adult, honestly scared and impulsive** rather than calculating. **The reserved half of that design is swept to 00** — the live files carry only what the page has: a bucket in a doorway, an absence from the forge, and a wrong reading of both.

**Two plants the design-time `clues.sh` returned and the chapters pay.** `THE COLONIAL QUESTION NOBODY ANSWERED` (Ch. 17, unspent, twenty-five chapters old) — *Who lives in it*, asked of a conquered city and answered with a shrug; Doresh is the old top, and neither Kael nor the princess assembles it. `THE BEGGING AT THE GAP` (Ch. 29) — he called a man *sir* and begged for passage and was refused, correctly and warmly; here **the princess begs wordlessly and is not refused, by people with a fraction of what the district had**, and Kael is unconscious for it.

**The draft, and four author interventions that each rebuilt something.** Drafted whole at 7,912 and committed at once. Then: **(a) *they should have a plan … war orphans who were taken*** — which inverted Claude's premise (*there is no story that survives a wrist*) into **the iron is the part a village believes**, gave the story the job of explaining why only one of three can speak, and opened three new beats: the headman taking it and the village's habit of taking in what comes down that water, the soldiers' own description destroying it while the village hides them anyway, and Damir naming the lie and working regardless. **(b) *technically, its kinda true*** → **Kael says *orphans* about himself and the word catches him**; Claude had had him deftly route round the word, which was a writer being clever, and the author's version is a boy ambushed by his own sentence. **(c) The arrival resolved for free** — a dog barked and a door opened. Rebuilt so **the plan runs and the planner fails**: he reads the smith off a banked fire and a shed roof, takes them over the wall onto ground that holds no print, and then goes down at the door. Kept inside POV by not letting him go cleanly under, so the two girls' wordless ask arrives in fragments through a narrowing hole. **(d) *too little aeliana dialogue … the more unexplored relationship is Kael and Aeliana's*** — she had none at all after the grass; added the Ivrit lessons (she asks, having built the request over two days; the first word she wants is thank you) and the whispered translation under the floor.

**The split (author).** One chapter became **Ch. 43 "The Water"** and **Ch. 44 "The Doors"**, cut at the boards going down — 43 gives them a bed, a healer, food, an iron promised and a name for the road, and closes with three children shut back into the dark.

**Tic passes.** First: em-dashes 11 → 2 (five gravity/appositive pairs restructured; only the dialogue cut-offs left), "the way" 13 → 7. **Two cross-chapter self-repeats caught by eye that no script would find** — *he knew it was wrong the way he knew a bout was going wrong* is Ch. 42's line in the same shape, and *the only thing he had that still worked as it should* is Ch. 42's count line; *put it where he put things* was running three times on top of one each in Ch. 40 and Ch. 41 and was cut to the single load-bearing use. **and/1k did not move on that pass (62.0 → 61.4) and was flagged rather than sanded**; the added dialogue then brought Ch. 43 to 55.0 on its own, inside 06's band, and Ch. 44 sits at 62.9, which is the fight-chapter register alongside Ch. 41's 62.6.

**Metrics recorded honestly, including one failure.** A commit message carried `and/1k 59.4` that had never been measured — the measuring regex had been mangled by shell escaping and returned 0.0, and a number was written instead of the discrepancy being noticed. Measured properly at 60.5 and the commit amended.

**The author's own passes, read as `git diff` and named in each commit.** Consistently cutting Claude's restatements and callbacks: *the iron is the part they will believe* (the gesture already showed it); Valeria's *It is not a story* (the interior ambush now does that job unremarked); *He had done that sum on a bound man in a wood six days ago* (the whisper now puts *I would give us up* in Kael's own mouth); Damir's *not one of them will come out* (checked — Ch. 44's forge payoff is not orphaned, because that section establishes the carry in situ); Kael's body refusing the boards; and the soldier's description of Valeria thinned. **One of their cuts left a sentence pointing at nothing** — the last line of Ch. 44 was removed and its frame (*It was the last thing he thought that night*) left behind; the orphan was cut, so the chapter now ends on *did not count one single thing*, and it was flagged at the close for reversal. **And the Valeria ground-sense exchange was cut whole**, which resolved a genuine Claude error against `02/magic.md`'s SHORT-RANGE limit (she had been reading cattle a long way down the fold); the Art's return on her side is now withheld.

**Ruled by the author, small:** ***Ishareth*, no accent** — normalised in the page, 04 and 05; `archive/session-log.md` keeps the accented form in the s64 record because records are appended, never rewritten.

**Still unruled and recorded in 05 §OPEN ITEMS, one edit away:** Ch. 43's *He had learned that at a table in Qeshet in about four minutes, and it had cost him everything he had*, which can be read as Kael joining the letter to the taking when `07` says he has not; the proposal was to end at *four minutes*.

**Close.** Records for Ch. 43 and Ch. 44 in `chapter-records.md`; `standing-rules.md` §THE WATER, AND THE DOORS; `established-facts.md` (the two chapter facts, the four household names, and ISHARETH); `open-threads.md` §New at Ch. 43–44; `timeline.md` rows 43 and 44; `planted-clues.md` — four rows carried forward (the painted iron, the hair, the begging at the gap, the colonial question) and **five new rows** (the hair in the yard, forty doors, the iron under the forge floor, the hand's future, the two empty hands), 212 → 217. `protected-phrasings.md`: Ch. 43 and Ch. 44 admitted — **and Ch. 42's, which the s64 close recorded as admitted and never added.** 03 refreshed: Kael, Valeria, Aeliana (Glance + DYNAMIC) and Hazoun (the pursuit on the page). 02: `geography.md` (Doresh from the inside; the road east to Ishareth), `naming.md` (the five mints), `magic.md` (painted iron comes off by cutting the peen and drifting the pin, and the Art answers the moment it is off). 04: the village beat spent to a pin and **beat 1 fully discharged** — both the pursuit *slower and bigger* and Ishareth. 00: the reserved half of the Doresh design and the Ch. 43–44 spend, plus the s63 entry's *all six wrists* synced to five. 05: status s67 (s63 dropped to hold the cap), next target Ch. 45, baselines + Ch. 43 = 6,841, Ch. 44 = 2,965, 00 = 21,830, closed enquiries appended. **Two phantoms of Claude's own were caught by the instrument during the close and fixed** — a proposal quoted as if it were page canon, and a phrasing lifted from the chapter record's paraphrase (*a child carrying a child*) instead of the page (*a grown one carrying a light one*). Instruments at the close: echoes 0, phantoms 0, baselines match.

Ch. 43's *and it had cost him everything he had* is cut and the sentence ends at *He had learned that at a table in Qeshet in about four minutes*, so nothing on the page has Kael joining the Drusane letter to the taking; `standing-rules.md` §THE BOX stands unchanged. ## SESSION 68 (2026-08-30/31) — CH. 45 AND CH. 46: THE BARGAIN, AND THE NARROW

**Open.** `verify.sh` clean. Cold read flagged three things, all fixed on the author's word. **Two author typos from `acce74a`:** Ch. 44's *It was finaly time* → *finally*, and Ch. 43's *and at the river flows to Ishareth* → *and the river flows to Ishareth* (their edit had introduced *flows* and stranded *at*; Ch. 43 = 6,832). **The Doresh day-count drifted:** they come down through the night of the 63rd, so the first morning is the 64th, *the second morning* (he wakes) the 65th, and *the sixth morning* — Tarim's run and the horses — the **69th**, not the 70th; the two forge nights then land Ch. 44 before dawn on the 71st, which is what *seventy days* without the Art wants. `07/timeline.md` and both chapter records corrected; no page carries a Doresh day-number.

**Design.** Claude brought its own version first: Kael **stops** Tarim and hands Doresh a lie that makes him the monster. The author's is bigger and is what was built — **Kael does not stop him; he buys him.** The one thing Claude's version kept and that carried the whole chapter: *Tarim's reason is Kael's own arithmetic wearing another face* — he ran the sum Kael had run under that floor two days earlier and said out loud (*I would give us up*), so Kael cannot argue him down, only correct a term. Four questions were put and ruled: **(1)** he tells Aeliana what he sold on her behalf — yes · **(2)** Doresh knows, and is worried · **(3)** she is hidden, but looking · **(4)** the decision is Ishareth.

**Ch. 45 drafted at 9,641**, then **split on the author's word** at the held breath named at design time — everything in the ground, the promise sold, and the next thing is them arriving. Ch. 45 "The Ground" ends *The first two horses came past him at a walk*; Ch. 46 "The Narrow" opens on the front horse going down. The cut fell on an existing scene break and no prose moved.

**The author's pass on Ch. 45**, read as a diff before staging and committed as theirs: **Damir carries Doresh** — the headman's yard scene is gone and Damir rules (*Doresh stands by children. We are all in this together and there is nothing to decide*), the two families packing are cut, and the village is behind them rather than fractured; **Kael says *Val***; **Valeria's Art is explicitly still returning** (*My Art is still returning to me Kael*; *I'm almost embarrassed at my lack of power*) and the pit exchange becomes a real conversation about what seventy days without it was; **Aeliana speaks Ivrit on the page** and Kael's accent joke is cut; his analytical narration is trimmed throughout and his voice made colder and more direct.

**Ch. 46 rewritten twice on the author's rulings.** First: *all of the soldiers need to die, not one should escape*, and *the fighting needs to be more brutal.* The escaper and the hour-and-ten hunt are gone; the count still comes up one short but the missing man is a body the water took, found **breathing** two hundred strides down the shallows and killed there in front of Tarim, which is where *That was not fighting* now lands. The four in the holes stop being a subordinate clause. **Tally closed and checked:** 19 in — 1 under his own horse, 9 down the water side (4 taken alive), 3 at the mouth, 6 held at the top including the officer; 14 dead, 5 alive, all five killed. Then: *be explicit, dont have fear* — the euphemisms (*did him*, *did them*, *it took a moment*) all out, and the mechanics in: the knife rather than the sword, the angle wrong from above a hole, a stroke turned over a collarbone that does nothing except make a man worse, weight on a wrist through his own splint, and what *making sure* actually is.

**Then the beat the chapters were missing**, on the author's note that these men have families and are mostly bought and he finishes them anyway. Rendered **through his hands**, because making sure is done with the hands and hands find pockets: no two coats the same, boots of six makes, a mail shirt over a shepherd's coat; a child's tooth on a cord, coins sewn into three hems, and a letter written for a man who could not read saying the roof had been done before the rain came and she was not to worry about it. Then the sum, coming out the same from every end he takes it from — *He had been right. He was still right* — against *the kind of men who sew coins into a hem*. **Both true, neither moving the other, and no verdict either way.** And the last beat, which **inverts an established fact about him**: 03 has had it since the island that a thing stays where he puts it and has not once asked him anything, *and that is the frightening part.* Here: *He put it down anyway … It did not stay down.* The front horse's *It did not stay down* was changed to *It would not lie still* so the punch line lands once.

**Claude's own calls, flagged to the author and standing:** Damir answering for the horses instead of the headman (following their Ch. 45 move); the princess binding Valeria's arm; Tarim stopping at the near end of the flat place; the thirteen horses left to Doresh and priced at one at a time down the water over four years; and Kael giving up three horses east on *the gate does not have to be clever, it only has to be able to read.*

**Parallel-session hazard, twice.** Three `held:` plate commits landed between Claude's commits, and the author edited both chapter files live while Claude was working (four trims on Ch. 46 mid-session). Every replacement was exact-match guarded and all asserts passed, so nothing was overwritten; only Claude's own paths were ever staged. **One author line flagged and not touched:** their *until the other commits a mistake* now sits two lines above *The ninth was the man's mistake.*

**Close.** Records for Ch. 45 and Ch. 46 in `chapter-records.md`; `standing-rules.md` §THE GROUND, AND THE NARROW; `established-facts.md` (the two chapter facts; **no new names** — the officer, the healer and the men are unnamed); `open-threads.md` §New at Ch. 45–46; `timeline.md` rows 45 and 46. `planted-clues.md`: three rows stamped forward (the hair in the yard — **answered**, and a new leg opened; forty doors; the iron under the forge floor) and **four new rows** (the promise made on her, it did not stay down, one bridge and it is his, he has told nobody anything), 217 → 221. `protected-phrasings.md`: Ch. 45's nine and Ch. 46's ten admitted. 03 refreshed: Kael (Glance, five DYNAMIC lines, Body, Knows), Valeria (Glance, five lines, Body), Aeliana (Glance, five lines), Hazoun (Glance, two lines). 02: `geography.md` §THE FOLD ABOVE DORESH, AND THE NARROW; `magic.md` (earth opening ground by subtraction, and what seventy days without the Art was in the one mouth that said so). 04: the one-betrayer beat spent to a pin, a new **1b** (the pursuit destroyed and the road east the only road), and §WHAT FOLLOWS turned to the road to Ishareth. 05: status s68 (s64 dropped to hold the cap), next target Ch. 47, baselines Ch. 43 = 6,832 · Ch. 45 = 5,570 · Ch. 46 = 5,438, closed enquiries appended. 00 synced. Instruments: echoes 0 against the ledger as it stood, phantoms 0, baselines match.

**NEXT:** Ch. 47 — **THE ROAD EAST**, design first, per 05 §NEXT TARGET.

## SESSION 69 (2026-08-31) — CH. 47 AND CH. 48: ISHARETH

**Open.** `verify.sh` clean (0 phantoms, baselines matching). Cold read flagged nothing against the page.

**Design.** Claude brought its own version first: the road east as a whole chapter — the hair cut and stained, the arithmetic of three, a muleteers' halt, the roof-letter night, ending at the lip. The author cut it down in three passes. **(1)** *no need for a whole chapter on the road — one movement of travel, and the rest is arriving and in the city.* **(2)** *no need for aeliana to cut her hair, have it remain hidden … one movement, they enter the city, then the rest of the chapter plays out, the search for the governour. information gathering, staying hidden, distrusting everything and everyone. kael hears of a famous criminal gang of outlaws is staying in the city.* **(3)** *this is the gang they'll soon join. they'll need their help for the raid on the governour.* — which set the destination and made the chapter's argument: **he has one thread to his family, it is a man with a wall of bought men round him, three people cannot open that, and the only thing in that city big enough to is a crew of professional criminals — which is the thing he did once above Doresh, at ten times the size.** Four calls were left to Claude (*you choose*): the crew's byword, whether to name their leader, why they are in that city, and the coin off the hems.

**What was minted.** **THE HONEST MEN** — a byword and not a name, because they have never broken into anywhere in their lives. **BORREN** reserved and unspent for the leader (soldier-worn register). **NALAR** — the country past the river, in the coalition register, the first coalition PLACE name on any page, discharging 04's *unminted* line. **ZAHIR** — the mule-yard keeper.

**Rulings (author).**
- **The road is one movement**; the hair stays hidden, not cut.
- **The princess stays in the room and Valeria wants to stay with her always — *that's all of her purpose*.** Claude had it backwards, and backwards against a line the author had already written into the chapter two hundred lines earlier (*I can't be away from her Highness*). The fix is the princess ordering her Vel out, which is the only authority that can, and which points the crown at Kael's work rather than away from it, so the crown-vs-Kael choice is still not made.
- **The ground-sense is a sort of projection.** `02/magic.md`'s *a reading, never a projection · nothing leaves her body · perception only* clause STRUCK. It costs nothing and buys coherence: it now sits under the distance law with Kael's own boot-thread, its three limits become consequences of spending, and no manuscript page had to move (Ch. 14's *nothing left her body* is Kael watching internal reinforcement in one duel, already fenced as his observation).
- **Register is apparatus, not vocabulary.** *"this is not a real world. i can use whatever words i think fit. this is just a world where the word heist exists."* `02/curriculum.md`'s *no modern terminology* clause retired — the ban is what a term DRAGS IN (notation, units, an apparatus or a discipline this world has not built), never how modern the word sounds to an English ear. *heist* ruled in. **Claude's objection to *reconnaissance* was wrong on the same grounds** and the author's line is one edit away.
- **Ishareth is not the empire's city.** Queried as a possible retro change; **no retro change was needed** — Ch. 43 already has *the empire holds it*, which is the Qeshet formula, and the name is minted in the south's register, which by naming law is itself the statement. What the page gained instead: **the hold is one bridge wide**, which re-reads the dying officer's *there is one bridge and it is his* as the whole of the man's holdings rather than a boast about the difficulty.
- **The count explained out loud**, at the author's ask, inside the hand-dressing scene — hyper-attention plus total retention, in Kael's own words, with no term for it, and he believes it is ordinary. Its second half answers the fold and neither of them joins it up.
- **Craft corrections, all taken:** Kael was speaking in riddles and should be *precise, realistic and pragmatic*, and Aeliana should *genuinely want to help and learn and be useful* — the foraging went from narration to a procedure she argues him into teaching her · they should not be hungry, Kael being a skilled hunter — snares first, then ruled out (*no sense to set snares on the move*) and rewritten as active hunting, thrown knives and foraging · *near fore* and *string* both queried as opaque, and *train* rejected too, so the page carries no collective noun for the mules at all · the Tarim callback was leaning on Ch. 46 and was rewritten to carry itself, then cut.
- **The trim.** *"this crossing chapter is pretty bloated and honestly quite boring. i like the dialogue, the rest needs to be a lot more to the point."* A narration-only pass took Ch. 47 from 7,384 to 6,621 without touching one spoken word: the four gate paragraphs to three, the tolls run-on split, the asking-method halved, and the fire paragraph, which was restating a rule given a page earlier. Narration 69% → 55%; words per paragraph 33 → 28.
- **The split**, at the wall: Ch. 47 ends on the sum with no door in it, Ch. 48 opens on the thing he had been carrying two days without knowing he had it.

**Process.** Two slips, both named to the author. At `d964347` the file was checked for author edits at the top of the turn and staged after other work, so four of their edits went in unread and unnamed; the practice is now to diff **immediately** before `git add`. And a heredoc quoting error made a `python` block fail while the `git commit` after it still ran, producing a commit whose message described work not in it — amended (nothing was pushed).

**Close.** Ch. 47 = 6,705 · Ch. 48 = 1,640. Echoes 0 in both; `check-quotes` 0 phantoms. Eleven new phrasings admitted. The ground-sense ruling is in `02/magic.md`; the register ruling in `02/curriculum.md`; Ishareth, the bridge, Nalar and the road in `geography.md`; the crew in `factions.md`; Nalar, Zahir and the reserved Borren in `naming.md`.

## SESSION 71 (2026-08-31) — PLATES: CH. 46 "THE NARROW" (image generation only; no prose, no canon moved). Ran in parallel with the Ch. 49 drafting session on the same tree

**Open.** `verify.sh` clean. Plate session, so no cold read: `08-Plates/README.md` · `canon-brief.md` · `prompt-system.md` · `scene-tests.md` · Ch. 46 in full · the `03` sheets for Kael, Valeria and Aeliana. Ch. 46 was the first chapter without two plates and had no candidate list. **The author asked for three and left the choice to Claude.**

**Design.** The chapter is the set-piece the folder has been short of — the author's s65 *we have little action shots and closeups* — and it is the first sustained fight in Book Two; but the chapter is not about the fight, it is about a thing done deliberately and correctly that was not fighting, and the bill arriving afterwards through a boy's hands. So the three take one moment from the narrow, one from the count and one from the bill, and they are **an action plate, a close-up and an object study** — three registers, three characters, no two alike. Eight further candidates are on `prompts/plate-candidates/ch46.md` with why each was passed over; **She sat down on the horse** is first in line for a fourth.

**Two framing rulings, both Claude's.** Valeria anchors on `valeria-14` and **not** on `valeria-14-full` though her body is in frame: the full figure carries the royal guard uniform, and a gorget bleeding into a fold in the south is the exact costume failure the ownership rule exists to stop; her road clothing is authored, as at Ch. 41–45. And **the princess's hair is out** — knotted back and dust-dulled as in `ch45/covering-the-holes`, not under Miresh's scarf: the page is silent there, every man who came looking for her is dead by that line, and the silver is the lightest mass in the frame.

**Three run, three adopted, all first run, all 1086×1448 exactly 3:4.** Full landed/drifted records are under each CHOSEN entry.

- **`ch46-nineteen`** — the fold came whole and the folded laced hands did exactly what they were written to do, the cloak carried and not worn with its weight pulling the arm down. **The crop widened past *chest-up, close, the head large in the frame* to waist-up and more** — the known widening — which cost the head size and bought both hands, the cloak and the whole fold, and is the better picture. The face is what did not land: *no colour in the face at all* and *one corner drawn tight and slightly down* both went, and it reads composed and tired rather than white with one small part stopped.
- **`ch46-the-strip`** — the folder's second fight picture and the first of Valeria at full: two bodies inside each other's reach with the sword and the spear shaft crossing hard where the eye lands, both grounded on the same grass, and the dead already small and face down behind them. Four drifts: *already going over backwards with his weight off his feet* came back as a braced man, so it reads as a bind; **the per-hand count came back with one hand too many** (sword right, knife right, and a third hand of hers on the shaft); the shaft is held rather than trapped under the arm; **the burn did not render** on the left side, which is the side turned away; and she reads several years above thirteen with the rung in.
- **`ch46-the-roof`** — **the hands' own view asked for plainly in a block and delivered first run.** `ch40/the-holding` was handed this camera by the tool and adopted for it; this is the first block to write it in, so the §Instruments wording is proven rather than inferred. The paper is quartered by its folds and split along both, six lines of cramped faded writing and no word legible; the linen on the splinted arm is both the whitest and the only clean thing in the picture. **The register is the drift and it is the first miss on the no-reference route** — the three before it held the STYLE Medium paragraph outright, and this one keeps the tooth and granulation in the ground, the grass and the coat but **loses the ink**: no scratchy linework anywhere, the hand modelled smoothly in wash, very little bare paper. One instance, cause not settled, recorded as evidence and not made a rule.

**One amendment to `prompt-system.md`, one clause:** §Instruments' *the hands' own view* now cites `ch46-the-roof` as the first block to write the camera in and land it. Nothing else in the folder moved — three drifts were recorded under their pictures and none became a rule (`README.md`: a drift is not a debt).

**Parallel-session hygiene.** Only Claude's own paths were ever staged; the Ch. 49 draft the author was writing on the same tree was never touched. This session took **71** rather than 70 so the live drafting session keeps the next number, per the s40 precedent.

**NEXT:** Ch. 47 "The Crossing" is the first chapter without plates.

## SESSION 70 (2026-08-31/09-01) — CH. 49 "THE PAPER": THE HONEST MEN'S ROOM
*(Appended after §SESSION 71 — records are appended, never rewritten. The parallel plate session took 71 on 2026-08-31 expressly to leave 70 for this one.)*

**Open.** `verify.sh` clean — tree clean, baselines matching, 0 phantoms. Full cold read: CLAUDE.md · 05 · 04 · 06 · 01 · every 02 topic file · every 03 sheet · the 07 law core · **Ch. 47 and Ch. 48 in full** (drafting) · the chapter records for Ch. 37–46. Target off 05: Ch. 49, design first.

**Three itches raised at the open, none of them ruled.** **(1)** `47:93` — *"Valeria responded."* sits where her answer used to be; `git log -S` puts it in `d964347`, the author's own pass, which took out *"The last one." / "No."* and put nothing back, so Kael's *Say which* is answered by a line with no content and then by a question that does not answer it. **(2)** *"Ok Highness"* (`47:141`) is the only *Ok* in forty-nine chapters. **(3)** 05's s68 status line carried Ch. 46 at 5,438 against a canonical baseline of 5,422 — **resolved by attrition**, since the s68 block rolled out of §STATUS at this close. (1) and (2) are still open and are the author's.

**Design.** Claude brought its own version of the scene first (per §Standing rules): a reading contest Kael loses, a house whose count returns nothing, a leader who is the least impressive person in the room, the crew's fear being *attention*, and the girl as the thing that opens the door. **The author's frame replaced most of it and is what the chapter runs on**, given as six beats: they go in and he says he has information; **the house is a fake-out and they are hooded and moved**; the leader asks what they want and what they can provide; the Art is shown in private and they are priced as the escapees, which Kael does not deny; the terms are information and hands **for a tenth**; and the leader agrees, likes their faces, and calls the band for the night after. Two of Claude's own additions survived into the shape and were then cut in draft (below).

**Drafted movement by movement, at the author's instruction**, with the author editing the file directly and each pass read as `git diff` before staging (CLAUDE.md §Work 6). Seven movements, seven commits.

**Author's rulings inside the drafting.**
- **The transition into the sacks was too fast** — *no considering? no thoughts on why or if they should?* Rewritten: Kael prices the sack before he takes it (**you do not blind a man you mean to kill; you blind him for the after**), asks for a distance and is refused one, and Valeria puts the cost on the record once without making it an argument.
- **Valeria's showing is an earth pillar** — *she can just form an earth pillar of the ground. simpler* — rather than the ground-sense or reinforcement, which keeps the ground-sense as the thing she said she would rather nobody in that city knew about.
- **The Art is fully back on both of them by Ch. 49** — Ch. 45's *my Art is still returning to me Kael* is discharged, and the cost-showing was cut out of the pillar.
- **Thirteen months to Solvenna from Ishareth**, and **a war makes hiding dearer, slower and more dangerous, never cheaper.** This does not touch Ch. 38's *five months to Solvenna in good weather*, which was said at Qeshet, two months and eleven days west. The exchange was rebuilt on it, and the tell moved: **the man offers a fifth and Kael does not take it**, which is the one thing a person who wants money never does.
- **They are not allowed to go home** — *I think it's more interesting if they're not allowed to.* This turned the ending: the rule binds the whole crew, so it is procedure rather than captivity, and **the price of getting a word to the princess is the address Kael had held out of the trade all day.**

**The trim standard, held for the whole session and worth recording.** Every author pass cut in the same direction: **narrated Kael-conclusions out, the observable action left.** Casualties included four of Claude's own plants — *the capital under the leader's voice*; the block where four men visibly work at going round a name (reserving BORREN by showing the labour of it); the before-dark promise and its reversal; and the *come out here and tell them* close. Also cut: the slot-in-the-door callback, the door-on-a-guess callback, *he had thought about the number for a long time in the night*, and the four-question pile-up in III. **The lesson is the one already in auto-memory** (callback-instead-of-present, prose-overexplaining): the page states the beat and stops.

**One question asked twice and not answered, written under a stated assumption.** The author's beat 4 carried *(he does not care for the money, he just wants to assassinate the governor, but he will disguise it as a want for money)*, which collides with Ch. 46's page canon that the governor is the only thread to his mother and *there is not going to be a second one.* Claude flagged the collision, proposed the tightest version, and asked; the author moved on twice. **Written with the want unnamed on the page**, carried by the refused fifth and by *Every word of it was true* under a functional reason, so both readings stay live and nothing is spent. **The ruling is still owed** and the intention itself is swept to 00 at this close.

**Metrics.** Ch. 49 = **5,641** words, no departure of any kind, 0 script echo hits, and/1k **41.9** across the chapter (per movement: 38.8 · 45.4 · 37.0 · 41.7 · 37.6 · 48.4 · 47.1 — one trim pass on movement II, which came in at 61.8 and was broken up at four and-chains). 2 em-dashes, 1 not-but (kept — a real distinction).

**Close.** State written to 05 (baselines, §STATUS rolled to s70/s69, §NEXT TARGET, closed enquiries) · 04 (the door beat spent; §BOOK TWO'S SHAPE beat 4 half-discharged) · 02 `factions.md`, `geography.md`, `magic.md` · 03 `kael`, `valeria`, `aeliana`, `hazoun-sabrane` (the s-hazard hit all four — the appended block carried its own `### DYNAMIC` and duplicated the header; caught by grep and repaired, no bullets eaten) · 07 `chapter-records`, `standing-rules` §THE PAPER, `open-threads`, `established-facts`, `timeline`, `planted-clues` (five new rows, the HONEST MEN row amended). **Parallel-session hygiene:** a plate session was committing to this tree throughout; only this session's own paths were ever staged.

**Noted, not fixed:** `planted-clues.md`'s section header count has been drifting against what `clues.sh` actually parses (221 typed vs 228 parsed before this session; 226 vs 233 after — the +5 was added to both, preserving the existing gap). A typed count where a script owns the fact is a ONE FACT, ONE HOME problem and wants the author before anything is restructured.

**NEXT:** Ch. 50 — the plan, and a job set for the night after. 05 §NEXT TARGET carries what it comes in holding.

### SESSION 70 — ADDENDUM (2026-09-01): the three itches, fixed
The author ruled *fix itches* after the close, so all three were closed in one pass and committed separately from the chapter.
- **Ch. 47's hole.** *"Valeria responded."* is replaced by the answer it was standing in for, with the author's own added line kept: *"The last one," Valeria said. / "No." / "So what do you suggest?"* — she takes the test, is corrected in one word, and goes straight to the practical without arguing, which is her register. Nothing else in the passage moved.
- **The only *Ok* in the manuscript** (`47:141`) becomes **Very well**, her established word since Ch. 40's *said "Very well," and lay down*. Ch. 47's baseline moves 6,705 → 6,710.
- **`planted-clues.md`'s typed section counts are removed** — PLANTED, VALVE and PAID now carry no number, because `scripts/clues.sh` prints the real one and a typed count is a second home for a fact the script owns (ONE FACT, ONE HOME). PLANTED had drifted seven rows and would have gone on drifting. **ZERO-LOSS verified, not asserted:** the 242 table lines are byte-identical before and after, `clues.sh` with no arguments returns the same three sections with the same row counts, and a wide parity query (`-a -p 'bridge|Vask|fire|gate|iron|princess'`) returns **95/233 rows matched** both ways with byte-identical output apart from the removed number.
- **05's *193 stay in the working set*** (the s54 open item) is left alone: it is a record of what that prune decided, not a live count.
